# Brain Gain

## Dedication

This book is humbly dedicated to two persons who inspired me in the wisdom of celibacy.

My beloved spiritual master

His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupada, Founder-Acaryaof the International Society for Krishna Consciousness

Dr. Raymond W. Bernard

## Introduction

Originally, I intended to title this work “Brain Drain,” hoping to alert readers to the mental disorders caused by loss of vital fluids through sex-related activities. However, a friend suggested that emphasizing the negative aspects of a topic would tend to repel potential readers or fail to inspire the serious readers. Thus, we have decided also to present the positive side of the issue, namely that by abstaining from sex-related activities, including subtle contemplation—the brain, mind and intellect gain tremendous strength and ability.

Be that as it may, the negative results of excessive sex life still hold, and we will present their dreadful results as convincingly as possible. Yet since our goal is not merely to frighten but to guide toward prevention and solutions of problems, we urge our readers to consider the advantages of abstinence for enlightenment as well as to avoid mental disorders such as Alzheimer’s disease and dementia.

In this book we will examine in both Western science and Vedic science of India, the benefits of sexual abstinence and the detriments of misused sexual activity. Interestingly, the two schools of thought were in agreement for 25 centuries, from the fifth century BC until the early 20th century.

This book has been inspired by two personalities. Firstly, my beloved spiritual master His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktive-danta Swami Prabhupada, Founder-Acaryaof the International Society for Krishna Consciousness instilled in me the impetus to practice celibacy. More is said about him and by him within the book.

Secondly, Dr. Raymond W. Bernard (1901-1965) born to a family of Russian nonpracticing Jews in New York City. His father was a doctor which gave him an early interest in health. He received a bachelor’s degree from Columbia University in 1924, and his MA and PhD from New York University (in 1930 and 1932, respectively). He studied biochemistry in Germany, where he took great interest in lecithin and became a pioneer in this field when he returned to the United States.

His book entitled Science Discovers the Physiological Value of Continence (Health Research, Mokelumne Hill, Calif.), has proved especially helpful. Dr. Bernard felt that an opinion had gained ground, not only among the general public, but also among physicians, that the belief in the physiological value of continence belonged to the dark ages of religious superstitions and scientific ignorance and was incompatible with physiological knowledge. Certain pseudosexologists, he claimed, have exploited this idea to their commercial advantage and have created in the public mind a phobia in regards to continence, which was even at the time of his writing (1957) regarded as a cause of nervous and mental diseases and a positive health danger. On the basis of this belief, physicians and psychoanalysts have looked on sexual continence for the cause of the nervous ailments of youth and have advised young men to indulge in sex to avoid the assumed hazards of abstinence. Bernard strongly disagreed with that view because of the richness of the semen in lecithin, cholesterol, phosphorus and other constituents of nervous and brain tissue. It is incontinence, or loss of these valuable nervenourishing substances that, by promoting under-nutrition, is responsible for disturbed functioning of the nervous system and brain, and never true continence, contrary to the unscientific views of the psychoanalysts. Continence can never do harm, but is always beneficial.

Dr. Bernard elaborately documented evidence and statements from eminent authorities in the field of neurology, neuropathology, sexology, gynecology, physiology, psychiatry, endocrinology, urology and genitourinology.

For many years Dr. Bernard has been a kind of folk hero for some devotees of the Krishna consciousness movement. The reason is that devotees of Krishna take firm vows at the time of initiation to abstain from an illicit sex life. Specifically, initiated devotees are expected to forego sex outside of sacred marriage and without a desire to procreate.

Consequently, the Western devotees who took up the challenge of becoming celibate did so in one of four categories: 1) a brahmacari, i.e. celibate student, 2) a grhastha, i.e. householder, 3) a vanaprastha, i.e. retired person 4) a sannyasi, i.e. renounced monk under lifetime vows of celibacy. This proved to be no easy task for many of the devotees. Thus, when someone discovered a booklet by an American PhD scientist boldly advocating celibacy and substantiating his position by vigorously quoting from many of the most respected names in science, it became an underground classic. Our investigation confirmed that Dr. Bernard’s references indeed were accurate.

Aside from being a researcher in biochemistry and endocrinology, Dr. Bernard was a true seeker of truth. He was celibate, vegetarian and had studied the Vedic literature extensively—in fact, he wrote a book called From Chrishna to Christ. If only he had met a bona fide spiritual master, perhaps he could have achieved the perfection of his search. Be that as it may, he died of pneumonia on Sept. 10, 1965. On that very day, perhaps coincidentally, the great world teacher from India, His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupada (known also as Srila Prabhupada) was onboard the Jaladuta en route to New York. On that day Srila Prabhupada wrote in his diary:

“Friday, September 10

Today the ship is plying very smoothly. I feel today better. But I am feeling separation from Sri Vrindaban and my Lords Sri Govinda, Gopinath, Radha Damodar. The only solace is Sri Chaitanya Charitamrita in which I am tasting the nectarine of Lord Chaitanya’s lila \[pastimes\]. I have left Bharatabhumi just to execute the order of Sri Bhaktisiddhanta Saraswati in pursuance of Lord Chaitanya’s order. I have no qualification, but have taken up the risk just to carry out the order of His Divine Grace. I depend fully on Their mercy, so far away from Vrindaban.”

Srila Prabhupada lived a perfect life according to celibacy principles. Up until his marriage at 22, he practiced brahmacarya, the complete celibacy of a spiritual student. After his marriage, he fathered five children in accordance with spiritual householder regulations. At 58 (1954) he retired, and then in 1959 he formally accepted the fourth order of sannyasa. Remarkably, by his example, association and precept, many thousands of followers, including this humble disciple, also embraced celibacy.

The first chapter is a summary of Dr. Bernard’s work, and the bracketed words are my addition to aid the reader through the more technical terms.

## 1. Neuroses and Psychoses

By Dr. R. W. Bernard, AB, MA, PhD

\[The following is an abstract taken from the writings of Dr. Bernard which summarizes his hypothesis.—DG\]

There exists considerable evidence from the field of psychiatry to indicate a definite relationship between the sex glands and the brain. Spermatozoa may have an internal function that is necessary for the normal metabolism of the brain, and dementia praecox \[schizophrenia\] may be due to an alteration or deficiency of their production due to degeneration of the seminiferous \[pertaining to the formation of semen\] tubules \[small tubes\] of auto-intoxication \[due to having been poisoned\]. This may result from masturbation and sexual excess in causing a chemical withdrawal from the circulation necessary for the nutrition of the brain.

We must remember that lecithin is a chief constituent of the myelin sheaths of nerve-cells and essential for their activity, during which it is consumed—for it is the nerve-oil that keeps the fire of nerve and brain activity burning. Since lecithin is also a principal constituent of the semen, we can readily understand why excessive sexual activity should lead to lecithin deficiency and under-nutrition of nerve and brain cells. The sex glands influence the brain—their influence on the nerves, however, is more immediate and profound.

Internal secretions of the sex glands stand at the basis of the individual’s physical and mental vitality. Sex hormones are present in the external as well as in the internal secretions of the gonads. The adrenal glands and the testes are embryologically \[considering their origins\] and histologically \[considering microscopic tissues\] closely related. Excessive gonadal \[from the gonads\] activity, by depriving the adrenal cortex \[the outer portion of the adrenal glands that produces several hormones\], of lipoids \[essential structural fatty components of living cells\], leads to its atrophy \[wasting away\].

A Biochemical Understanding of the Origin and Treatment of Neuroses and Psychoses, Based on Knowledge of the Chemical Effects of the Secretions of the Sex Glands, Both Internal and External, Upon the Central Nervous System

Conservation of semen means conservation of sex hormones and increased vigor, while loss of semen means loss of hormones and diminished vitality; also chronic deficiency of such hormones leads to the symptoms of senility.

1\. There is a remarkable similarity of chemical composition between the semen, the brain and the central nervous system, being especially rich in lecithin, cholesterin \[hormones affecting sex organs\] and phosphorus compounds, which would indicate that seminal emissions withdraw from the body substances necessary for the nutrition of nerve and brain tissue.

2\. Loss of seminal vital fluid promotes under-nutrition of the nervous system and brain.

3\. \[Sexual\] Continence is beneficial to the brain.

4\. Physiological evidence, pointing to the fact that the seminal fluid contains substances of great physiological value (such as Poehl’s Spermine, lecithin, cholesterin, vitamin E, sex hormones, etc.)

5\. Leading physiologists, urologists, genito-urinary specialists, neurologists, psychiatrists, sexologists, gynecologists and endocrinologists \[from the early 20th Century\] endorse the physiological value of continence.

Wild animals in a state of nature practice copulation only at certain mating seasons for the purpose of reproduction. Civilized man practices this act at all times, and in most cases without intention to conceive. Such considerations must lead one to the conclusion that the sex life of civilized men is unnatural and that the excessive manifestation of the sex urge among them is due to certain aphrodisiacal stimuli rather than to natural instinct.

The harmful effects of the seminal discharge involve a sudden withdrawal from the body of calcium, lecithin and other substances necessary for the normal functioning of the nervous system. The sudden withdrawal of calcium produced by the seminal discharge biochemically produces the tetany-like symptoms of the orgasm, which are so similar to those of the epileptic attack. During the sexual orgasm of coitus symptoms occur which border on psychopathology \[behavioral disorder\]; and there can be little doubt that excessive frequency of such symptoms may indelibly impress themselves on the brain and nervous system.

The eminent physiologist, Prof. Eugen Steinach has performed experiments which definitely showed that the internal secretions of the sex glands, after being resorbed into the circulation, pass principally to the brain and spinal cord, wherein they are stored. He therefore concluded that the primary action of the internal secretion of the sex glands, after passing into the blood, is upon the central nervous system, through the medium of which, probably by producing local changes in blood supply, effects are produced in various parts of the body.

The physiologist, Nussbaum, conducted similar experiments, on the basis of which he concluded that the internal secretion of the testis had a specific action on certain local groups of ganglion \[brain and spinal cord nerve centers\] cells, and that the influence of the testis on the metabolism of different tissues is intermediated through the nervous system.

In the light of these and other physiological experiments, the fact is well established that the action of the secretions produced by the sex glands and resorbed \[assimilated\] into the bloodstream is primarily on the brain and spinal cord.

## Brain and Semen

Spermatozoa, when not discharged, are resorbed into the blood-stream and carried to the brain. Both in their chemical composition and their elongated form, they have a remarkable similarity to brain-cells, which, like them, lack the capacity of reproduction, in contrast to most other cells of the body which have this capacity.

Loss of seminal fluid involves lowered nutrition of nerve and brain tissue, and, when excessive, it leads to nervous and mental disorders. The concentration of lecithins and cholesterols, both of which are constituents of the semen, the brain and nerves, often vary, depending on intake and outgo. Activity of the sex glands causes withdrawal of both. This means a lessened supply available to the nervous system.

One of the earliest discoveries about the chemistry of the brain is its high content of phosphorus, which is either combined with proteins and sugar as nucleo-proteins, with proteins alone as phosphoproteins, or with lipoids as phospholipins, to which group lecithin belongs. These substances for the best nutrition of nerve and brain tissue are without doubt the phospholipins, including lecithin. They are present in the semen and internally fed to the body when the seminal fluid is conserved and resorbed, rather than wasted. It then becomes a true internal nerve and brain food.

The gray matter of the brain contains 17% lecithin, which is the essential and indispensable medium through which the higher intellectual processes manifest themselves. The greater the purity in which lecithin is found: the higher the intelligence. The quality of these highly organized phosphorus compounds (i.e., lecithin): appears to be vitally connected with the intellectual capacity of the brain. In idiocy and insanity there is a qualitative and quantitative diminution of brain lecithin.

The action of alcohol, like that of anesthetics, is dependent on its activity to dissolve and remove lecithin from the brain; and when the concentration of brain lecithin is sufficiently lowered, insanity is the result. It has been shown by experiment that in the series of agents which act as narcotics, the anesthetic power increases in proportion to the quantity of lipoids that the liquids employed are capable of dissolving from the brain. Sexual excess produces a similar effect; and, together with alcohol, constitutes a principal cause of neuropsychopathic \[disordered nervous behavior\] conditions.

May not the deep unconsciousness that follows sexual activity be due to withdrawals of lipoids from the brain by the sexual orgasm, producing results similar to those that follow the administration of an anesthetic, which likewise withdraws lipoids from this organ?

Since both the brain and the sex organs extract identical substances from the blood, this would mean that there exists a chemical antagonism between them since increased activity of the latter means decreased nutrition of the former. The more lipoids that the sex glands withdraw from the blood, the less is available to the brain. Spermine \[a group of organic compounds (originally isolated from semen), composed of only carbon, nitrogen, and hydrogen\] has also been found to be present in the gray matter of the brain. No two organs show greater similarity in their lecithin, cholesterin and phosphorus contents as the semen and the brain.

These considerations indicate that all loss of seminal lipoids, whether through coitus \[sexual intercourse\], masturbation or nocturnal emissions, is at the expense of the brain: and this effect is most detrimental during childhood and before maturity, when the brain is in the process of growth.

No adequate comprehension of the sexual question can be had without understanding the chemical composition of the semen and spermatozoa. When it is realized that they contain in high concentration phospholipins essential to the nutrition and normal functioning of the central nervous system, it will be realized that withdrawal of these substances from the body by seminal emissions must have an adverse effect on the nutrition of the brain and nerves, predisposing to neurasthenia \[nervous breakdown\] and other nervous and mental affections. Here we have the biochemical basis for a new neurology and psychiatry.

## Chemistry of the Semen

Seminal fluid is composed of the secretory products of the testis, epididymis, \[A long, narrow, convoluted tube, part of the spermatic duct system, that lies on the posterior aspect of each testicle, connecting it to the vas deferens\] seminal vesicles \[sacs\], prostate gland and Cowper’s gland. The semen is a viscid \[thick and adhesive\] albuminous \[coagulatable proteins\] fluid, alkaline in reaction, opalescent \[whitish like an opal\] in color which is very rich in calcium and phosphorus, also in lecithin, cholesterol, albumen, nucleoproteins, iron, vitamin E, etc. In the ejaculation of the normal man, the average amount given off at each ejaculation, which varies with individuals and with the same individual at different times, is about 5 grams; about 226 million spermatozoa are given off; these are rich in phosphorized fats (lecithin), cholesterol (the parent-source of sex hormones), nucleoproteins and iron. An ounce of semen is considered to be equal in value to sixty ounces of blood, of which it constitutes an extract of some of its most valuable of constituents, as far as its vitalizing power is concerned. The semen contains substances of high physiological value, especially in relation to the nutrition of the brain and nervous system. Loss of semen must deprive the organism of vitality and valuable substances necessary for the nutrition of nervous tissue.

Microscopic studies have definitely shown the presence of fatty particles in the interstitial \[narrow spaces between tissues or parts of an organ\] cells and lymphatics \[fluids derived from body tissues that contains white blood cells\] of the testis, the fat content varying with the sexual cycles.

The interstitial cells of the testis, like the brain cells, are characterized by richness in lipoids. It is thus clear that emissions of semen can withdraw considerable minerals from the body, and so predispose to mineral deficiency.

It is due to its prostatic \[relating to the prostate gland\] components that the semen owes its milky turbidness and peculiar odor. This milky turbidness is due to the lecithin globules (fatty-nitrogen-phosphorus compounds) of the prostate gland, the decomposition of which leads to the odor. The prostatic secretion also contains sodium, potassium, calcium, magnesium, chlorides, phosphorus, sulfur, nucleoproteins, albumin, etc. The loss of this \[i.e. prostatic secretion\] deprives the nerve cells of nourishment; and this explains the relation between prostatitis \[inflammation of the prostate gland\] and neurasthenia.

## Chemistry of the Brain

Nerve and brain tissue are fatty substances; approximately half of this fat is cholesterin and approximately half consists of lipoids, half of which is lecithin. These lipoids contain large amounts of phosphorus \[thus phospholipins\]. These phospholipins have a very important function in the brain and increase in quantity with its development. The most important of them is lecithin. The growth of the brain in infancy has been found to be proportional to the lecithin content of the milk. Human milk, intended to nourish a more rapidly growing brain, therefore contains more lecithin than animal milk.

Brain lipoids are of two kinds. Some, like lecithin, are found in other organs, while others, like cephalin, phrenosin and keratin, are found only in the brain. The white matter of the brain contains twice as much cholesterin as the gray matter; the latter, on the other hand, contains twice as much lecithin and three times as much cephalin. This explains the reasons for Lassaigne’s observation that in insane subjects the amount of fat and lecithin in the brain decreases in enormous proportions.

Students of the physiological chemistry of the central nervous system emphasize the fact that in the active protoplasm of each nerve and brain cell, lecithin and cholesterin are the most prominent constituents. They are also the principal constituents of the semen, which, like the brain, is also a fatty substance. These facts clearly indicate the existence of an important biochemical relationship (through the medium of the blood) between the semen and the central nervous system:

The albumens found in the brain are similar to those in the semen. Both are composed chiefly of albumen and lipoids; and both contain more cholesterol, lecithin and phosphorus than other parts of the body. The brain also contains cephalin, cerebrosides, cerebric acid, myelin, neuroplastin and lactic acid.

The sex glands and the brain have an intimate physiological connection with each other, which is antagonistic in the sense that greater activity of one leads to decreased activity of the other. It is thus clear that phosphorus, oxygen and sufficient thyroid hormone (iodine) are all necessary for the normal generation of brain electricity and that in the absence of either of these three elements, there will be deficient brain action. When the lack of lecithin and organic phosphorus is more serious, the brain itself suffers lecithin deficiency and becomes disturbed in its functioning, just as any other starved organ is when deprived of the elements it requires for its normal nutrition and functioning. In this way, psychoses commence to manifest.

## Nerves

It is interesting to note that the cerebro-spinal fluid, like the semen, is rich in calcium, phosphorus, sodium, magnesium and chlorine, and has an alkaline reaction. That they \[seminal losses\] cause spinal weakness is well known.

Lipins, including lecithin, play a role in the maintaining of irritability of the nerves. It is therefore clear that phosphatides, most important of which is lecithin, are of great importance to the nutrition of the nerves, and that an abundant supply of them in the blood makes for the best nutrition of nervous tissue, while a deficiency, as is caused by excessive activity of the sex glands, leads to under-nutrition and diminished functioning of nerve and brain cells, which can lead to the appearance of neuroses and psychoses.

Sexual neurasthenia should be viewed as a condition of lipoidal undernutrition of nervous tissue due to a lack of lecithin and phosphatides in the medullar \[bone marrow\] sheaths—due to the withdrawal of these substances from the blood by the sex glands and their discharge through the semen.

Chemically and physiologically, there appears to be a close relationship between the spinal cord and prostate glands. The removal of the prostate through prostatectomy, which leaves the patient physically, nervously and mentally incapacitated, has also shown that this gland has an important hitherto unsuspected physiological function. Clear evidence of the importance of the prostate secretion to the body is afforded by the study of its loss as occurs in cases of spermatorrhea, a disease characterized by the involuntary emission of prostatic and other seminal secretions unaccompanied by any erotic sensation—a condition closely allied to prostatitis. The loss of lecithin, cholesterin, phosphates, etc. thus occasioned, exercises its most immediate and profound effect on the spinal cord and entire central nervous system.

The loss of prostatic secretion, a constituent of the semen, should tend to cause under-nutrition of nerve-cells by depriving them of lecithin, and thus bring about chronic undernourishment of the nervous system. The fluids elaborated by the testes, the prostate gland and the accessory sex glands are very rich in phosphorus, as are the spermatozoa themselves. The loss of semen must therefore lower the phosphorus content of the blood, for it is from here that these glands derive the phosphorus for the manufacture of their secretions. This must deprive the nervous system of an element so necessary for its nutrition and normal well-being.

Formerly spermatorrhea and gonorrhea were identified as the same disease, and also gonorrhea and syphilis. Spermatorrhea appears to represent a catarrhal \[moist\] inflammation of the genital mucous membranes, accomplished by a mucous discharge—the male homologue of leucorrhea \[thick whitish or yellowing discharge from the vagina\] in the female.

When the inflammation of the genital mucosa advances from a catarrhal to a purulent \[discharging pus\] stage, the discharge assumes a purulent character; and in place of whitish or colorless mucus, there occurs the characteristic yellowish purulent discharges of gonorrhea accompanied by the gonococcus. From the above considerations it is clear that the pathological symptoms of gonorrhea, especially those affecting the nervous system, must have a relation to the adverse effects on nervous tissue produced by previous sexual orgasms and the urethral \[genital\] inflammation and involuntary discharges they produce. That gonorrhea is not entirely due to germ infection, but represents a more advanced state of inflammation of the genital mucosa than spermatorrhea, is indicated by cases of innocent gonorrhea resulting from sexual intercourse during menstruation by married couples both free from the disease, and as the result of masturbation in young girls. It is clear that the neurological symptoms of gonorrhea, like those of spermatorrhea, are produced, if not exaggerated, to a great extent by the loss of lecithin, through the seminal discharges which invariably precede this disease.

As the inflammation of the genital mucosa advances from a purulent gonorrheal, state of inflammation to a fibroid \[with fibers\] and atrophic one, we have the characteristic fibroid growths and cancers of the uterus in the female, while in the male, the cancer-like growths on the sexual organs characteristic of the beginnings of syphilis appear. That the demineralization \[loss of minerals\] and dealkalization of the blood through previous seminal discharges prepare the soil for such cancerous developments, there can be no doubt, while a resulting condition of acid intoxication can prepare the biochemical conditions of the organism for the skin pathologies of secondary syphilis, which bear a resemblance to those that accompany the seminal discharges resulting from the masturbation and involuntary emissions of puberty.

It is clear that there is an important internal physiological relation between the secretions of the sex glands and the central nervous system, that the loss of these secretions, voluntarily or involuntarily, exercises a detrimental effect on the nutrition and vitality of the nerves and brain, while, on the other hand, the conservation of these secretions has a vitalizing effect on the nervous system, a regenerating effect on the endocrine glands and a rejuvenating effect on the organism as a whole.

## Thymus

The phosphatide withdrawal caused by activity of the sex glands and seminal emission exercises a most powerful effect upon the thymus glands, which are most dependent on adequate phosphorus supply for their normal well-being and activity. Now it is interesting to note that coincident with the increased activity of the sex glands at puberty and the subsequent withdrawal by them of phosphatides, the thymus gland degenerates. Such degeneration may be viewed as a product of lecithin deficiency, similar to the endocrine degeneration which McCarrison notes to result from vitamin B deficiency. If lack of vitamin B causes the thymus to degenerate, lack of lecithin, which is similar in its action, should do the same.

It has also been noted that excessive withdrawal of nucleoproteins and other substances from the blood to form spermatozoa may cause diminution in the size of the thymus gland and its atrophy, which probably is the reason why this occurs after puberty. (Could the atrophy of the pineal gland, accompanying that of the thymus, not be due to a similar cause, in view of the richness of the pineal in lecithin?) These facts indicate that the sexual changes of puberty, instead of being the effect of thymus atrophy at this time, are the cause.

However there are individuals in whom this thymus degeneration does not occur and in whom the thymus persist throughout life. It is probable that phosphatide starvation as the result of the seminal emissions of puberty cause the customary degeneration of the thymus at this time. This results from a disturbance of phosphorus metabolism, which leads to a similar disturbance of calcium metabolism.

There is no time in life when the endocrine glands of the individual may be more powerfully affected by a deficiency of phosphatides than during the months of embryonic development, when these glands are most sensitive to their chemical environment, the maternal blood-stream. Deficiency of phosphatides in the mother’s blood at this time, due to ovarian over-activity (as the result of sexual intercourse) may affect the development of the thyroid and other endocrine glands of the embryo, as well as of its central nervous system. This explains the origin of cretins and Mongolian idiots, when born of parents with normal heredity.

## Summary

\[In response to a letter:\] “You ask whether the draining from the body of lecithin and phosphorus through the sexual act will hinder the highest intellectual achievement and debilitate body and brain. Most definitely this is the case. The loss of these nerve-and-brain foods through sexual indulgence in any form deprives the nerves and brain of needed nourishment and leads to nervous and mental disorders.

“Measurements have shown an actual decrease in the lecithin content of the brains of the insane. This was due to previous sex indulgence, as a result of which the sex glands took up the blood’s lecithin to replace expended fluids. Insane asylums are now overfilled with the victims of thoughtless sexual indulgence which has withdrawn valuable nutrients from the brain and disordered its functioning. A time is reached when their brain is so sapped of lecithin that it ceases to function.

“You say: ‘I do not understand the very function of the sexual apparatus.’ It has two functions: internal secretion, which is primary, and reproduction, which is secondary. Any other use of these endocrine organs is a perversion which will reap its penalty in the form of nervous disorder and premature old age.

“You ask me for good literature on the sex question. Permit me to introduce to you some of the most daring and original writers in this field, Melville Keith, M.D. The following is quoted from his work, The Marriage Law:

“We tell you that at every emission of semen you are losing the food and the best portion of the (blood) corpuscles, inasmuch as every particle of semen which is ejected will be replaced by more taken from the blood. This is enough to convince you that when you are ejecting the semen which should stay in the body and become reabsorbed so as to go and form the oil for the joints, new muscles, the brain material, as well as every other part of the body, you are really destroying or throwing away your life.

“You eject the substance of the synovial \[lubricating membrane secretion\] fluid. You send forth, to gratify a moment’s passion, the very material of which the brain is made. This is a fact which you will acknowledge when you consider that all this semen is the material of which all these substances are made and supplied, and when the semen is ejected you have selected the best part of the body to go out and become to you a useless inert mass, which can never be restored to you under any circumstances. It is lost and gone. Then, the semen being out of the body, the sexual penalty comes apace. The penalty for this loss of semen is so far reaching and so concealed from the body of people of the present day that we do not know the exact spot to look for it.

“But we tell you, when you see paralysis, palsy, apoplexy and brain softening you may set it down that there have been sexual losses and a waste of the bodily substance from the sexual orgasm. We assure you nothing can be so very enervating as this sexual excess, and any sexual mating is an excess, providing it is not for the purpose of having children.”

## 2. Classical Continence

It is interesting to note that Dr. Bernard’s “Neuroses and Psychoses” arrives at the same conclusion as do India’s ancient literatures (the Vedas) and their corollary the Ayurveda. Ay-urveda is a branch of the Vedic literature (veda is a Sanskrit word that means knowledge) which deals specifically with ayuh (longevity). There it is stated that the best use of the food is to produce another body.

In the ancient world, scientists and philosophers subscribed to the belief that the reproductive organs produce an internal secretion that flows through one’s body by way of the bloodstream. These secretions, like their external counterparts, perform notable functions. Since the days of Pythagoras, the ancient Greeks specifically had much to say on the matter—they believed that semen retained in the body served a vital function in maintaining one’s good health, both physically and mentally. Indeed, semen that is concentrated within the body becomes nourishment for one’s brain, according to ancient Greek physicians. Experts argued that fornication in copious amounts negated the vital function of this fluid.

## Pythagoras

Pythagoras (582-496 BC) Greek philosopher and mathematician known best for formulating the Pythagorean theorem. He is known as “the father of numbers.” Born on the island of Sá-mos, he settled in Crotona, a Greek colony in southern Italy, where he founded a movement with religious, political and philosophical aims, known as Pythagoreanism. The Pythagoreans, who included some of the greatest mathematicians, astronomers, philosophers and physicians of antiquity, believed in immortality and in the transmigration of souls.

• Pythagoras called semen the “flower of the purest blood.”

Author’s Comment: According to Vedic science, there are seven physical elements or tissues of the body (sapta-dhatus) in relation to the material body.

Within four months from the date of conception, the seven essential ingredients of the body, namely plasma (rasa), blood (rakta), muscle (mamsa), fat (medah), bone (asthi), marrow (majja), and semen (sukra), come into existence.

• Pythagoras taught that there was a direct connection between the semen and the brain and that loss of semen weakens the brain, while its conservation improves the brain’s nutrition, since the substances thus conserved act as brain nutrients.

## Hippocrates

Wikipedia: Portraits from the Dibner Library of the History of Science and Technology

Hippocrates (470-360 BC) is considered the greatest physician of antiquity, and is regarded as the “Father of Medicine” and medical ethics. He was born on the Aegean Island of Cos in Greece and died in Larissa, Greece. Hippocrates traveled widely. His name is associated with the Hippocratic Oath. Approximately 70 works are ascribed to him in the Hippocratic Collection. He left many texts on brain surgery, seizures, spasms, epilepsy, etc. He wrote Sacred Disease, a treatise on epilepsy, which he believed to be caused by insufficient air. It was thought to be carried by the veins to the brain and limbs.

• Hippocrates believed that semen came from all humors of the body.

Author’s Comment: According to Ayurvedic science, semen is found in a subtle state in all the cells of the body, just as sugar is all-pervading in the sugarcane and as butter is all-pervading within milk. The two testes or secretory glands exact semen from the blood just as bees collect honey in the honeycomb drop by drop. The secretory glands then deposit the semen in the seminal vesicle (vesiculae seminalis).

• Hippocrates: “Tabes dorsalis \[syphilis\] proceeds from the spinal cord. It is frequently met with among newly married people and libertines.”

Author’s Comment: Hippocrates believed that involuntary seminal losses also can cause syphilis of the spinal cord, characterized by degeneration of sensory neurons and stabbing pains in the trunk and legs and unsteady gait and incontinence and impotence. Hippocrates advised sexual abstinence and avoidance of alcohol as a cure.

Diseases such as syphilis (known as phairanga-roga in Ay-urvedic texts), which bring on madness, leprosy, etc., are said to have originated from Western zoophilia.

• Hippocrates believed that there was a coordination between the testicular fluid and the nervous system, brain and spinal cord.

It is so subtle that it is the only essential substance that can do all those functions. Had it been gross, it would not be able to move freely through the spinal cord, all the nerve endings, the brain and the cells.

These are the sapta dhatus or the seven tissues that support this life and body. Bone marrow’s concealed essence, the essence of essences, emanates as semen.

THE SEVEN DHATUS: 1) RASA \[plasma) — maintains functions of menstruation (ARTAVA) in the uterus and lactation (STANYA) in the mammary glands. 2) RAKTA \[blood tissue or red blood cells)—maintains muscle tendons (KANDARA) and blood vessels (SIRA). 3) MAMSA \[muscle tissue)-maintains flat muscle (SNAYU) and the skin (TWACHA). 5) MEDA \[adipose tissue)-maintains subcutaneous fat (VASA) and function of sweat (SWEDA). 5) ASTHI \[bone tissue)-maintains teeth (DANTA), nails (NAKHA) and hair (KESHA). 6) MAJJA (bone marrow, nerve tissue)-maintains function of lacrimal secretion (AKSHiVIT SNEHA). 7) SHUKRA \[semen, reproductive tissue)-maintains function of sexual organs.

Aristotle (384-322 BC) more than any other thinker, determined the orientation and the content of Western intellectual history. Born in Stagirus, Macedonia, Greece, and died in Chalcis, Euboea, Greece. His father was Nicomachus, a medical doctor, and his mother was named Phaestis.

## Galen

Wikipedia: Portraits from the Dibner Library of the History of Science and Technology

Claudius Galenus of Pergamum (modern-day Bergama, Turkey) (131-201 AD) better known as Galen, he was an ancient Greek physician and philosopher. His views dominated European medicine for over a thousand years. His father Ni-con, from whom he received his early education, is described as remarkable both for excellence of natural disposition and for mental culture. His mother was a Greek physician. He composed, it is said, nearly 500 treatises on various subjects, including logic, ethics and grammar.

Amongst Galen’s own major works is a 17-volume On the Usefulness of the Parts of the Human Body. He also wrote about philosophy and philology. His collected works total 22 volumes.

• Galen believed that semen came from all humors of the body and that “losing sperm amounts to losing the vital spirits.”

Furthermore, nervous disorders are attributed to deficient kidneys’ failure to store semen caused by excessive sexual activities, intemperate diet, drinking alcohol, etc. This condition is known as shenkui (sometimes translated as “kidney weakness”).

## Lord Dhanvantari

Lord Dhanvantari (appeared during the Satya age)—the twelfth incarnation of the Supreme Lord who is the best of physicians and the father of Ayurveda, the section of the Vedas that expounds the Vedic science of medicine. He appeared when the ocean of milk was churned by the demons and demigods. At that time he carried a jug of nectar which could bestow long life upon those who drank it.

Atharva Veda is one of the four original divisions of knowledge from the Vedic scriptures. It is written in the Sanskrit language. Sanskrit means literally the most reformed language. The Ayurveda contains abundant medical information. The four Vedas were compiled by Srila Vyasadeva approximately 5,000 years ago in the written form. Prior to that, the knowledge was passed on from spiritual master to disciple in the oral tradition.

The author of Charaka-samhita (the major text of Ayurveda), sage Charaka, says that women are liable to be subjected to twenty various yoni (genital disorders). Stri rogam (gynecology) deals with the signs and symptoms of various diseases related to women along with the diagnosis, pathology and treatment. Prasuti-tantra (obstetrics) is concerned with the methods of achieving excellence of reproductive dhatu (tissue), measures for the maintenance of healthy pregnancy, factors injurious to pregnancy, factors responsible for the inhibition of fetal growth and its management, dietary and other regimen for a pregnant woman, child delivery and post-natal care.

## 3. Modern Abstinence

## Boerhaave

Herman Boerhaave (1668-1738) regarded by many as one of the most influential surgeons of the early 18th century, was born at Voorhout about 15 km from Leyden, Netherlands; the son of a Dutch clergyman. He initially studied fine arts and obtained a PhD from the University of Leyden in 1690. He entered medicine at the University of Harderwyk and qualified in 1693. He remained in Leyden for almost his entire career and was appointed Professor of Medicine and Botany in 1709. Among his greatest contributions to medicine were the use of post-mortem examinations to find the cause of fatal illnesses and the use of the Fahrenheit thermometer in the clinical assessment of patients. Boerhaave’s syndrome was named after him.

• Boerhaave, in his “Treatise on the Diseases of the Nerves,” claims that during the sexual orgasm all the nerves are affected, therefore he has called coitus \[sexual intercourse\] a true epilepsy.

Authors Comment: Scientists Havelock Ellis and Emile Roubaud put forth the notion that epileptics normally begin to show signs of epilepsy at the age of puberty and that the symptoms resemble those of sexual orgasm. Years after Ellis and Roubaud presented this hypothesis, authorities take this belief one step further and hypothesize that orgasm actually is the cause of epileptic fits. These fits are caused by a loss of calcium through discharge of semen during orgasm; indeed, the convulsions one experiences during orgasm resemble those experienced during an epileptic seizure. These observances would lead one to believe the two actions are linked.

## Von Haller

Albrecht von Haller (1708-1777) Swiss physiologist, born in Bern, Switzerland, Albrecht von Haller was educated at the University of Tubingen and at the University of Leyden. In 1735, after practicing medicine for eight years in Bern, von Haller accepted the position as chair of medicine, anatomy, surgery and botany at the University of Göttingen.

Von Haller, called “the Great” even in his lifetime, was an illustrious scholar and prolific writer whose interests included poetry, botany, ancient languages, biography and philosophy, as well as medicine. His primary claims to fame are in medical bibliography and physiology. Haller compiled 20 volumes of bibliographies on anatomy, botany, surgery and medicine. As a physiologist, he proved the concept of “irritability” of tissue, distinguishing between nerve impulse (sensibility) and muscular contraction (irritability). In 1747, he published his observations in Primae Lineae Physiologiae (First Lines of Physiology).

• Haller: \[Sexual orgasm\] “is an action very violent, similar to a convulsion, and which of itself astonishingly weakens and affects the brain and nerves.”

Author’s Comment: Those who suffer from epilepsy are in danger of experiencing an attack during or after sex.

## Tissot

© Epilepsy Museum Kork - Museum for Epilepsy

Samuel-Auguste-Andre-David Tissot (17281797) distinguished Swiss professor of medicine and one of the founders of rational medicine. He practiced in the Swiss city of Lausanne. In his most significant book on the traits of epilepsy published in 1770 he describes the symptomatology of nearly every form of epileptic seizures and expresses his views on the pathogenesis, course and prognosis of epilepsy. The book remains one of the cornerstones of modern epileptology. He also was renowned for another popular book published in 1761. It furthered the understanding of sanitary principles and dietetics. He is also credited with establishing systematic evaluation of treatments and correlations of symptoms through post-mortem.

• Tissot believed that orgasms are also sometimes accompanied by a convulsive action, and such violent palpitations are the result of an over-stimulation of the nervous system. Tissot theorized that the extreme activity of the nerves during orgasm is necessarily followed by a depression of the same nerves, and the bipolar nature of this mechanism causes permanent derangement if it occurs too frequently.

Author’s Comment: Put simply, when vital fluid is lost, the body will not be able to think properly. Why? Because the thought process analyzes and assimilates sensory perceptions by breaking them down with a subtle energy. Unnecessary loss of semen puts one out of balance since digestion and assimilation of sensory things becomes diminished and distorted. This causes anxiety about the future and lamentation about the past. The combination of bodily and mental indigestion affects the intelligence and one becomes victim to what is called degenerative diseases.

• Tissot said the wealthy, urban, and especially the intelligentsia with their lives of luxury and idleness, are prone to fight their boredom in a search for stimulation, a search that leads them to practices “opposed to the usage of nature,” including onanism. Once the practice begins, it becomes an addiction leading to enervation and death.

Author’s Comment: Dr. E. P. Miller writes: “All waste of spermatic secretions, whether voluntary or involuntary, is a direct waste of the life force. It is almost universally conceded that the choicest element of the blood enters into the composition of the spermatic secretion. If these conclusions are correct, then it follows that a chaste life is essential to man’s well-being.”

• Tissot said that the increase in blood flow to the brain during orgasm also “oppresses the nerves,” making the individual more susceptible to apoplexy \[a sudden loss of consciousness resulting when the rupture or occlusion of a blood vessel leads to oxygen lack in the brain\], paralysis, epilepsy, insanity and other nervous disorders.

Author’s Comment: Material obsession with sex indulgence is a perverted display of spiritual affection which bears none of the debilitating effects of mundane coitus. Caelius Aurelianus, one of the leading physicians of antiquity, said that “coitus is a brief epilepsy.”

• Tissot wrote that the body is an energy system which needs constant care to maintain equilibrium. The body, he said, is continually depleted of the vital fluids the Greeks called “humors” through excretion. He argued that the unnatural loss of semen weakens mind and body.

Author’s Comment: Once wasted, this powerful energy can never be recouped by any means. A sexual act damages the brain and the nervous system. Thus one should try to preserve every drop of the vital fluid—even if married.

• Tissot believed one ounce of semen lost has the same consequences as the loss of forty ounces of blood.

• Tissot considered the analog of semen in women to be the fluid of vaginal lubrication, and this “seed” is even more precious than male semen because women’s weaker nerves make the loss of female seed far more dangerous than male masturbation.

The intense sensuous excitement of orgasm shatters the nervous system and causes debility as well. Women should preserve their precious vital force.

• Tissot: “The debility of all those affected with convulsive diseases prove that the weakness is produced by the orgasm. The debility caused by these \[sexual\] excesses derange the functions of all organs... digestion, perspiration and evacuation do not take place in their usual healthy manner.”

There are however some very continent men who have no emission of semen for whole years. What then becomes of it if it be not returned to the circulation?”

This immense length shows that it is not only difficult for the semen to be reproduced, but that its excessive loss must be attended with disastrous consequences on the general organism.”

The semen is retained in the seminal vesicles until it is used, or expelled, by nocturnal emissions. During this time, the greater part is reabsorbed by the blood, and produces in entering it remarkable changes.”

## Chassaignac

Charles Marie Idouard Chas-saignac (1805-1879) French surgeon and ophthalmologist, famous for “Chassaignac’s Space” and “Chassaignac’s Tubercle.” He commenced his medical studies in his native town of Nantes, continued in Paris and was promoted doctor in that town on 1835 with a dissertation on fractures of the neck of the femur. Two years later he became chief professor at the faculty. He then held various positions, among them surgeon to the Bureaus Central des Hôpitaux, and vice president of the Anatomical Society. He published the Complete Principles and Practice of Surgery (1835-1837). In 1857 Chassaignac was elected president of the Surgery Society and in 1868 he became a member of the Acadimie de midecine. Other associated eponyms: Chassaignac’s hook; Chassaignac’s operation; Chassaignac’s paralysis.

## Morel

Benedict Augustin Morel (1809-1873) French psychiatrist, introduced the term dementia praecox to refer to a mental and emotional deterioration beginning at the time of puberty. The disorder was renamed schizophrenia in 1908 by the Swiss psychologist Eugen Bleuler. Morel was born in Vienna to French parents. He studied in Paris and obtained his doctorate in 1839. He traveled in the Netherlands, Switzerland, Germany and Italy particularly studying their lunatic asylums. In 1848 he was appointed director of the lunatic asylum Asile d’Aliinis de Mariville at Nancy of Mariville. His name is associated with several diseases: Kraepelin-Morel disease; Morel’s ear; and Morel’s sclerosis.

• Morel found in his research that 75 percent of idiotic children, from one to five years old, showed absence of the thymus gland.

## Acton

Even if the average person’s mind would not have always been absorbed in sexual thoughts, the media makes sure it is. ## Humphreys

Dr. Frederick Humphreys MD (1816-1900) was born in Marcellus, Onondaga County, New York. In 1850 he received his degree as Doctor of Homeopathic Medicine from Pennsylvania Homeopathic Medical College in Philadelphia. In 1853, he assisted in forming and was prominent in the New York State Homeopathic Medical Society. He was made chairman of the “Bureau for the Augmentation and Improvement of the Materia Medica” of the American Institute of Homeopathy. He wrote a Manual of Homeopathic Remedies (1930) and many other works.

## Brown-Sequard

Professor Charles Edouard Brown-Sequard (18171894) French physiologist, neurologist and often considered to be the “father of endocrinology,” was born in Port Louis on the island of Mauritius, of American and French parentage. In 1846, at the age of 29, he graduated Medical Doctor from Paris and then practiced medicine in the United States, Britain and France. In 1854 he returned to Mauritius where he was presented with a gold medal by his countrymen for subduing a cholera epidemic there. He was appointed professor of the Institutes of Medicine and Medical Jurisprudence at the Virginia Medical College in Richmond, Virginia.

Back in Paris he was awarded a prize by the Academie des Sciences and established the Journal de la Physiologie de l’Homme et des Animaux. He won renown for a course of lectures on the physiology and pathology of the central nervous system which he delivered at the Royal College of Surgeons of England.

Brown-Sequard became famous for his spinal cord syndrome which was given his name. Through his work on the localization of the tracts in the spinal cord, he traced the origin of the sympathetic nerve-fibers into the spinal cord.

• Brown-Sequard has shown that spermatic secretions increase nerve and brain vitality.

## Beale

Lionel Smith Beale (1828-1906) Professor of physiology, King’s Royal College, London. He is famous for his earliest published book The Microscope and its Application to Clinical Medicine \[1854\]. He advanced medical science relating to nerves and to germs. This work foreshadowed much later work on bacterial disease, anticipating by five years the microbic theory of disease and Pasteur’s doctrine of immunization. He was the author of Protoplasm; or, Matter and Life, London: J. & A. Churchill.

## Hutchinson

Jonathan Hutchinson (1828-1913) was born in Selby, Yorkshire, England, where his father had a prosperous flax business. He received his professional qualification from Bartholomew’s Hospital in 1850. Hutchinson developed a special interest in congenital syphilis, which was common in London in his time, and he was responsible for delineating the natural history of the disorder. It is said that he saw more than one million patients with syphilis in his lifetime. Hutchinson had a vast clinical experience and he published his observations in more than 1,200 medical articles. Despite his busy practice he produced the quarterly Archives of Surgery, which ran into 11 volumes from 1890 to 1900; although he was the only contributor, this journal had a wide circulation and was held in high regard.

Hutchinson is hard to beat when it comes to presidencies: president of the Pathological Society 1879-1880, the Ophthalmological Society of Great Britain 1884-1885, the Royal College of Surgeons 1889, the Neurological Society 1887, the Medical Society of London 1892, the Royal Medical & Chirurgical Society 1894-1896, and the International Dermatological Congress 1896. He was knighted for distinguished service to medicine in 1908, at age 80.

• Hutchinson: “The belief that the exercise of the sex function is necessary to the health of the male at any age is a pure delusion while before full maturity it is highly injurious.”

## Von Leyden

Ernst Viktor von Leyden (1832-1910) German internist and professor born in Danzig (Gdansk), Prussia, which is now part of modern-day Poland. He studied at the Friedrich Wilhelms Institute in Berlin. He had a great clinical reputation and specialized in neurological problems. Von Leyden published articles on a wide variety of medical topics, and several monographs.

## Hegar

Ernst Ludwig Alfred Hegar (1830–1914) German gynecologist born in Darmstadt, Germany. He spent his student years at the universities of Giessen, Heidelberg, Berlin and Vienna. When the University for Women’s Clinic opened in Freiburg in 1868 he became its first head. There he established an association for obstetrics and founded the journal Contributions to Obstetrics and Gynecology of which he was the first editor. Hegar was among the first to introduce antiseptic principles. He was known as a hard-working man and a skilled surgeon who often began operating at five o’clock in the morning. He retired in 1904. Courtesy Humboldt University of Berlin; Magnus Hirschfeld Archive for Sexology

Professor Paolo Mantegazza (1831-1910) Eminent Italian physiologist, neurologist and anthropologist, was born at Monza. After spending his student days at the universities of Pisa and Milan, he gained his medical doctorate degree at Pavia in 1854. After traveling in Europe, India and America, he practiced as a doctor in the Argentine Republic and Paraguay. Returning to Italy in 1858, he was appointed surgeon at Milan Hospital and professor of general pathology at Pavia. In 1870 he was nominated professor of anthropology at the Instituto di Studii Superiori, Florence. Here he founded the first Museum of Anthropology and Ethnology in Italy, and later the Italian Anthropological Society. His published works include Fisiologia del dolore (1880); Fisiologia deli amore (1896); Elementid igiene (1875); Fisonomia e mimica (1883).

## Allen

Timothy Field Allen AM, MD, LLD, (18371902) respected physician and one of the best exponents of homeopathy. His father was David Allen, a physician of note in Vermont and his grandfather was Silas Allen of Heath, Mass., also a practitioner of medicine.

## Beard

George Miller Beard (1839-1883) a distinguished physician and specialist in the treatment of mental disorders, especially neurasthenia (nervous exhaustion). He was born in Connecticut, attended Yale, served in a non-specialist medical capacity during the civil war and received his medical degree from Columbia University in 1866. Each one of these secretes one or several hormones that act as messengers and take orders to different parts of the body to regulate organic processes, such as growing; blood pressure; temperature; calcium, sugar, phosphorus level and the sexual function. The endocrine system is affected with all kinds of consequences when one of its glands is not working properly.

The leading gland of the endocrine system is called the pituitary gland and the one that gives force to the system is the sexual gland. The famous hipofisis-testicular/ovary axis, that is, pituitary gland sex hormone, controls the hormonal regulation in man and woman. In man, the hipofisis gland is related to the beginning of puberty when it sends hormones to the blood stream for stimulation of the sexual organs. In woman, the hipofisis gland initiates puberty stimulating the follicle’s growth in ovaries. Each follicle secretes female sexual hormones that develop the sexual characteristics in woman. Excessive sex harms the functioning of the endocrine glands.

He believed that nocturnal emissions like spermatorrhea \[involuntary emission of semen without orgasm\] are pathological and a related condition of seminal emission.

Furthermore the loss of considerable quantities of seminal fluid, involuntarily or voluntarily, leads to undernourishment of the cells of the central nervous system, causing them to be weakened and exhausted.

## Forel

Auguste Henri Forel (1848-1931) Swiss neuroanatomist, psychiatrist and entomologist; known as one of the most important psychiatrists of the 19th century; born in Morges (Vaud), Switzerland.
