Author’s Comment: The action of alcohol, like that of anesthetics, is dependent on its activity to dissolve and remove lecithin from the brain, and when the concentration of brain lecithin is sufficiently lowered, insanity is the result. Sexual excess produces a similar effect, i.e. neuroses and psychoses, in that it withdraws lipoids (lecithin) from the brain. From a scientific view, there is sufficient evidence to postulate that a basic cause of nervous and mental disorders is a lecithin deficiency in the organism due to expended secretions.

## 4. Celibate Geniuses

## Kumaras

The Kumaras (born at the beginning of the universal creation) are four learned ascetic sons of Lord Brahma appearing always as children. Their names are Sanaka, Sanatana, Sanat-kumara and Sanandana. They were impersonalists but became great devotees of the Lord and authorities on devotional service. Their line of disciples is called the Kumara-sampradaya.

Author’s Comment: Maitreya Muni (lived in the Dvapara age) was a great sage who instructed Vidura in the science of selfrealization. He was personally instructed by Lord Krishna just before His departure from the earth. He also was an associate of Vyasadeva and held discussions with him. As an authority on Vedic wisdom, Maitreya Muni gives the following two descriptions of the Kumaras:

• Maitreya Muni: “The four great Kumara sages headed by Sanaka, as well as Narada, Rbhu, Hamsa, Aruni and Yati, all sons of Brahma did not live at home, but became urdhva-reta, or naisöhika-brahmacaris, unadulterated celibates.”

Author’s Comment: It is significant to note that from the very beginning of the universe the principle of chastity and celibacy has been practiced. Modern so-called psychologists and physiologists are recommending that people should enjoy their senses as much as possible and have free sex, but that is not what we learn from the fathers and grandfathers of the universe. From the beginning it was encouraged to control the senses and to raise the semina to the brain as indicated by the word urdhva-reta.

It is not unnatural if the secondary creator of the universe, Lord Brahma, and his sons, the Kumaras, accepted the principle of unadulterated celibacy. Today’s permissiveness cannot overturn the entire culture and establishment of science and morality.

• Maitreya Muni: “In the beginning, Brahma created four great sages named Sanaka, Sananda, Sanatana and Sanat-kumara. All of them were unwilling to adopt materialistic activities because they were highly elevated due to their semen’s flowing upwards.”

Author’s Comment: The great Kumara sages are so highly regarded within the Vedic culture that persons interested in overcoming the powerful force of lust are advised to pray as follows:

“May Sanat-kumara protect me from lusty desires” (sanat-kumaro ’vatu kamadevad).

The four Kumaras are sons of Brahma, the first living being within the universe. Because Brahma is in charge of populating the universe, he wanted to share the responsibility with his first sons, the Kumaras, so he requested them to get married, become householders and beget children. However, the boys flatly refused his request and disobeyed his instruction because they wanted to remain unattached from family life. Thus it happened that these boys were empowered to remain perpetually as physically young boys, appearing as if five years old.

• Maharaja Prthu offered his welcome to the four Kumaras, addressing them as the best of the brahmaёas. He welcomed them, saying: “From the beginning of your birth you strictly observed the vows of celibacy, and although you are experienced in the path of liberation, you are keeping yourselves just like small children.”

Author’s Comment: Their example is unique in the universe and even though they disregarded the request of their exalted father, the desire to be celibate and to pursue a spiritual path exclusively is never to be considered faulty. The word “kumaras” means young, celibate boys.

## Narada Muni

Narada Muni (born at the beginning of the universal creation) a son of Brahma, who travels throughout the universes in his spiritual body delivering the science of bhakti (devotional service). He sometimes is called the “eternal spaceman.” He is a pure devotee who leads a life of celibacy and is the spiritual master of many exalted personalities such as Vyasadeva, Prahlada, Dhruva, etc.

• Narada Muni: “In this way, increasingly overwhelmed by illusion, King Puranjana, although advanced in consciousness, remained always lying down with his head on the pillow of his wife’s arms. In this way he considered woman to be his ultimate life and soul. Becoming thus overwhelmed by the mode of ignorance, he could not understand the meaning of self-realization, of his self or of the Supreme Personality of Godhead.”

Author’s Comment: Modern man has disregarded the directions of God and scriptures as well as sages and thinkers when it comes to the purpose of life. As explained here by Narada Muni, the purpose of life is self-realization, not simply sense gratification. Therefore, even within marriage one has to understand the ultimate goal and not become captivated simply by the temporary pleasures of male-female interaction. Married men and women are not spared ridicule by the enlightened if they overindulge their senses.

• Narada Muni: “A student should practice completely controlling his senses. He should be submissive and should have an attitude of firm friendship for the spiritual master. With a great vow, the brahmacari should live at the guru-kula, only for the benefit of the guru.”

Author’s Comment: It is for this reason that the author has begun the Rupanuga Vedic College under the direction of his spiritual master. All intelligent men are welcome to attend without tuition charge. Similarly, parents are invited to consider sending their sons to our college when they reach adulthood.

Here we learn about the proper student training by the exalted authority, Narada Muni. He says that the students should practice celibacy or brahmacarya. One who practices brahmacarya or celibacy is called brahmacari. The whole environment should be devoid of the sensual stimulus. In other words, a child should not be exposed to illicit types of sexual behavior, by viewing, hearing about, experiencing or associating with those who are engaged in these types of activities.

• Narada Muni: “Woman is compared to fire, and man is compared to a butter pot. Therefore a man should avoid associating even with his own daughter in a secluded place. Similarly, he should also avoid association with other women. One should associate with women only for important business and not otherwise.”

Author’s Comment: One spiritual master said, “Boys are good and girls are good, but together they are bad.” He meant that when boys and girls associate indiscriminately together, they degrade themselves just as butter melts when in close proximity with fire.

Young persons are very receptive and eager to learn, so whatever they come in contact with, they will be influenced greatly.

• Narada Muni: “All the rules and regulations apply equally to the householder and the sannyasi, the member of the renounced order of life. The grhastha, however, is given permission by the spiritual master to indulge in sex during the period favorable for procreation.”

Author’s Comment: The period favorable for procreation as mentioned here by Narada Muni is six days after the menstrual period. Otherwise, there is no chance of having procreation so sex performed at other times would be considered illicit sex and should not be performed. In this way, even marriage is to be regulated for the higher purpose of self-realization, and in this way husband and wife voluntarily agree to help each other and to help their children to become spiritually oriented persons. It also is said by Narada Muni that one should not become a father or mother unless he or she can deliver their dependents from the cycle of birth and death— freeing them from having to take birth again in the material world.

## Nara-Narayana Rsi

Nara-narayana Rsi (lived during the Svayamb-huvaMillennium) appeared as an incarnation of the Supreme Lord in the form of twin sages born of Dharma and Murti. Nara and Narayana Rsis taught by example the practice of austerities and lived a life of celibacy. In Kalapa-grama, Narayana Rsi instructed the great sage Narada.

• Amongst all kinds of living entities begotten by Brahma, namely men, demigods and animals, none but the sage Narayana is immune to the attraction of maya in the form of woman.

Author’s Comment: The twin sages, Nara-Narayana Rsi, were once tested by Cupid while they were performing their austerities of celibacy. Cupid executes the thankless task of causing all creatures in the material realm to fall into love or lust with each other by shooting them with his five types of arrows. He tried this also with Nara-Narayana Rsi by sending extraordinarily beautiful women to entice Them away from Their chastity vows, but it was unsuccessful because the twin sages were uninterested. In fact, They displayed Their own mystic power by bringing forth women far more beautiful than Cupid’s. In this way they remained unattracted by Cupid’s powers. The analogy is given that a confectioner does not become bewildered if someone offers him some sweets because he already has sufficient sweets of his own creation.

• Narayana Rsi: “O son of self-born Brahma, once long ago on Janaloka, wise sages who resided there performed a great sacrifice to the Absolute Truth by vibrating transcendental sounds. These sages, mental sons of Brahma, were all perfect celibates.”

Author’s Comment: This statement confirms that the sons of Brahma were perfect celibates respected by intelligentsia and that celibacy has been practiced since the beginning of the creation.

## Kapila

Kapila (lived during the Satya age) appeared as an incarnation of Krishna. He was the son of Devahuti and her husband Kardama Muni. Kapila led a life of celibacy and expounded the devotional Sankhya philosophy, vis. the analysis of matter and spirit, as a means of cultivating devotional service to the Lord. There also is an atheist named Kapila, but he is not an incarnation of the Lord.

• Kapila: “One should practice nonviolence and truthfulness, should avoid thieving and be satisfied with possessing as much as he needs for his maintenance. He should abstain from sex life, perform austerity, be clean, study the Vedas and worship the supreme form of the Supreme Personality of Godhead.”

Author’s Comment: The advice given by Kapila to be clean, study scriptures, worship God, etc. is quite respectable and standard even for our Western culture as delineated by Lord Jesus and other authorities. The practice of nonviolence, truthfulness, not stealing are natural moral principles that should not be given up. Also included in the list is chastity which should not be given up. If chastity is abandoned, we also will be inevitably obliged to relinquish nonviolence, truthfulness, the avoidance of stealing, being satisfied with one’s own maintenance, etc. Once society opens Pandora’s box and disregards one of the divine principles, the tendency will be to stop the other practices as well. This is unmistakably visible today.

• Kapila: “The infatuation and bondage which accrue to a man from attachment to any other object is not as complete as that resulting from attachment to a woman or to the fellowship of men who are fond of women.”

Author’s Comment: One may wonder why this male-female attachment is considered to be so severe. This attachment is like the bondage of chains. Sex desire keeps all living entities, humans and other species, imprisoned inside the material world, birth after birth. Thus, one is forced by nature to transmigrate from body to body simply to enjoy this so-called highest pleasure in the material world.

## Hanuman

Hanuman (lived in the Treta age)  was a lifelong celibate. For those who are unfamiliar with the Vedic culture, Hanuman is a popular and famous personality as a devotee of Lord Rama. As we can see from the illustration, he is a monkey, technically appearing in a race called the Vanaras. He was instrumental in rescuing Rama’s wife, Sita, who was captured by the demon Ravana. Hanuman was a great fighter possessed with tremendous powers and it was he who first ascertained Sita’s whereabouts when she was kidnapped. He was able to jump over a large portion of the Indian Ocean to the island of Sri Lanka, where Ravana was residing.

## Bhismadeva

Bhismadeva (lived prior to the advent of the Pandava dynasty) was the son of King Santanu and the river Ganges personified. Bhismadeva is unique in history, for although in line to become the king, he took a vow to remain celibate throughout his life and thus he is known as Bhisma, or one who took an “awesome vow.” Aside from being the most formidable warrior, he also is recognized as one of the twelve mahajanas, authorities on devotional service to the Lord. At the end of his long life he instructed King Yudhisöhira in the science of dharma (divine duty).

Bhismadeva’s vow of celibacy took place as follows: Bhismadeva’s father (Emperor Santanu) wanted to remarry after his first wife returned to the heavenly abode. However, the intended bride’s father (a fisherman) placed stringent conditions to the proposed marriage. The fisherman desired that his grandson-to-be should inherit the throne of Santanu instead of Bhisma. Knowing the greatness of his son Bhismadeva, Santanu could not accept the offer. Nevertheless, he lamented the inability to marry the fisherman’s exquisite daughter Satyavati. When Bhismadeva learned of his father’s lamentation from one of the confidential royal ministers, he paid the fisherman a visit. Bhismadeva promised to the fisherman that he would not accept the throne and that the fisherman’s future son would become the emperor. The fisherman, however, was afraid that Bhismadeva’s future son(s) might compete with his daughter’s son for the throne, and so he demanded assurance that his daughter’s future son would attain the throne uncontended by both Bhismadeva and any future sons or grandsons of Bhismadeva. Bhismadeva could understand the conditions, and thus he immediately took a vow to never marry throughout his life. The fisherman knew the value of Bhismadeva’s vow and so agreed to the marriage of his daughter to Santanu. When the emperor heard of his son’s astonishing vow, he was so moved by his son’s sacrifice that he blessed him to not die unless and until he desired to do so. Bhismadeva remained a warrior prince surpassing all others in prowess, assisted by celibacy.

## Sukadeva Gosvami

Srila Sukadeva Gosvami (circa 3000 BC) was the son of the author of the Vedas, Srila Vyasadeva. He heard and memorized the 18,000-verse beautiful story of the Personality of Godhead, Srimad-Bhagavatam, from his father. Soon afterward, amidst an august assembly of sages, he recited it from memory to King Pariksit during the last seven days of the king’s life. Su-kadeva Gosvami led a life of celibacy.

• Srila Sukadeva Gosvami: “A man or woman who indulges in sexual intercourse with an unworthy member of the opposite sex is punished after death by the assistants of Yamaraja in the hell known as Taptasurmi. There such men and women are beaten with whips. The man is forced to embrace a red-hot iron form of a woman, and the woman is forced to embrace a similar form of a man. Such is the punishment for illicit sex.”

Author’s Comment: Equality between the sexes is experienced in some punishments. We prefer not to think about the possibility of hell to be faced for misconduct but the learned saint gives us a glimpse of what to expect as a reaction for our lusty affairs. This is further explained:

Generally a man should not have sexual relations with any woman other than his wife. According to Vedic principles, the wife of another man is considered one’s mother, and sexual relations are strictly forbidden with one’s mother, sister and daughter. If one indulges in illicit sexual relations with another man’s wife, that activity is considered identical with having sex with one’s mother. This act is most sinful. The same principle holds for a woman also; if she enjoys sex with a man other than her husband, the act is tantamount to having sexual relations with her father or son. Illicit sex life is always forbidden, and any man or woman who indulges in it is punished in the manner described in this verse.

The most important austerity, according to Sukadeva Gosvami, who is considered to be one of the top authorities in Vedic knowledge, is celibacy. It is the first austerity that one should practice.

• Srila Sukadeva Gosvami: “To concentrate the mind, one must observe a life of celibacy (brahmacaryena) and not fall down. One must undergo the austerity of voluntarily giving up sense enjoyment.”

Author’s Comment: Vivid examples of brahmacaris who accepted this principle of life are the four Kumaras, Narada and others. Even today in India the system of brahmacarya is held in high esteem and millions of students practice it. About 25 years ago we visited South Africa to help establish a training seminary. Among those participating in our program were 22 young unmarried men born from East Indian heritage aged 17 through 28. I was astonished to learn that none of them had indulged in any type of sex throughout their lives. The results in India are equally impressive.

## Prophets Elijah and Elisha

Elijah, also known as Eliahu or Elias (c.927-c.850 BC) often considered the loftiest prophet of the Old Testament. He is especially known for his successful contest at Mt. Carmel. Elijah learned at Mt. Horeb that Elisha (also known as Eliseus) was selected by God to be his own (Elijah’s) successor. Elijah found Elisha working the plough with oxen and made him his disciple. Elisha inherited the wonder-working power of Elijah.

• Elijah and Elisha were celibate all their lives. (Zohar Hadash 2:1; Midrash Mishlei 30, 105, Pirke Rabbi Eliezer 33).

Author’s Comment: Today there is a strong prejudice against lifelong celibacy among many members of the Jewish religion including its priestly class. It is so prominent that there exists no formal arrangement for any member of the male section to pursue such a commitment—yet in the Jewish scriptures both Elijah and Elisha embraced lifelong celibacy.

## John the Baptist

John the Baptist (between 8 and 4 BC-about 27 AD) according to all four Gospels he is the precursor of Jesus Christ, born in Judaea, the son of the priest Zacharias and Elizabeth, cousin of Mary, the mother of Jesus. He led a celibate life. John was a Nazarite from birth and prepared for his mission by years of self-discipline in the desert. At about age 30 he went into the country around the River Jordan preaching penance to prepare for the imminent coming of the Messiah. He baptized penitents with water as a symbol of the baptism of the Holy Spirit that was to come. With the baptism of Jesus, his office as precursor was accomplished, and his ministry came to a close soon afterward. John angered Herod Antipas, the Judaean ruler, by denouncing him for marrying Herodias, the wife of his half-brother Herod, and was thus imprisoned. At the request of Salome, daughter of Herodias and Herod, John the Baptist was beheaded.

• “But the fact that John the Baptist probably lived a celibate life and that in the community of his disciples it was held in high esteem would support the supposition that Mary’s choice of virginity belonged to this new cultural and religious context.”

—Pope JohnPaul II

Author’s Comment: The example and teaching of John the Baptist is that our sexual desire is simply a perverted reflection of our heart’s deepest longings to love God. If we knew this we could all be happy. However, in the darkness of the modern age it is very difficult to convince anyone that there is anything wrong with sex at all.

## Jesus Christ

Jesus Christ (0-33) (Iesous, the Greek form of Joshua or Jeshua, contracted from Jehoshua, meaning help of Jehovah, or saviour; Christos, anointed) also called Jesus of Galilee or Jesus of Nazareth; founder of Christianity, one of the world’s largest religions. He led a celibate life. Jesus was born in Bethlehem, Israel, of the Virgin Mary; his father was Joseph. He was baptized by John the Baptist and later chose 12 disciples as apostles. He was crucified in Jerusalem.

• Jesus Christ: “Ye have heard that it was said by them of old time, Thou shalt not commit adultery.” (Matthew 5. 27)

Author’s Comment: Adultery is on the rise. Since 1970, the rate of single parenting has increased from 1/10 families to 1/3 families (Norton & Miller 1992), and there have been dramatic increases in teenage sexual activity and teenage childbearing without marriage (Furstenberg 1991).

• Jesus Christ: “For there are some eunuchs, which were so born from their mother’s womb: and there are some eunuchs, which were made eunuchs of men: and there be eunuchs, which have made themselves eunuchs for the kingdom of heaven’s sake. He that is able to receive it, let him receive it.” (Bible: Book of Matthew 19.12)

Author’s Comment: Poetic and potent—Christ here stresses the importance of sexual abstinence as a primary means to attain the spiritual kingdom.

There is a story about a bridegroom’s party who had to go to the bride’s house down the river. It was settled that they would start at night by boat and reach the destination early in the morning. Therefore at night, after supper, the jubilant party got aboard a boat, made themselves comfortable and instructed the boatmen to start. Since all the members of the party were seated comfortably and since the river breeze was very pleasant, they slept soundly that night. In the morning they all got up early, but to their astonishment they saw that the boat had not moved an inch toward the destination, even though the boatmen had rowed vigorously all night long. Finally, after inquiring, they found that despite the boatmen’s rowing, the boat had not moved because they had failed to raise the anchor. The marriage ceremony was thus spoiled because of a foolish mistake.

Illicit sex life is the mainstay of the anchor that keeps us from reaching the spiritual destination. As long as we do not draw the anchor of attachment to mundane sex life, we shall be unable to cross the ocean of material existence.

• Jesus Christ: “But I say unto you, that whosoever looketh on a woman to lust after her hath committed adultery with her already in his heart.”

—(Bible: Book of Matthew 19.28)

Author’s Comment: Technically in the Bible “adultery” refers to those who are married to another and “fornication” refers to the unmarried. Lord Jesus confirms the Vedic principle that a lustful look itself is a break in brahmacarya or celibacy. From the Ayurvedic science it is explained that lustful glances or thoughts create an internal discharge causing virya or semen to become separated from the system. Thus there will be a tendency for the semen to flow downward and will flow out of the body when the opportunity arises such as during dreams.

## Apollonius of Tyana

Apollonius of Tyana (1st century AD) born in Cappadocia, Asia Minor (modern Turkey, east of the Taurus Mountains and north of Syria). Apollonius was a charismatic teacher and miracle worker frequently compared to the Jewish sage and miracle worker Jesus of Nazareth. Apollonius tried to reform cultic practices in modern Greece, Turkey and Syria. He had several disciples, traveled extensively, met important Roman officials such as emperors Vespasian, Titus, and Domitian and discussed with several other philosophers. He lived a celibate life.

• “He \[Apollonius\] declined to wear apparel made from dead animal products and, to guard his chastity, he abstained from all animal foods. He resolved never to wed nor to have any sex relations whatever with women.”

—Philostratus

Author’s Comment: Aside from the scientific effects, it is easy to understand why, since the beginning of time and religion, many religious sects require their devotees to practice abstinence, much for the same reason they require devotees to abstain from eating meat or meat byproducts, especially when they observe certain religious holidays. Examples of ancient religions that practice this abstinence include the Orphics, Pythagoreans, Essenes, Gnostics and neo-Planists. For members of these religions, abstinence equals continence, and it is one of the only ways to achieve rejuvenation of body and mind. Specifically, Apollonius of Tyana was a strict vegetarian, incorporating a diet abundant in fruits and vegetables into his chaste lifestyle. Because of this, he was better able to maintain his chastity throughout his long life.

• “In this respect he \[Apollonius of Tyana\] greatly surpassed Sophocles, who only said that in reaching old age he escaped from a mad and cruel master; but Apollonius, by dint of virtue and temperance in eating, never even in his youth was so overcome. While a mere stripling, in full enjoyment of his bodily vigor, he mastered and had complete control over this maddening passion.”

—Philostratus \[The biographer of Apollonius\]

A book by Philostratus; Illumination on parchment; National Szichinyi Library, Budapest

Philostratus (c170-c245 AD) the biographer of Apollonius of Tyana. He was probably born on the isle of Lemnus, one of the overseas territories of Athens; in his writings, he indicates that he spent a part of his youth at the island. From an inscription it has been deduced that his full name was Lucius Flavius Philostratus, a threefold name that means that he was of Roman citizenship. His father, who was known as an author, sent his son to Athens to study rhetoric under Proclus of Naucratis. He visited Tyana (in modern Turkey), where a temple was dedicated to the charismatic teacher Apollonius. Maybe it was on this occasion that Julia Domna commissioned Philos-tratus’ biographical work, Life of Apollonius, which was finished during the reign of Severus Alexander.

## Plotinus

Plotinus (204-269 AD) philosopher and author of the Enneads in the line of Plato, was born at Lycopolis in Egypt. He led a life of celibacy. He once joined an expedition of the emperor Gordia-nus against the Persians and as a result gained an acquaintance with Eastern (Persian and Indian) thought. After the failure of this campaign, Plotinus went to Rome in 244 and founded a school over which he presided until his death.

• “Plotinus’ own lifestyle was rigidly regulated by his philosophical principles: he was said to have limited his sleep and food to a minimum, was a strict vegetarian and completely celibate.”

—Molloy College Philosophy Department, New York

Author’s Comment: The lifestyle of Plotinus was comparable to the famous Six Gosvamis (renunciates) of Vrndavana, about whom it is written:

“I offer my respectful obeisances unto the six Gosvamis—Sri Rupa Gosvami, Sri Sanatana Gosvami, Sri Raghunatha Bhaööa Gosvami, Sri Raghunatha dasa Gosvami, Sri Jiva Gosvami, and Sri Gopala Bhaööa Gosvami—who cast off all aristocratic association as insignificant. To deliver poor, conditioned souls, they accepted loincloths and became mendicants, but they were always merged in the ecstatic ocean of the gopis’ love for Krsna, and they were always bathing repeatedly in the waves of that ocean. I offer my respectful obeisances unto the six Gosvamis, who were engaged in chanting the holy names of the Lord and bowing down in a scheduled measurement. In this way they utilized their valuable lives and in executing these devotional activities they conquered over eating and sleeping and were always meek and humble enchanted by remembering the transcendental qualities of theLord.

## Porphyry

Porphyry Malchus of Tyre (233-309) was born in Tyre (now Sur, Lebanon) to Syrian parents and studied in Athens, before joining the Neoplatonic group of Plotinus in Rome were he studied philosophy. As a young man, Porphyry tried to gain as broad a knowledge as he possibly could by studying many languages and religions. He wrote extensively on vegetarianism including his On Abstinence from Killing Animals. He wrote a commentary on Euclid’s Elements which was used by Pappus when he wrote his own commentary.

Another important contribution made by Porphyry was in writing his Life of Pythagoras. Certain important fragments of other mathematician’s writings also could have been preserved in the works of Porphyry including Nicomachus and Eudemus. He led a celibate life.

• “He \[Porphyry\] lived an austere celibate life.”

—Peter Kirby

Author’s Comment: When I first moved into the Hare Krishna asrama (spiritual center) we practiced celibacy, ate only spiritual, vegetarian foodstuffs, engaged in devotional service to guru and Krishna throughout the day and at night slept on the bare floor using a blanket or sleeping bag. Porphyry would have been pleased with our attempts.

## Proclus

Proclus Diadochus (410/412-485 AD) was the last of the great Platonic teachers. Poet, philosopher and scientist, he was born in Constantinople into a well-off family. Proclus was sent to Alexandria for schooling until, as a teenager, he moved to Athens where he studied at Plato’s Academy under the philosophers Plutarch and Syrianus. Soon he himself was teaching at the Academy, and later he succeeded Syrianus as director, a position he held for the rest of his life. The title Diadochus was given to him at this time, the meaning of the word being successor. Proclus was author not only of many Platonic commentaries but also numerous astronomical, mathematical and grammatical works the most important of which are Elements of Theology, a systematic evaluation of Neoplatonic metaphysics, and the Platonic Theology. He led a celibate life.

• “Proclus was a vegetarian and never married, and was very highly regarded by his contemporaries.”

—M. AlanKazlev

Author’s Comment: Here we see the link between vegetarian diet and celibacy. It is not accidental—there is a connection, that if one eats properly, it will be easier to practice celibacy, and he will be more inclined to do so as well.

• “Olympiodorus offered Proclus his daughter in marriage, but while admiring her attainments in personal dignity and philosophy, he chose to remain unmarried throughout his life.”

—Theosophy Library Online

Author’s Comment: Proclus was not a “woman-hater.” He appreciated the good qualifications of Olympiodorus’s daughter; however, he felt disinclined to accept marriage because he already was experiencing a higher taste from his devotion and celibacy. Although such an achievement is rare, its value is not diminished. Diamonds too are rare but they are not less important for that fact.

Because he chose not to associate intimately with women also does not imply that he was effeminate or homosexual. I must admit, when I first encountered devotees of Hare Krishna, I incorrectly assumed and accused them of being homosexual, because it seemed unimaginable how young Americans could live without some form of sexual activity.

## Sankaracarya

SankarAcarya(788-820) an incarnation of Lord Siva who appeared in a village called Kaladi in Kerala, South India to a brahmana couple, Sivaguru and Aryamba. He took the lifelong vow of celibacy (sannyasa) while barely a teenager. He is famous for the commentaries he wrote establishing an impersonal philosophy similar to Buddhism whereby he substituted the term “Brahman” (spirit) for Buddha’s void nirvana. SankarAcaryatraveled all over India defeating the great scholars of the day and converting them to his doctrine of Mayavada, the advaita (non-dualism) interpretation of the Upanisads and Vedanta.

• Sankaracarya: “The breasts of women, especially young women, are nothing but a combination of muscles and blood, so one should not be attracted by the illusory energy of raised breasts with nipples. They are agents of maya \[illusion\] meant to victimize the opposite sex.”

Author’s Comment: Inordinate fascination with women’s breasts is so conspicuous in modern culture that very little in the field of advertising or entertainment can take place without their prominence.

## Fra Angelico

Fra Angelico (1387-1485) also known as Blessed Giovanni de Fiesole, or “the angelic friar,” was born Giovanni Pietro, in the Mugello region to the north of Florence. He is famous as a Dominican friar and a saintly artist. He led a life of celibacy. The monks at the monastery greatly admired both Fra Angelica’s work and piety. “Fra Angelico does not paint, he prays,” said one of them. His art, in effect, was a canticle of prayer. In the state of grace he placed his angels in the florid gardens of Heaven.

## Michelangelo

Michelangelo (1475-1564) his full name is Michelangelo di Lodovico Buonarroti Simoni. He was a Renaissance painter, sculptor, poet and architect. He led a continent life. He was born at Caprese, a village in Florentine territory, where his father, named Ludovico Buonarroti Simoni was the resident magistrate. He is famous for creating the fresco ceiling of the Sistine Chapel, one of the most stupendous works in all of Western art, as well as the Last Judgement over the altar, “The Martyrdom of St. Peter” and “The Conversion of St. Paul” in the Vatican’s Cappella Paolina. Among his many sculptures are those of the Pieta and David, sublime masterpieces of their field, as well as the Virgin, Bacchus, Moses, Rachel, Leah, and members of the Medici family.

• “Michelangelo consistently reaffirmed his conviction to remain celibate... The master artisan simply had more passion for art and for the care of his father and brothers than he did for sexual adventure.”

—Beck

We have gone down with no deficiency diseases, and we are not walking round in agonies of sexual frustration. Far from it. We are pleased to be freed from the dark and desperate urges of the body. It is a wonderful liberation, a release, a life-enhancing choice.”

• “Benvenuto Cellini, whose sexual adventures are detailed in a three volume autobiography, frequently refers to Michelangelo’s oath of celibacy with hostility and derision.”

—Beck

## Pascal

Blaise Pascal (1623-1662) a French mathematician, physicist and religious philosopher. His contributions to the natural sciences include the construction of mechanical calculators, considerations on probability theory, studies of fluids, and clarification of concepts such as pressure and vacuum. Following a profound religious experience in 1654, Pascal abandoned mathematics and physics for philosophy and theology. He led a continent life. In honor to his scientific contributions, the name pascal has been given to a unit of pressure and to a programming language, as well as to many

• “Pascal never married because of his decision to dedicate his life to physics, philosophy, religion, and mathematics.”

—Carole Bersani

Author’s Comment: Pascal is famous for having devised a wager called Pascal’s Wager, in which he weighed the odds in favor or against, pro or con spiritual life. His wager went like this:

Let us then examine this point, and say, “God is, or He is not.” But to which side shall we incline?... You must wager. It is not optional. Let us estimate these two chances. If you gain, you gain all; if you lose, you lose nothing. Wager, then, without hesitation that He is \[that He exists\]... There is here an infinity of an infinitely happy life to gain, a chance of gain against a finite number of chances of loss \[namely one life\].

—Blaise Pascal, Pensees #233

The summary of Pascal’s Wager would be:

• If God REALLY exists, and we believe and serve Him—we derive an infinite gain, heaven.

• If God REALLY exists, and we don’t believe that and disregard His instructions, then we have the potential of an infinite loss (hell, or at least separation from God).

• If God really does NOT EXIST, and we believe that God exists, we essentially lose nothing.

• If God really does NOT EXIST, and we believe that God doesn’t exist, we essentially gain nothing.

He was born in Amsterdam, Holland, to parents who had fled from Portugal. He led a continent life. Bertrand Russell described him as “the noblest and most lovable of the great philosophers.”

## Newton

Sir Isaac Newton (1642-1727) was an English alchemist, mathematician, astronomer, scientist and philosopher. Newton published the Philosophiae Naturalis Principia Mathematica where he described universal gravitation and, via his laws of motion, laid the groundwork for classical mechanics. Newton also shares credit with Gottfried Wilhelm Leibniz for the development of differential calculus. He led a continent life.

## Leibniz

Gottfried Wilhelm von Leibniz (1646 -1716) German philosopher, scientist, mathematician, diplomat, librarian, and lawyer of Sorb descent. Born in Leipzig, Germany, Leibniz is generally, with Newton, jointly credited for the development of modern calculus in particular, for his development of the integral and the Product rule. He led a continent life.

## Handel

George Frederic Handel (1685-1759) the foremost composer of oratorio Baroque music, most famous of which is “The Messiah.” Handel was born in Halle, Germany; in 1706 he left for Italy and produced operas. ## Kant

Immanuel Kant (1724-1804) German philosopher, considered by many the most influential thinker of modern times. Born in Konigsberg, Germany (now Kaliningrad, Russia), Kant received his education at the Collegium Fredericianum and the University of Konigsberg. Complicated in thought but simple in lifestyle, Kant wrote and taught on a broad range of subjects from physics to metaphysics, from theology to philosophy. He led a continent life.

## Beethoven

Ludwig Van Beethoven (1770-1827) born in Bonn, Germany was one of the greatest composers of all time and was revered for his talent throughout the world even during his lifetime. He composed entire symphonies “in his head,” hearing the part for every instrument before he set the first note on paper. He composed nine symphonies and wrote five piano concertos, piano sonatas, string quartets and one opera. He led a continent life.

—“The Immortal Beloved” (Beethoven’s Website)

## Schopenhauer

Arthur Schopenhauer (1788-1860) is one of the most important 19th century philosophers. Schopenhauer was born in Gdansk (now Poland), the son of Heinrich Floris Schopenhauer and Johanna Schopenhauer. Arthur Schopenhauer studied at the University of Gttingen and was awarded a PhD from the University of Jena. In 1820, Schopenhauer became a lecturer at the University of Berlin. In his most famous work, The World as Will and Representation, he espoused a sort of philosophical pessimism that saw life as being essentially evil and futile, but saw hope in aesthetics, sympathy for others and ascetic living.

• “The celibacy of Schopenhauer, Kant and Nietzsche has reduced German philosophy to...”

## Thoreau

Henry David Thoreau (1817-1862) writer, philosopher and naturalist. He was born in Concord, Mass., and associated with the Concord-based literary movement called New England Transcendentalism. After graduating from Harvard in 1837, Thoreau held a series of odd jobs. Encouraged by Concord neighbor and friend Ralph Waldo Emerson, he started publishing essays, poems and reviews in the transcendentalist magazine The Dial. The American classic book Walden; or, Life in the Woods chronicles his experiment in self-sufficiency. He led a continent life.

• “Thoreau, however, was certainly a Puritan in his habits, if not in his philosophy. He was celibate, vegetarian, a teetotaller, otherwise abstemious, frugal...”

## Spencer

Herbert Spencer (1820-1903) was a highly acclaimed British social philosopher, often regarded as one of the first sociologists. Spencer was born in Derby, England, and was mainly self-educated. He led a continent life.

## Nietzsche

Frederick Nietzsche (1844-1900) German philosopher, foresaw a European collapse into nihilism. In works of powerful and beautiful prose and poetry he struggled to head off the catastrophe. He was born in Rocken, Prussia, studied classical philology at the universities of Bonn and Leipzig and was appointed professor of classical philology at the University of Basel. A prolific writer, he wrote several major works, among them The Birth of Tragedy (1872), Thus Spoke Zarathustra (1883-1885), Beyond Good and Evil (1886), On the Genealogy of Morals (1887), The Antichrist (1888), Ecce Homo (1889), and The Will to Power (1901).

• “Kaufmann \[his chief biographer\] argues that Nietzsche lived a celibate life.”

• “Nietzsche’s relationships with women were outwardly quite ‘correct.’”

—H. L. Mencken

## Tesla

Nikola Tesla (1856-1943) Serbian-American inventor and genius who discovered the rotating magnetic field—the basis of most alternating-current machinery. He immigrated to the United States in 1884 and sold the patent rights to his system of alternating-current dynamos, transformers and motors to George Westinghouse the following year. In 1891 he invented the Tesla coil, an induction coil widely used in radio technology. Tesla was a lifelong celibate, and he turned to vegetarianism later in life.

## Sajous

Charles Eucharist de Medicis Sajous (1852-1929) a prominent name in the late 19th-century medical profession as physician, endocrinologist, teacher, author and editor. He was born in France and came to the United States at age nine, where he received further education from private tutors before attending the University of California and Jefferson Medical College in Philadelphia.

He was the first president of the Association for the Study of the Internal Secretions. He served as professor at Temple University. In 1898, Sajous assumed the editorship of Sajous’s Analytic Cyclopedia of Practical Medicine. Ten editions (six to eight volumes each) of this work were issued before his death. Dr. Sajous also was the author of The Internal Secretions and the Principles of Medicine (two volumes, 1903-1907), in which he reviewed all the available literature on endocrinology and set forth his own views on the subject. From 1911 to 1919, Sajous was managing editor of the New York Medical Journal.

• Sajous states that lecithin is a conspicuous component of the brain, nerve cells and semen. Further he believes that lecithin and lipins of the myelin sheaths have a nutritive function in relation to the nerves.

Author’s Comment: Vital fluid contains profuse amounts of lecithin which keeps arteries and the heart strong by preventing them from hardening or deteriorating. It also helps the body absorb Vitamin A, which is important to the good health of cell membranes. The cell membranes are comprised of a lecithinladen protective surface, which also can be found in the muscles and nerve cells.

• Sajous: “Lecithin, therefore, becomes the functional ground-substance of the cell-body of the neuron, just as it is in the nerve. Both in the neuron and its continuation, the nerve, therefore, the vascular fibrils carry blood-plasma, which, by passing through their walls, maintains a continuous reaction, of which the phosphorus of the lecithin and the oxygen of the blood-plasma are main reagents, and chemical energy is the end-result.”

Author’s Comment: In addition to cell membranes, phospholipids also are essential to the health of other glands and organs, such as the reproductive tract and muscles. The functions of the cell membranes are numerous: ion exchange, regulation of pressure in the bloodstream, and transfer of wastes, to name a few. Lecithin, composed mostly of Vitamin B, choline, inositol and phosphorous acids, aids in the support of the circulatory system. Lecithin also protects the body from viral infections and cleans harmful substances out of the kidneys. Sajous believes that myelin doesn’t merely cover the sheath of the membranes. It also provides nerve-electricity by way of oxidation. Therefore, lecithin is essential in maintaining the health of the membranes’ sheaths.

• Sajous: “I believe the liquid portion of the semen is rich in adrenal principle. This is suggested by the fact that spermin, the purest of testicular preparations, given the same tests, acts precisely as does the adrenal principle... The inference that spermin consists mainly of the adrenal product suggests that it is not specific to the testes, but instead, a constituent of the blood at large; not only did this prove to be the case, but it was found in the blood of females as well as in that of males.”

Author’s Comment: Like the testes in males, the ovaries of the female body produce internal hormones similar to seminal fluids. Like these fluids, the hormones contribute to a woman’s physical and mental health. And, like their male counterparts, the female prostate produces many similar alkaline fluids. Females also have semina called technically ovum. Both seminas have reproductive purposes as well as nutritional, internal purposes for body and brain.

## Spitzka

Dr. Edward Charles Spitzka (1852-1914) the eminent brain anatomist, neurologist and psychiatrist. He was the son of United States immigrants, educated in urban public schools without contacts or resources to attend prestigious institutions. After graduation from medical school in New York City, he returned to Eastern Europe and studied in Leipzig and Vienna, gaining extensive training in embryology. He founded the American Journal of Neurology and Psychiatry in 1882, served as the chief liaison for neurology at the Ninth International Medical Congress (1887), was elected president of the American Neurological Association in 1890 and was selected as the principle representative to the Congress of Arts and Sciences (1904).

• Spitzka presented case studies in which masturbation caused insanity. He claimed this is primarily due to arrested brain nutrition which results from the withdrawal from the circulation of brain-nourishing lecithin and other phosphorus compounds through excessive seminal discharges.

This combustible substance contains immense energy that can rejuvenate the brain and nervous system during periods of weakness or inactivity. Indeed, the nerve fibers, the brain and the semen are dependent on lecithin for its stored energy. Thus withdrawal of energy through sexual activity deprives these entities of their much-needed energy.

• Spitzka: “That a connection between the development of the mind and the male genitals exists is indisputable. Even if we assume that the defective development of the genital system found in brain monstrosities, idiots, imbeciles, original monomaniacs and the periodically insane is an accidental accompaniment of the neural maldevelopment, we must admit the convincing proof that the early extirpation \[removal\] of the testicles, as in eunuchs and castrated animals, exerts an influence on the mental complexion and development.”

Excessive ven-ery and masturbation have from time immemorial been supposed to exert a deleterious influence on the nervous system, and may provoke insanity, partly through their weakening effect on the general nutrition. That there is a close connection between pathological nervous states and the sexual function is exemplified in the satryriasis \[excessive and uncontrollable sexual desire\] of mania and in the early stages of paretic dementia \[insanity\], as well as in the sexual delusions of manomania \[being possessed by a ghost or strong supernatural force\], and abnormal genital sensations of that condition. In the former case the sexual exaltation is a result, in the latter the genital sensations are collateral phenomena of the psychosis, but there are certain cases in which, while an original predisposition may have existed, masturbation is the factor responsible for the production of insanity.”

## Fischer

Hermann Emil Fischer (1852-1919) German Nobel Prize-winning chemist, considered by some to be one of the greatest scientists of all time, was born at Euskirchen, in the Cologne district as the son of a successful businessman. He studied chemistry at the University of Bonn and took his PhD at Strasbourg University in 1874. He served as Professor of Chemistry at the University of Wurzburg and University of Berlin.

Fischer made great contributions in the fields of sugars, amino acids, proteins and enzymes. Throughout his life he was well-served by his excellent memory, which enabled him to memorize manuscripts of lectures that he had written. When he died, the Emil Fischer Memorial Medal was instituted by the German Chemical Society.

• Fischer considered the sex glands as reservoirs of lipoids which are to be released into the blood to energize the brain. Conversely, he concluded that through external emission, the sex glands withdraw lipoids from the blood and thus indirectly from the brain.

• Fischer: “The content of the brain in combined lipoids seems, then, to have some relation to intellectual power and to its modifications as well.”

## Von Gruber

Max von Gruber (1853-1927) Austrian physician, bacteriologist and hygienist. He was born in Vienna as the son of Ignaz von Gruber, the first specialist in otology in Austria, and publisher of a two-volume textbook on medical chemistry (1835). Max von Gruber studied medicine at the University of Vienna, receiving his medical doctorate in 1876. He became the president of the Bavarian Academy of Sciences and contributed significantly in the study of typhoid. Later he became professor at Munich University.

Author’s Comment: Kindly read the following extract of a recent article describing the passionate rise of Alzheimer’s cases, keeping an open mind about the possibility of a connection between Americans’ sexually-free youth and the king of degenerative diseases.

## Alzheimer’s Cases Expected to Triple

By Kathleen Doheny

Monday, Aug. 18, \[2003\] (HealthDayNews) the number of Americans with Alzheimer’s disease (news—web sites) will triple by 2050, according to new predictions. Although the projected figures come as no surprise to those familiar with the brain disorder, one expert calls the new research a “wake-up call” to prevent a crisis.

Currently, 4.5 million older Americans have Alzheimer’s disease; the new study says that the figure could balloon to 13.2 million by 2050. Unless new ways are found to prevent or treat the disease, the dramatic rise in cases is inevitable, says the study’s author, although he hopes the prediction won’t become reality.

“We don’t want these projections to come true,” says Dr. Denis A. Evans, director of the Institute for Healthy Aging at Rush-Presbyterian-St. Luke’s Medical Center in Chicago. His study is published in the August issue of the Archives of Neurology.

To achieve his Alzheimer’s forecast, Evans and his research team first looked at the incidence of Alzheimer’s over a four-year period among 3,913 Chicago residents ages 65 and older.

They calculated the national prevalence of Alzheimer’s and then used Census Bureau (news—web sites) data to project the number of cases to the year 2050. The projections, Evans adds, assume that not much progress will be made in combating the disease, for which there is now no cure.

Evans says his estimate is higher than some previous ones partly because “the science of doing these projections has progressed a bit in the last 15 years, so we think these are more accurate.” Among other factors, the new techniques take into account the longer life span of Americans as a result of modern medicine.

Sheldon Goldberg, chief executive officer of the Alzheimer’s Association, calls the findings “a wake-up call.” He adds: “This is not the first study \[to predict the coming increase in cases\]. It really validates earlier research that says from a public health standpoint, a crisis is coming.”

Alzheimer’s patients suffer memory problems that grow progressively worse, are often confused, and can become quite agitated.

## Strumpell

Ernst Adolf Gustav Gottfried von Strumpell (18531925) German neurologist famous for postulating “psychic trauma” as the cause for hysteria. He was at various times professor of medicine at Leipzig, Breslau and Vienna. He is credited with classic descriptions of polioencephalomyelitis in children (1885) and pseudosclerosis of the brain (1898). From 1883 to 1884 he published a two-volume text on the pathology and treatment of internal diseases. The second volume included his original description of ankylosing spondylitis. The disease was described independently by Pierre Marie in 1898; it is now sometimes called Marie-Strumpell disease.

• Strumpell considered that sexual continence was not injurious and is indirectly useful in preventing venereal infection.

## Mott

Sir Frederick Walker Mott (1853-1926) eminent neurologist and author, largely responsible for the establishment of the Institute of Psychiatry as a postgraduate institute of the University of London (1923). Mott was born at Brighton, Sussex, and was a pioneer of British biochemistry. He was knighted in 1919 and became a Fellow of the Royal Society in 1896. His books include Effects of High Explosives upon the Central Nervous System. Hearnshaw wrote of Mott in 1964, “Mott’s work at Claybury constituted the most important stream of psychiatric research in the country prior to the First World War.”

• Mott noted that the primary cause of brain degeneration in the majority of insane subjects he studied was habitual masturbation which caused testicular degeneration (im-potency). He concluded that spermatozoa may have an internal function that is necessary for the normal metabolism of the brain and that schizophrenia may be due to an alteration or deficiency of their production due to degeneration of the seminiferous tubules of auto-intoxication.

Author’s Comment: Schizophrenia means “fragmented mind” and typically is a catastrophic illness that begins in adolescence or early adulthood. Because the symptoms of schizophrenia often produce severe incapacity, the illness was originally called “dementia praecox” meaning “premature insanity.” Schizophrenia’s essential features are psychotic features, e.g. delusions, thought disorder, emotional incongruity, hallucinations, functional deterioration onset before the age of forty-five. It initially was defined in the late 19th century by Kraepelin and a team of psychiatrists who worked with him, which also included Alois Alzheimer. Schizophrenia, or dementia praecox, was originally distinguished from dementia in the elderly (later named Alzheimer’s disease) because it occurred in relatively young people rather than older people. It is hypothesized that the emotions that are the initial cause of metabolic changes are accompanied by chemical processes that cause temporary or chronic mental disturbances.

• Mott found that there is a definite relationship between the sex glands and the brain, i.e. degeneration of the brain is usually accompanied by a degeneration of the sex glands. He also noted that in cases of schizophrenia, many of whom were habitual masturbators, there was noted by atrophy of the adrenal cortex together with progressive atrophy of the testicles.

Author’s Comment: Although there may have been other factors at work, it appears that masturbation contributed to their schizophrenia.

## Neisser

Albert Ludwig Sigesmund Neisser (1855-1916) German physician and bacteriologist. He was born in Schweidnitz \[now Swidnica, Poland\]. One of the greatest names in the pioneering work for the diagnosis and treatment of venereal diseases was Albert Neisser. Neisser is remembered for his discovery of the gonococcus, the bacterium that causes gonorrhea, for his work on leprosy and the contagion of syphilis in man and animals.

Neisser began his medical studies in Breslau in 1872 and received his medical degree in 1877. He worked in the dermatology clinic originally founded by Heinrich Koebner (1838-1904) where Neisser discovered the gonococcus at the age of 24. He designed and organized the building of a new dermatological clinic, opened in 1892 which became an internationally famous research center.

Several medical terms were named after him: Neisser’s diplococcus; Neisser-infection; Neisseria; Neisseria Catarrhalis; Neisseria hoemolytica; Neisseria meningitidis; Neisseria sicca; and Neissericeae.

• Neisser: “Most of our young men could remain continent much longer than in the case nowadays.”

Author’s Comment: Arguably one of the world’s greatest authorities on venereal disease expresses regret that young men are being drawn into sexual activities more than they should be—and that was a hundred years ago.

## Freud

Sigmund Freud (1856-1939) Austrian physician, neurologist and founder of psychoanalysis. Freud was born in Freiberg (now Moravia, Czech Republic) and educated at Vienna University. When he was three years old, his family, fleeing from the riots then raging in Freiberg, moved to Leipzig. Shortly thereafter, the family settled in Vienna, where Freud remained for most of his life.

• Freud attributed neurasthenia \[neurosis\] to “immoderate masturbation or spontaneous emission.”

Author’s Comment: Despite the prominent role Freud played in ushering in a sexual new age, he did have the forthrightness to attribute seminal loss by masturbation as a cause for pernicious effects on brain functioning.

In his article Sigmund Freud, C. George Boeree, Professor of Psychology at Shippensburg University writes, “Freud felt that masturbation, oral sex, homosexuality, and many other things we find acceptable in adulthood today, were immature.”

## Belfield

William Thomas Belfield MD (1856-1929) American bacteriologist from Chicago, considered the first to write an original general bacteriology book in English. He was president of the American Urological Society. The book entitled Diseases of the Urinary and Male Sexual Organs was published in New York by W. Wood & Co. (1883).

• Belfield claims that other glands take part in sex hormone production in addition to the testes and ovaries. He therefore concludes that there is “a force independent of the gonads which enters into the determination of the sex features.”

Author’s Comment: The importance of the prostate secretion is being emphasized. Belfield explains that along with the prostatic fluid lost during sexual emissions go vital hormones produced by the prostatic gland.

## Kraepelin

Emil Kraepelin (1856-1926) born in Neustrelitz, Germany, often regarded as “the father of modern psychiatry.” Kraepelin developed the first widely accepted classification of mental illnesses, established the categories of manic-depressive psychosis, bipolar disorder and dementia praecox, which he defined as the “sub-acute development of a peculiar simple condition of mental weakness occurring at a youthful age” (now called schizophrenia). According to the eminent psychologist H. J. Eysenck in his Encyclopedia of Psychology, Kraepelin should be credited with being the founder of modern scientific psychiatry, psychopharmacology and psychiatric genetics. Kraepelin also was a colleague of Alois Alzheimer, and co-discoverer with Alzheimer of Alzheimer’s disease. His fundamental theories on the etiology and diagnosis of psychiatric disorders which form the basis of all major diagnostic systems in use today, especially the American Psychiatric Association’s DSM-IV and the World Health Organization’s ICD system.

• Kraepelin endorsed the physiological value of sexual continence. He wrote that sexual continence is not injurious, and that its advantages in avoiding venereal infection are apparent.

Author’s Comment: Dementia is an organic brain syndrome, which can occur as a result of a variety of neurological diseases, that results in global cognitive impairments. Dementia of the Alzheimer’s type is the primary degenerative dementia because statistically it is the most significant dementing disease usually occurring in more than 50% of demented patients. Alzheimer’s disease is usually divided into three stages; mild, moderate and severe.

The mild stage begins with memory, attention, speed dependent activities, and abstract reasoning dysfunction and mild language impairments begin to surface. In the moderate stage, language deficits such as aphasia \[partial or total loss of the ability to articulate ideas or comprehend spoken or written language\] and apraxia \[total or partial loss of the ability to perform coordinated movements or manipulate objects in the absence of motor or sensory impairment\] become prominent. Dysflu-ency, paraphasias and bizarre word combinations are common midstage speech defects.

In the severe stage, the patient is gradually reduced to a vegetative state. Speech becomes nonfluent, repetitive and largely non-communicative. Auditory comprehension is exceedingly limited, with many patients displaying partial or complete mutism. Late in the course of the disease many neuropsychological functions no longer can be measured. Also primitive reflexes such as grasp and suck emerge. Death usually results from a disease, such as pneumonia, which overwhelms the limited vegetative functions of the patient.

Kraepelin is considered the foremost authority in today’s world of psychiatry, and the fact that he does not feel sexual continence is injurious but rather advantageous is most significant. It would behoove today’s psychiatrists to carefully consider this in their prescriptions for patients. If they really care about their patients, they should advise them at the outset to stop the misuse of their sexual abilities.

## Lydston

G. Frank Lydston (1858-1923) professor of Genitourinary Surgery at the Medical School of the University of Illinois. He was born in Jacksonville, Tuolomne County, and authored many books including Impotence and Sterility.

• Lydston considered the results of sexual excess and masturbation to disturb blood chemistry and general metabolism caused by the withdrawal from the body of the substances of which the semen is composed: calcium, phosphorus, lecithin, cholesterol, albumen, iron, etc.

Author’s Comment: According to Ayurvedic texts, sexual energy may be transformed into ojas or spiritual energy by controlling the sex impulse, conserving the vital fluid and then diverting it into higher channels. This type of sex sublimation is not a matter of suppression or repression, but a positive, dynamic conversion process.

• Lydston stated that for persons with weak nervous systems, repeated sexual orgasms can procure “actual structural alterations of nerve-fibers and cells and vessals of the brain, with coincident psychopathic phenomena,” which “are naturally to be expected as occasional results of these severe and repeated shocks to the susceptible nervous system produced by the sexual orgasms.”

Author’s Comment: In 1907 Alzheimer published his landmark case “A unique illness involving the cerebral cortex” in which he described a fifty-five year old demented woman. The case was very unusual for two reasons: its clinical course, and the discovery of a striking microscopic lesion in the woman’s brain. The clinical course was unusual because of the young age of the patient and the rapidity of degeneration (the patient died within four and a half years of symptom onset). At autopsy, neuropathological findings were even more unusual. One quarter to one third of cerebral cortical \[the extensive outer layer of gray matter of the cerebral hemispheres, largely responsible for higher brain functions, including sensation, voluntary muscle movement, thought, reasoning and memory. Source: Merriam-Webster Medical Dictionary, © 2002 Merriam-Webster, Inc.\] neurons had disappeared, and many of the remaining neurons contained thick, coiled masses of fibers within their cytoplasm.

• Lydston: “Moderation in sexual intercourse is not only conducive to prolonged virility, but to longevity. It is certain that many cases of neurasthenia \[nervous breakdown\] in both male and female are due to sexual excess.”

Author’s Comment: By loss of semen, a considerable amount of substances of high physiological value is lost, creating a lessened supply available to the nervous system.

• Lydston: “From a priori considerations involving the immediate effects of sexual excitement and indulgence upon the brain and spinal cord, we might naturally expect insanity to be a frequent result of masturbation and excessive venery \[indulgence in sexual activity\].”

Author’s Comment: Dr. Alzheimer presumed that a chemical change had occurred in the neurofibrils, thus he described for the first time neurofibrillary tangles (NFT), which together with senile plaques (SP) are considered to be the neuropathologocal hallmarks of Alzheimer’s disease. Alzheimer concluded that he discovered a unique entity separate from senile dementia as it was known at that time.

Note: Amyloid plaques are components of senile plaques. (American Journal of Pathology; July, 2004)

One of the challenges that remains to be solved by the human race is the dementia epidemic. Dementia (senility) is not the natural outcome of aging, rather it is the result of a variety of possible abnormal brain processes. For instance in Alzheimer’s disease (the most important dementing disease) the formation of senile plaques and NFT (neurofibrillary tangles) in the brain have been proven to be the main causal factors of the disease.

• Lydston: “Sexual excess is the most prolific cause of that most civilized and most fashionable of all hydra-headed diseases, neurasthenia.”

Author’s Comment: Another name for neurasthenia is Chronic Fatigue Syndrome (CSF).

• Lydston: “Considering the abundant sensory and sympathetic nerve supply of the prostate and its intimate relation to the sympathetic nervous system in general, the frequency with which nervous symptoms develop in patients suffering from prostatic disease is not remarkable... It is the author’s belief that the prostate secretes a hormone, the perversion of which, conjoined with the absorption of infection toxins, often has much to do with the etiology of sexual neurasthenia.”

Author’s Comment: Sexual excess and prostatitis cause nervous disorders because of considerable loss of phosphorus. Sexual activity entails the loss of vital fluids containing the most essential elements and hormones and chronic deficiency of such hormones leads to the symptoms of and diminished vitality and senility.

• Lydston: “Continence per se, probably never is harmful. The non-elimination of the seminal secretion from the testes often is productive of great bodily and mental vigor.”

Author’s Comment: Lydston directly opposes modern theories encouraging sex. To maintain, as do modern medical spokespersons, that one can expel these seeds and vital fluids with no consequence is, according to Lydston, absurd to the highest degree. Furthermore, such a proposition would mean that all the scriptures of the world are a farce, the greatest scientific minds of the early 20th century were helplessly mistaken, classical ancient Greek minds were in total illusion, the Chinese system of medicine is corrupt and the world’s oldest system of medicine Ayurveda (still extant) is bogus.

• Lydston: “One may be perfectly healthy and physically vigorous while leading a life of absolute continence.”

Author’s Comment: When will the courageous scientists, medical practitioners, psychiatrists, psychologists, biologists come forward with the evidence to destroy the myth of illicit sex without consequences?

• Lydston: “As might be inferred from the fact that sexual excess and masturbation bear an important relation to locomotor ataxia, spermatorrhea is associated with that form of nervous disease more often than any other. The evil habit of masturbation, if continued, produces great irritation of the procreative organs—especially of the seat of sexual sensibility in the prostatic urethra... Erotic dreams result, with losses of seminal secretion. This may merge into true spermatorrhea, the morbid condition finally becoming so pronounced that with little or no provocation, losses occur in the daytime. Spermatorrhea, in the majority of instances is the result of sexual excess or masturbation, and, moreover, the effects of the venereal organs being expended upon the nervous system, it is rational to infer that the disease when fully developed essentially is a neurosis.”

Author’s Comment: According to modern writers, acceptance of masturbation and sexuality in the 20th century can be traced back to the work of three important pioneers in their respective fields: Sigmund Freud, Alfred Kinsey and Betty Dodson. Sigmund Freud normalized masturbation and sexuality, Alfred Kinsey proved that people masturbate, and Betty Dodson showed how to masturbate and enjoy sexuality. There is a Bengali proverb: Pagale kim abole chagale kim na khaoya—what will a madman not speak and what will a goat not eat? To write books and make videos encouraging society to indulge in more wastage of the vital fluid is, in Lydston’s view, itself madness leading to still further insanity.

## Ellis

Henry Havelock Ellis (1859-1939) English physician famous for his extensive case histories (10,000 men and women), medical research and anthropological reports. He was born in Croydon, London, as the son of a sea captain. He traveled widely in Australia and South America before studying medicine at St. Thomas’s Hospital in London. His interests in human biology and his personal experience led Havelock Ellis to write his six-volume Studies in the Psychology of Sex.

• Havelock Ellis remarked that the symptoms of orgasm bear a strong resemblance to those of epilepsy, and he referred to the statement of this effect by the sophist of Abdera, who said that orgasm is a slight fit of epilepsy, “judging it to be an incurable disease.”

• Ellis noted that the epileptic convulsion in some cases involves the sexual mechanism and that it is noteworthy that epilepsy tends to appear at puberty.

Author’s Comment: Epilepsy is defined as a disorder of the central nervous system characterized by loss of consciousness and convulsions. The connection between semen and the central nervous system has been well established and the similar characteristics of orgasm and epilepsy also are difficult to ignore. Thus it is warranted to draw a connection between the two as Ellis does. One who is able to resist the urge to use the genital for sex is called a goswami (go—senses; swami—master). Such a person can make disciples all over the world.

• Havelock Ellis: “The brain and the sexual organs are the great rivals in using up bodily energy, and there is an antagonism between brain vigor and extreme sexual vigor, even though they may sometimes appear at different periods in the same individual.”

Author’s Comment: This statement provides a classic and succinct description worth remembering. We learn a most important lesson:

THE BRAIN AND THE GENITAL ARE RIVALS IN USING SEMEN

There is no known cure for Alzheimer’s disease or many other dementing diseases. Several medications are available but their effectiveness is limited. Three different labs (Bowen et al., 1976; Davies & Maloney, 1976; and Perry et al.), reported low levels of choline acetyltransferase, the marker enzyme for acetylcholine 1977, in the brains of patients who died from Alzheimer’s disease. Acetylcholine deficiency has since been the target of most therapeutic efforts in Alzheimer’s disease.

• Havelock Ellis: “The masters of all the more intensely emotional arts have frequently cultivated a high degree of chastity. This is notably the case as regards music. One thinks of Mozart, of Beethoven, of Shubert. At the age of twenty-five, when he had already produced much fine work, Mozart wrote in a letter that he had never touched a woman.”

Author’s Comment: During the 1970s, several studies have shown that demented patients have low levels of the neurotransmitter Acetylcholine. A modern expert (see below, next Author’s Comment) explains that Acetylcholine is lost during orgasm. Consider this carefully for an explanation of why excessive orgasm leads to Alzheimer’s disease.

• Havelock Ellis: “When we have realized how profound is the organic convulsion involved in \[the\] process of detumescence \[after orgasm\], and how great the motor excitement involved, we can understand how it is that very serious effects may follow coitus \[orgasm\].”

Author’s Comment: “Semen contains hormones, neurotransmitters’ chemicals and brain/spinal fluids. Ejaculation suddenly discharges these life-support chemicals from the brain/spine and the local muscles, in conjunction with burning the sexual hormones DHEA and testosterone, and the neurotransmitters acetylcholine and dopamine. The deficiency of these chemicals in the local joints and muscles sets off the alarm in the sensory nerves. In the brain, you feel dizzy and drowsy upon ejaculation due to the deficiency of the hormones DHEA and testosterone and the neurotransmitters acetylcholine and dopamine. That is why ejaculation makes men fall into sleep in few minutes.”

Oesterling—a former Professor of Tubingen University, Germany.

• Oesterling: “One cannot repeat too often that abstinence and the most absolute purity are perfectly compatible with the laws of physiology. Sexual indulgence is not more justified by physiology and psychology than by morality and religion.”

## Steinach

Eugen Steinach (1861-1944) eminent Viennese physiologist, best known endocrinologist of his day as the discoverer of the puberty gland. He gained worldwide acknowledgment for his theory of aging. He deduced that an increased internal hormonal production would ensue following the cessation of the secretory output of the gonads.

• Steinach’s research showed that testes and ovaries secrete hormones into the bloodstream that influence not only physical development but also sexual behavior. These secretions, he argued, were responsible for the “sexualization” of the brain as male or female.

Author’s Comment: Eminent German physiologist Paul Hoffman wrote: “One easily understands why there is so intimate a connection between the brain and the testicles, since these two organs separate from the blood the most exquisite part of the lymph... The seminal fluid is distributed in all the nerves of the body; it seems to be of the samenature.”

• Steinach has performed experiments which definitely showed that the internal secretions of the sex glands, after being resorbed into the circulation, pass principally to the brain and spinal cord, wherein they are stored.

• Steinach concluded that the primary action of the internal secretion of the sex glands, after passing into the blood, is upon the central nervous system.

Author’s Comment: The semen-energy ascends up to the brain. The idea that sexual restraint retains vigor has a strong scientific tradition reached a forceful expression in the work of Eugen Steinach, who became a professor in Prague in 1895, before returning to Vienna as professor of physiology. Steinach found that the secretion of the prostate gland facilitates fertilization, since spermatozoa are impotent without it.

• Steinach considered the internal secretions of the sex glands to stand at the basis of the individual’s physical and mental vitality and that sex hormones are present in the external as well as in the internal secretions of the gonads. Many of the effects attributed to such hormones are due to the physiological effect of resorbed semen.

Author’s Comment: According to Ayurvedic science, semen is changed into spiritual energy by chastity, elevating thoughts of the self, prayer, meditation, chanting God’s names, worship and yoga just as heat is changed into light and electricity or as a chemical substance is purified by raising it through heat into vapor.

• Steinach regarded conservation of semen to mean conservation of sex hormones and increased vigor, while loss of semen meant loss of hormones and diminished vitality. Steinach concluded that chronic deficiency of such hormones leads to the symptoms of senility.

Author’s Comment: Internal secretions of the sex glands provide an individual’s physical and mental vitality and many of the effects attributed to such hormones are due to the physiological effect of resorbed semen.

## Moll

Albert Moll (1862-1939) physician, born at Lissa, Germany, and educated at the universities of Breslau, Freiburg, Jena, and Berlin (MD 1885). He took postgraduate courses at Vienna, Budapest, London, Paris and Nancy, and in 1887 established himself as a neuropathologist. Edward L. Thorndike wrote: “Dr. Moll is a gifted physician of long experience whose work with those problems of medicine and hygiene which demand scientific acquaintance with human nature has made him well known to experts in these fields. With dignity and frankness Dr. Moll combines notable good sense... critical, matter-of-fact mind, long experience as a medical specialist, and wide scholarship.”

• Moll: “At the present time, most medical men agree that sexual abstinence, in a general way, is not harmful.”

Author’s Comment: The fact that today’s scientists and medical men have indicated that sexual abstinence is harmful brings suspicion upon all professions involved since they have so blatantly disregarded the sage advice of all the great former experts in the field, such as Moll, Kraepelin, etc.

## Crile

George Washington Crile (1864-1943) highly accomplished pioneer in the field of surgical physiology, was born near Chili, Ohio. He received the MD degree in 1887 from University of Wooster Medical Department (later absorbed by the Western Reserve University School of Medicine) in Cleveland. Crile’s first monograph was awarded the Cartwright Prize by Columbia University in 1897. In 1900 Crile became clinical professor of surgery in the Western Reserve School of Medicine, and in 1911 professor of surgery.

He wrote extensively on blood pressure, hemorrhage, transfusion, anemia, anesthesia and resuscitation as well as on other surgical diseases such as cancer, peptic ulceration and diseases of the biliary tract. He compiled a very large series of successful operations (25,000 by 1936) on patients with hyperthyroidism. He was a founder of the American College of Surgeons and served as its president (1916-1917) and on its board of regents (1913-1941).

• Crile believed that the autonomic nervous system and the endocrine glands controlled energy release within the body, and that a number of disease states resulted from overproduction and release of energy.

• Crile demonstrated that the electrical energy of the nervous system is generated in the brain, which represents a central storage battery of the body.

Author’s Comment: This explains the adverse effects of fluid loss on the brain and nervous system, which depend on phosphorous, oxygen and thyroid hormones to operate effectively. Brain malnourishment likely will occur with the outpouring of fluid and the subsequent deterioration of the thyroid.

## Aschaffenburg

Gustav Aschaffenburg (1866–1944) born in Cologne, Germany; pioneering professor of psychiatric criminology. Aschaffenburg produced one of the first systematic analyses of the psychiatric causes of crime in his seminal 1903 study as Crime and Its Repression that stressed both individual-hereditary and social-environmental factors. He also founded the leading journal in the field.

• Aschffenberg: “Even those who are predisposed to nervousness do not suffer any harm from sexual continence if the impression is awakened in them that abstinence can never be injurious.”
