Author’s Comment: Two of the greatest tragedies of the 20th and 21st centuries inflicted by the medical profession are the abortions of fetuses and the wide promotion of the notion that sexual abstinence is detrimental to human health.

## Obregia and Parhon

Anastasie Obregia (1864–1937) chemist and first professor of organic chemistry at Technical University of Iasi, Romania in 1892.

Constantin I. Parhon (1874-1969) the founder of the Romanian School of Endocrinology. He was professor at the University of Bucharest, member of the Romanian Academy and member of other Romanian and foreign Academies and scientific bodies. Practicing first as a neurologist and then as a psychiatrist, C. I. Parhon investigated the cell structure of the spinal marrow and demonstrated that there were vegetative centers on the cortex. He collected clinical evidence as to psychosis, nervous syphilis, epilepsy and multiple hallucinations. In 1909 he co-authored with M. Goldstein the world’s first book on endocrinology, Internal Secretions.

• Obregia and Parhon concluded that spermatozoa may have an internal function that is necessary for the normal metabolism of the brain, and that dementia praecox may be due to an alteration or deficiency of their production due to degeneration of the seminiferous tubules of auto-intoxication.

• Obregia and Parhon confirmed Mott’s observations by finding degeneration of the seminiferous tubules and absence of spermatogenesis in dementia praecox \[insanity\].

Author’s Comment: The French psychiatrist Benedict A. Morel (1809-1873) introduced the term dementia praecox which means any of several psychotic disorders characterized by distortions of reality and disturbances of thought and language and withdrawal from social contact.

## Blair-Bell

William Blair-Bell (1871-1936) was co-founder and first President of the Royal College of Obstetricians and Gynecologists. The second son of William and Helen Bell, he was born in Wallasey, England, in 1871 and educated at Rossall School, King’s College London and King’s College Hospital. In 1918 he became senior surgeon and in 1921 was appointed to the Chair of Obstetrics and Gynecology at Liverpool University, a position he held until 1931.

• Blair-Bell claimed that all of the endocrine glands, and not exclusively the sex glands, enter into the determination of sexual traits.

Author’s Comment: Internal secretion of the prostate stimulates metamorphosis through its valuable influence on the thyroid and pituitary glands. Conversely, loss of fluids through ejaculation diminishes the tissue of the spinal marrow.

## Guthrie

Kenneth Sylvan Guthrie (18711940) was an Episcopalian priest, medical doctor and professor. He studied at the University of the South, Tulane University, Harvard, Columbia University, and elsewhere. Guthrie published widely and translated The Pythagorean Sourcebook and Library, as well as the writings of Plotinus, Proclus and Numenius.

• Guthrie: “Those persons who are seeking the highest state of vitality possible to them... will insist on preserving every drop of their precious semen, permitting it naturally to be resorbed, assuring them first of all of a perfect body, next of increased mental faculties, and finally, if they progress, and the higher nervous centers be nourished and developed, of the fullest spiritual development.”

• Guthrie: “It would not be too much to say that if a man were absolutely continent, he would be free from all disease and more or less so in proportion as he was not quite continent.”

Author’s Comment: The maximum life expectancy for humans in this age (Kali) is 100 years. Typically, however, people live less than that because of pollution and degenerate personal habits. Although one’s individual karma or accumulated reactions from previous lives’ activities affect one’s longevity, so does his behavior in this life. The thrust of this book focuses on the cerebral benefits of chastity, thus we can learn from Guthrie’s statements that one who is free from sexual misuse should mostly be spared from mental disorders. We have noticed in nearly thirty visits to India that the pious, elderly persons living there retain brilliant intellect and clarity of mind throughout their old age largely because they did not indulge in promiscuous activities during their youth.

## Hunt

James Ramsay Hunt (1874-1937) pre-eminent 20th-century American neurologist. He graduated from the University of Pennsylvania in 1893 with an MD, then he studied in Paris, Vienna and Berlin and returned to practice neurology in New York at Cornell University Medical School from 1900 to 1910. He did major research on the anatomy and disorders of the corpus striatum and the extra-pyramidal system. He was consulting physician at several New York hospitals and was appointed professor of neurology at Columbia University College of Physicians and Surgeons, New York in 1924.

Several neurological disorders bear his name: Ramsay Hunt’s paralysis, Ramsay Hunt’s syndrome I, Ramsay Hunt’s syndrome II, Ramsay Hunt’s syndrome III, Ramsay Hunt’s zone and Ramsay Hunt’s atrophy.

• Hunt: “The prostate is definitely responsible, with the testicle, both for sex function and sex desire.”

• Hunt: “Hypertrophy \[unnecessary growth\] of the prostate occurs too often with the onset of impotence or diminished function to be attributed to mere coincidence.”

Author’s Comment: According to Ramsay Hunt the prostate functions in connection with the testis in the production of sex hormones. The loss of prostatic fluid through sexual excesses depletes the nutrients required for proper mental functioning, thus the high probability of derangement.

## Marshall

Francis Hugh Adam Marshall (1878-1949) eminent English physiologist received the Royal Medal from Royal Society Academy of Science (1940). He was a research physiologist at Edinburgh University.

• Marshall pointed out the need for restraint over the reproductive function and the sublimation of sex energy into higher cerebral forms of expression.

## McCarrison

Sir Robert McCarrison, CIE, MD, DSc, FRCP (1878-1960) British Physician to the King (1928-1935), considered by many, to be the “father of modern nutrition.” He was born in Portadown, County Armagh, Ireland, and qualified in medicine at Queen’s College, Belfast, in 1900. As a British army doctor he had, during the early part of the 20th century, made a name for himself through his brilliant research into deficiency diseases, especially of goiter and cretinism.

He was stationed in northern India where he was impressed by the physical health, beauty, longevity and emotional health of a certain race of people (Hunza).

In 1935 he moved to Oxford and became the Director of the Laboratory of Nutrition Research Laboratories, Oxford University. During this period, with encouragement from the viceroy, Lord Linlithgow, he built up one of the finest institutes of its kind in the world. Awarded the Kaisar-i-Hind gold medal for public service in India 1911 and in 1914 the Prix Amussat of the Academy of Medicine of Paris for original researches on goiter and cretinism. McCarrison was knighted in 1933.

• McCarrison found that atrophy of the testicles is frequently found in cerebral and spinal diseases which indicate that the internal secretion of the sex glands may have a nutritive function in relation to nervous tissue and brain cells and that mental diseases may result from its absence.

Author’s Comment: As a lack of Vitamin B causes the thymus to degenerate, so does a lack of lecithin. The thymus contains phosphorus-rich nucleinates which are crucial for development of body and mind and without them, a diminished mental state occurs.

A portion of the thymus contains nucleinates rich in phosphorus. The richness of the thymus in phosphorus shows that it is important not only for the proper development of the bones, but also the brain. Premature wasting of the thymus leads to the appearance of idiocy.

## Thorek

Max Thorek (1880-1960) surgeon and eminent professor of internal medicine, was born in the Tatra Mountains of Hungary. His exposure to medicine began early in life, for his parents, Isaac Thorek and Sarah Mahler, were both physicians. Thorek received his preliminary education in Budapest and immigrated to the United States in 1898, and settled in Chicago. Thorek received his MD from Rush Medical College (University of Chicago) in 1904. Thorek was primarily known for his innovative operation for removing the gallbladder.

Through his writings, Thorek was a prolific contributor to American, German, French and Italian medical journals. He also wrote numerous books, including Surgery of the Brain and Spinal Cord in 1912, The Human Testis and Its Diseases in 1924, Surgical Errors and Safeguards in 1933, and Thorek’s Illustrated Surgical Technic in 1935.

• Thorek found developmental defects in the reproductive system of idiots and cretins.

• Thorek: “The gonad elaborates through its internal secretions the chemical products which are taken up by the circulation and carried to the central nervous system, and there erotization results. That these substances of internal secretion have a selective action seems probable, and that such substances are stored in the central nervous system, seems, in view of recent experiments, quite certain.”

Author’s Comment: Man’s lust is not merely a physical phenomenon but stems from the mind. Thus when one contemplates sexual subjects the subtle act manifests in triggering physical changes and secretions.

• Thorek: “O’Malley thinks that the direct action of the chemical products of the gonads through the nervous system influences the growth and increased metabolism of every tissue of the body. That there is a direct relationship between the gonads and the hypophysis is fairly well established.”

Author’s Comment: Hypophysis refers to the master gland of the endocrine system, located at the base of the brain, also called the pituitary gland.

• Thorek: “Since the time of Hippocrates and Aristotle, it has been believed that there was a coordination between the testicular fluid and the nervous system, brain and cord.”

Author’s Comment: In the past 60 years medicine has rejected the wisdom of the past in favor of free sex, homosex, self-sex, etc. The results are apparent in the form of dementia and Alzheimer’s disease, etc.

## Ruggles

Arthur Hiler Ruggles (born 1881) superintendent of Butler Hospital in Rhode Island and president of the American Psychiatric Association. He was the author of Mental Health: Past, Present, and Future.

• Ruggles: “Sexual abstinence is compatible with perfect health and tends to increase vitality through resorption of the semen.”

Author’s Comment: American Medical Association (AMA) resolution: “Continence is not incompatible with health.”

## Roubaud

Emile Roubaud (1882-1962) eminent French biological scientist in the field of tropical medicine pathology. In 1907, he was the recipient of the Chalmers Medal Award from the Royal Society of Tropical Medicine and Hygiene awarded by Nobel laureate of medicine Louis Alphonse Laveran. Roubaud served as the President of the Society for Tropical Medicine from 1936 to 1946.

• Roubaud: \[Describing the immediate effects of the sexual orgasm\] “The circulation quickens, the arteries beat strongly, the venous blood, arrested by muscular contraction, increases the general heat, and this stagnation, more pronounced in the brain by the contraction of the muscles of the neck and the throwing of the head backward, causes a momentary cerebral congestion, during which intelligence is lost and the faculties abolished.”

• Roubaud: “The eyes, violently injected, become haggard, and the look uncertain. Or in the majority of cases the eyes are closed spasmodically to avoid the contact of the light. The respiration is hurried, sometimes interrupted, and may be suspended by the spasmodic contraction of the larynx, and the air, for a time compressed, is at last emitted in broken and meaningless words.”

• Roubaud: “The congested nervous centers only communicate confused sensations and volitions; mobility and sensation show extreme disorder; the limbs are seized by convulsions and sometimes by cramps, or are thrown wildly about or become stiff like iron bars.”

• Roubaud: “The jaws, tightly pressed, grind the teeth, and in some persons the delirium is carried so far that they bite to bleeding the shoulders their companions have imprudently abandoned to them. This frantic state of epilepsy lasts but a short time, but it suffices to exhaust the forces of the organism.”

Author’s Comment: Although a person experiences a type of ecstasy via sex indulgence, the medical analysis is here poignantly expressed. Simply because something feels good, it should not be concluded that it actually is good for the occupant of the body. Driving at exorbitant speeds also affords one a type of immediate exhilaration, but a wise person resists the temptation considering the dangerous risk.

## Brinkley

Dr. John Richard Brinkley (1885-1942) American specialist in glands, he worked mainly in Kansas and Texas.

• Brinkley placed the sex glands in the position of master glands in the endocrine chain, for they alone, through their external secretion, are able to withdraw considerable amounts of lecithin and phosphatides from the circulation.

Author’s Comment: With the prominent losses of lecithin and phosphorus through sex indulgence, the brain’s functions become less productive, much like any other gland or organ in the human body. From beriberi to polyneuritis, to psychoses, to Alzheimer’s disease is only a transition of graver nerve-and-brain-cell starvation.

One of my students made an interesting observation regarding the attempt to restore lost lecithin by eatable supplements. Foods high in protein such as eggs are known for having lecithin, yet higher protein intake often leads to greater sexual activity. It’s almost as though nature designed the body in such a way that if one tries to replace the wasted vital fluids through artificial means rather than natural sexual abstinence, he can’t do it properly and ends up wasting more vital fluids.

Dr. Bernard recapitulated Brinkley’s findings thus: “Chronic phosphatide deficiency causes other glands to become hyperactive but produces underactivity and atrophy of the endocrines. Thus the basic cause of endocrine dysfunctions—hypoactivity or hyperactivity—is to be found in the sex glands and their ability to alter the lecithin or phosphatide content of the blood, which is the primary raw material from which the endocrines manufacture their hormones. Thus sexual excess leads to an earlier appearance of senility.”

## Schlapp

Max Gustav Schlapp (1869-1928) professor of Neuropathology at the New York PostGraduate Medical School. He was the author of several books among them Causes of Defective Children (J. Hered., XIV: 87-97) and New Criminology: A Consideration of the Chemical Causation of Abnormal Behavior (New York: Boni & Liveright, 1928.)

• Schlapp studied hundreds of cases of cretins born of normal parents and his conclusion was that a prenatal injury to the thyroid and other glands of the embryo by an endocrine disturbance in the mother was the basic cause of such conditions.

Author’s Comment: One is born a cretin when deprived of the iodine-rich hormone thyroxin. In humans and animals, a thyroxin deficiency halts development of the brain’s neurons. The mental development of cretins is retarded throughout life.

• Schlapp believed that glandular depletion of the mother during gestation is the basic cause of the production of cretins and idiots, when there is no direct hereditary causation. It is clear that such “glandular depletion” can result from the excessive withdrawal of phosphatides from the mother’s blood as the result of sexual intercourse during pregnancy, which also tends to produce endocrine dysfunction in the form of glandular hyper-and-hypoactivities.

Author’s Comment: The thymus glands require high levels of phosphatides for proper functioning, but engaging in sexual acts deprives the glands of nourishment. This degeneration occurs frequently when one enters puberty.

• Schlapp: “The development of the embryonic brain is largely determined by chemical conditions affecting the brain of the embryo during its development. Thus, a lack of iodine in the diet of a pregnant mother may cause underdevelopment of the thyroid gland and brain of the embryo, resulting in an idiot. On the other hand, an abundance of iodine will lead to the birth of a child with a superior brain.”

Author’s Comment: Phosphorus, when it is in the form of lecithin, is a main component of the endocrine glands. Modern studies show that sexual activity causes a disturbance in these glands, as well, causing permanent damage to the body and mind. The brain is dependent on oxygen, phosphorus and the thyroid hormone for its normal productivity and that productivity will decrease if the level of even one of these elements is lowered, or if the element itself is absent. The thyroid here is especially important, as it depends on iodine for its secretion and for the normal productivity of the brain.

## Finkler

Rita Sapiro Finkler (1888-1968) was the first woman on the senior medical staff at Beth Israel Hospital, where she founded the department of endocrinology in 1939 and directed it until 1951. Finkler emigrated from Ukraine as a young woman, graduated from the Women’s Medical College of Philadelphia in 1915, and lived most of her professional life in Newark.

• Finkler said that sexual continence is not injurious to young men, but, on the contrary, is beneficial to body and mind.

Author’s Comment: It is common for Hare Krishna devotees to be asked why they eschew sex, as it is practiced in regular society. Girls and women seem worried that if too many men take up the life of celibacy, then there could be a shortage of husbands or partners. The female population should not become anxious over this concern because it is quite rare that a man renounces sex life altogether. Regulated sex within sacred marriage is recommended for the general populous.

## Shelton

Dr. Herbert Macgolfin Shelton (1895-1985) American natural hygienist. He was born in Texas, attended Bernarr Macfadden’s College of Physcultopathy in Chicago, Lindlahr College of Natural Therapeutics (for post-graduate work) and interned at Crane’s Sanitarium in Elmhurst, Illinois, Dr. Lindlahr’s and Sahler’s Sanitariums. He lectured widely and wrote many books on preventative principles of living to avoid disease.

• Shelton claimed that sexual excess can further the development of any disease to which the individual is subject.

Author’s Comment: Herbert Shelton had high regard for Dr. Keith. From his book Orthography, Shelton wrote: “Dr. Melville C. Keith... was much ahead of his time in his dietary views.”

• Shelton considered that a person predisposed to epilepsy is almost certain to have an attack after sexual acts. Spinal and heart disorders are apt to occur. There is an increase of blood-pressure, which predisposes to apoplexy.

Author’s Comment: Dr. Melville C. Keith wrote: “There is no place where you may go but you will see the fruits of this sexual indulgence to excess. The penalty may be denied, and all these diseases may be attributed to every other cause under the sun, but you will be right in laying it at the proper place, and that is to the sexual drain which has sapped the blood corpuscles of the victim before you.”

• Shelton: “No function is so exhausting to the whole system as this \[orgasm\]. If excessively indulged in, no practice can possibly be so enervating.”

Author’s Comment: Dr. Melville C. Keith: “The penalty for the disobedience of this law is in the shortened lives and the increased amount of disease which are everywhere around us.” (from The Marriage Law by Melville Keith)

• Shelton: “Anything is excess when procreation is not the end. Man is sexually perverted. He is the only animal that has his ‘social problem,’ the only animal that supports prostitution, the only animal that practices self-abuse, the only animal that is demoralized by all forms of sexual perversions, the only animal whose male will attack the females, the only animal where the desire of the female is not the law, the only one that does not exercise his sexual powers in harmony with their primitive constitution.”

Author’s Comment: The conjunction of two biological sewage pipes has been artificially elevated to the special status of romantic nobility. Very few persons are sober enough to try to get free from sex indulgence.

## Rabinowitch

Dr. I.M. Rabinowitch (or spelled Robinowitch) prominent neurologist and psychiatrist at Montreal General Hospital and formerly president of the New York Neurological Society. He is well known for his work with diabetes and was the author of Some Clinical Aspects of Endocrine Therapy and Effects of the High Carbohydrate-Low Calorie Diet Upon Carbohydrate Tolerance in Diabetes Mellitus.

• Rabinowitch: “Sexual continence is not only harmless but beneficial.”

Author’s Comment: What is striking about the 1960s ‘sexual revolution,’ as it has properly been called, is how revolutionary it was. In 1965, 69 percent of American women and 65 percent of men under the age of 30 said that premarital sex was always or almost always wrong; by 1972, these figures had plummeted to 24 percent and 21 percent... In 1990, only six percent of British men and women under the age of 34 believed that it \[premarital sex\] was always or almost always wrong.

## Lowenfeld

“One of the first public commentaries regarding Freud’s attempt to recover from the Seduction Theories was a quote included in Leopold Lowenfeld’s book, Psychic Obsessions.”

—CliffNotes: Freud’s Seduction Theory

Leopold Lowenfeld or Loewenfeld (1857-1924) German gynecologist, psychiatrist and author. He wrote several books on neuroses, psychoanalysis, obsessions, etc., mostly published in Wiesbaden (1904-1906).

• Loewenfeld considers it possible for a sexually normal individual to live in permanent continence without any ill-effects whatsoever.

Author’s Comment: Dr. Lowenfeld’s opinion is applauded by the American Medicine (magazine) which wrote: “It should be an easy matter to convince any developed man that continence can be a normal state of civilized man.”

Author’s Comment: A tremendous waste of energy takes place as millions of seeds are produced and strewn on barren land, etc.

## Lorand

Dr. Arnold Lorand MD—Austrian author of several books, most famous of which is Old Age Deferred (published in 1910). Dr. Lorand revealed that old age is a chronic disease due to degeneration of the glands with internal secretions of the thyroid, sexual glands and adrenals in particular.

• Lorand: “The ancient Hindus recommended to men sexual abstinence of long duration, thinking that by this means the internal secretion of the sexual glands would be absorbed into the system and that they would thereby reap all the benefits inherent in such a secretion. By this it seems that thousands of years before Claude Bernard and Brown-Sequard, the Hindus already appreciated the great importance of the internal secretions...”

Author’s Comment: The Western analysis of semen/blood loss is 40 to one, and the Ayurvedic is 80 to one. In either case, if one loses 10 drops of semen, it is equivalent of losing the value of 400 to 800 drops of blood. That is a lot of blood! This is easy to understand. We need blood in our body and the blood has to be of good quality. The more the expenditure of semen, the more the body’s physical, mental and spiritual proficiency becomes depleted.

• Lorand: “Impelled by their desires, many persons are quite ready to carry on their sexual functions, but unwilling to

fulfil their duty of procreation. They are desirous, indeed, of experiencing the attendant enjoyment, but want to cheat nature out of the payment exacted therefor, viz., the fulfillment of the duty of procreation... The frequent local hyperemia resulting from the sexual excitement may very readily lead to inflammatory states of the ovaries and uterus and its covering layers, this, in turn, having a tendency to shorten life and even frequently causing death.”

Author’s Comment: Increased sexual activity during puberty disturbs the metabolism and causes thymus deterioration through the loss of phosphatides. For women, wasting away of the ovaries is often found in women with dementia. It is well documented that the removal of ovaries often causes neurotic malfunctions.

## 6. Freud—Fraud

Sigmund Freud is perhaps the most radical psychologist of the 20th century. His ideas have affected almost every facet of society. His studies began in the 1890s and his ideas were gathered from years of patient/doctor sessions and various contributions from outside sources.

## Freud

• Freud: “As a depth psychology, a theory of the mental unconscious, it can become indispensable to all the sciences which are concerned with the evolution of human civilisation and its major institutions such as art, religion an social order.”

Author’s Comment: Freud is speaking here of his psychoanalysis procedure. A guru tells of a Bengali proverb, “How to cry and how to enjoy sex, it doesn’t require any education.” When one is aggrieved, he cries automatically and when there is a sex impulse, he enjoys it automatically. It doesn’t require Mr. Freud. Without the help of any educator, everyone knows—cats, dogs, animals, human beings—ev-eryone knows how to enjoy sex life. It doesn’t require any education.

• Freud: “A single idea of general value dawned on me. I have found, in my own case too, the phenomena of being in love with my mother and jealous of my father, and I now consider it a universal event in early childhood... Later between two and two and a half years—my libido toward matrem was awakened, namely, on the occasion of a journey with her from Leipzig to Vienna, during which we must have spent the night together and there must have been an opportunity of seeing her nudam...”

Author’s Comment: According to the fallacious logic of atmavan manyate jagat, everyone thinks of others according to his own position. Because Freud felt a sexual longing for his mother, he surmised that everyone did. This is preposterous! Freud believed that even after a child grows and has become educated about sexual activity, that deep inside they really want to return to their mothers for pleasure. Why should the whole world have to accept this theory based on a frank, yet perverted confession?

• Freud: “But even after sexual activity has become detached from the taking of nourishment, an important part of this first and most significant of all sexual relations is left over, which helps to prepare for the choice of an object and thus to restore the happiness that has been lost.”

Author’s Comment: Freud develops his theory by proposing that the more affection a mother shows her child, the more the child ultimately will wish to return to her for sex. The most radical idea about this is that a child might wish to have sex with his mother. He would then see father as a threat because he is with mommy and wishes to kill him. Also, the idea of returning to one’s mother could explain why as humans grow they are never fully satisfied or happy due to repressed sexual energy that society has created. The idea of being with your mother is seen by society as preposterous and morally unacceptable. However, to Freud, it is seen as natural sexual energy being released.

• Freud: “And now, the main thing! As far as I can see, my next work will be called ‘Human Bisexuality.’ It will go to the root of the problem and say the last word it may be granted to say—the last and the most profound.”

Author’s Comment: Another inane brainchild of Sigmund Freud is that everyone ultimately is bisexual. When we observe the effect that Freud’s philosophy has had on the world it is no wonder why there is such chaos. Freud convinced people that it is natural and good for a man or woman to pursue sexual relations with members of both genders. Evidently, the fleeting pleasure derived from sex is so desirable by men and women that they are unresistingly content to remain bewildered by Freud’s beliefs.

• Freud: “The work keeps on coming to a stop and they \[patients\] keep on maintaining that this time nothing has occurred to them. We \[psychoanalysts\] must not believe what they say, we must always assume, and tell them, too, that they have kept something back... We must insist on this, we must repeat the pressure and represent ourselves as infallible, till at last we are really told something... There are cases too in which the patient tries to disown \[the memory\] even after its return. ‘Something has occurred to me now, but you obviously put it into my head.’... In all such cases, I remain unshakably firm. I... explain to the patient that \[these distinctions\] are only forms of his resistance and pretexts raised by it against reproducing this particular memory, which we must recognize in spite of all this.”

Author’s Comment: Dr. Bernard unleashes a brilliant accusation against Freud and his cult, the sex monger society. “Freudianism is in a large sense a rationalization of modern sexual behavior, which seeks to provide scientific justification for sexual actions that are really unnatural and are products of aphrodisiacal food stimulation. Chief among the errors of this new pseudo-scientific phallic cult is the superstition that Freud picked up from the gutter and dressed in scientific garb that sexual abstinence is harmful and a cause of nervous and mental disorders as the result of ‘sexual repression’ that it involves and that orgasm is a normal expression of the sex impulse which is necessary for health. This belief is without scientific foundation... Freud makes this myth, in the form of his doctrine of repression, the cornerstone of his pseudo-scientific edifice.”

As a matter of fact, the present post-Freudian neurotic age suffers not from sexual abstinence and repression but from the reverse, from sexual over expression and overindulgence.

• Freud: “However, a certain part of the suppressed libidinous excitation deserves satisfaction. We should remember that individual happiness ought to be one of the aims of our culture. If the narrowing of sexuality is pushed too far it will have all the evil effects of a robbery.”

Author’s Comment: Freud has given the ultimate bad name to “repression” indicating that it is always bad—not only bad but the supreme evil. Therefore he says that at all costs we must defeat repression. Repression is his code word for any higher authority such as God, religion, morality, society, conscience, etc.

However, repression is sometimes necessary. All the desires which pop into our head are not desirable, thus some of them must be repressed. What if I am looking out atop a skyscraper and a peculiar thought inside my head says, “Jump?” Should I heed that desire just because it is present? No, I must reject that irrational thought immediately. Rational thinking means to weed out the irrational, unproductive desires from the beneficial.

• Freud: “The excessive excitations from individual sexual sources are discharged and utilized in other spheres, so that no small enhancement of mental capacity results from a predisposition which is dangerous as such.”

Author’s Comment:

Student: Freud felt that sexual repression would be harmful, but sexual sublimation can often be beneficial. In other words, he didn’t believe that, in total sexual freedom as it’s conceived today, but that a man would be better, instead of trying to totally deny the sex drive, to try to redirect it, oh, perhaps in artistic activity or in, in study, or in some other activity. Not to deny it. Srila Prabhupada: That means, in one word, to divert his attention.

Student: Yes.

Srila Prabhupada: That is brahmacari. \[a celibate student\].

That is recommended in the Vedic culture, that from the very beginning of his life, divert his attention for spiritual activities, he will forget about sex life. That is the experience. Not only a trained-up child, even a grown-up person, if he takes Krsna consciousness seriously, he also forgets sex life. So that is possible by training, one can forget sex life.

The work of psychiatrist Sigmund Freud did not decry sexual excess, but instead linked mental illness to repression of sexual expression. In stating this, Freud offered a theory completely void of scientific verification utilizing only superstitious nonsense. Freud’s theories serve no purpose other than to sell his works or to furnish what the public wished to hear, instead of the truth, which might not be so widely accepted.

## Jung

Carl Gustav Jung (1875-1961) Swiss psychiatrist, founder of analytical psychology and was instrumental in bringing psychology into the 20th century by developing one of several theories of the unconscious. He was born in Kesswill, Switzerland, in 1875, studied medicine at the School of Basil and studied psychology in Paris. He became a physician in Zurich and a lecturer in psychiatry, and he traveled around the world. In 1912 he founded his own school of psychology in Zurich.

• Jung: “Freud never asked himself why he was compelled to talk continually of sex, why this idea had taken such possession of him. He remained unaware that his monotony of interpretation expressed a flight from himself, or from that other side of him which might perhaps be called mystical. So long as he refused to acknowledge that side, that is the mystical side, he could never be reconciled with himself.”

Author’s Comment:

Student: Jung sees the mind as being composed of a balance of the conscious and the unconscious, or subconscious. It is the function of the personality to integrate these. For instance, if one has a strong sex drive, he can sublimate or channel it into art or religious activity.

Srila Prabhupada: That is our process. The sex impulse is natural for everyone in the material world. But if we think of Krsna embracing Radharani or dancing with the gopis, our sex impulse is sublimated and weakened. If one hears about the pastimes of Krsna and the gopis from the right source, lusty desire within the heart will be suppressed, and one will be able to develop devotional service.

Student: This would be an example of what Jung would call integration or individuation, whereby the energies of the subconscious sex impulse are channeled into conscious, creative activity directed toward God realization.

Srila Prabhupada: What we must understand is that Krsna is the only purusa, the only enjoyer. If we help Him in His enjoyment, we also receive enjoyment. We are predominated, and He is the predominator. On the material platform, if a husband wants to enjoy the wife, the wife must voluntarily help him in that enjoyment. By helping him, the wife also becomes an enjoyer. Similarly, the supreme predominator, the supreme enjoyer, is Krsna. And the predominated, the enjoyed, are the living entities. When the living entities agree to help Krsna’s sex desire, you might say “sense enjoyment,” they become enjoyers.

• Jung: “Sexuality evidently meant more to Freud than to other people. For him it was something to be religiously observed... One thing was clear: Freud, who had always made much of his irreligiosity, had now constructed a dogma. Or rather, in the place of a jealous God whom he had lost, he had substituted another compelling image, that of sexuality.”

Author’s Comment:

Srila Prabhupada: “Yes. He \[Freud\] was under the leadership of sexuality. That’s a fact. Everyone is under the leadership. Just like sometimes we say, ‘The material scientists say like this, they say like this.’ He accepts the leadership. So we have to accept the leadership, but if we accept the leadership of Krsna, then our life is perfect. Other leadership is maya, maya’s leadership. But we have to accept leadership. There is no doubt of it. So he accepted the leadership of sex, but he did not admit it, but going on speaking on sex.”

## McCalden

David McCalden (1951-1991) writer; considered by some to be a unique personality and one of the truly great free-thinkers of our time. He was born into a working-class family in Belfast, Northern Ireland. He was educated at the University of London, Goldsmiths’ College and graduated with a Certificate in Education (Sociology) in 1974.

In 1978 he moved to California and established the Institute for Historical Review. He died from complications due to pneumonia.

• McCalden: “He \[Freud\] was for many years an enthusiastic user \[of cocaine\] to the point where his nose bled and became filled with pus—which he treated with more cocaine.”

• McCalden: “Freud’s friend Ernst von Fleischl-Marxov (18461891) had become a despairing addict after Freud had prescribed cocaine as medicine for a painful hand tumor. There is no doubt that the addiction brought about his early death.”

Author’s Comment: Freud was such a large supporter of cocaine that he published an article “On Coca” in 1884, at the beginning of his own cocaine use, which would last roughly twenty years.

• McCalden: “Freud continued to be plagued by bad health: migraine, nightmares, heart trouble and eventually— mouth cancer. He began to interpret his dreams, and when he experienced one where two bird-men laid his mother on the bed, he figured that this represented his boyhood desire to kill his father and have sex with his mother. Another analyst may have interpreted it as having too much cheese and pickles before going to bed, but Freud insisted that such dream symbolisms were ‘typical’, and labeled the phenomenon the ‘Oedipus Complex’ after the famous Greek fable.”

Author’s Comment:

Srila Prabhupada: That is experience of Yamunacarya. He \[is\] expressing, yadavadhi mama cetah krsna-padaravinde. He says that “Since my mind and attention has been diverted to Krsna consciousness activities, as soon as I think of sex life, I spite \[spit\] on it.” That is possible. It is simply question of training. And if one indulges in sex life without any restriction, the physical problem is there. He will be impotent. He will not be able to, even though he has got sex organs, he will not be able to use it. That is nature’s way of punishing. There are so many impotent person\[s\]. So it is a question of training. So the Vedic training is to train the small child, from the very beginning of his life, how to avoid sex life. That cannot be artificially done, but there is a process of training. By accepting that training, one can remain without sex life throughout the whole life. That is possible.

Student: Well he \[Freud\] felt it couldn’t be stamped out. If it, if you try to stamp out the sex drive, it will manifest itself in neuroses, in undesirable...

Srila Prabhupada: That is the defect. He does not know perfectly anything, and he is philosophizing. That is the defect. Everything is possible, but our Krsna consciousness movement is different from his imagination. Our philosophy is that so long one has the sex inclination, he will have to accept a material body.”

• McCalden: “Freud’s neurotic dysfunctions manifested themselves in unusual behavior patterns and in psychosomatic ailments—particularly those affecting the mouth, the genitals and the anus. He suffered phobias about riding in trains, about death and about visiting Rome. More often than not, he was chronically depressed, and bad-tempered.”

Author’s Comment: Sexual freedom is synonymous with atheism and science is also largely based on atheism propounding that there is no God in control. Bhagavad-gita, chapter 16, verse 8 explains:

“They \[the demoniac\] say that this world is unreal, with no foundation, no God in control. They say it is produced of sex desire and has no cause other than lust.”

This verse fits Freud like a glove. Freud thinks there is no God in control and that sex is the only cause (kim anyat kama-haitukam). Freud and Darwin are godless brothers in crime having participated in the illegitimate births of anthropology and psychoanalysis. Freud’s undeniable contribution to excessive social sex activity must be linked as well with problems such as Alzheimer’s disease, AIDS, venereal diseases, abortions, broken homes, increases in homosexuality, sexual perversions, mental disturbances, etc.

## Gross

Martin L. Gross—has written more than a dozen books; three of his prior nonfiction works, The Brain Watchers, The Doctors and The Psychological Society stimulated public debate in the fields of psychological testing, medicine and psychiatry, resulting in Congressional hearings and reforms. Mr. Gross has been a member of the faculty of The New School for Social Research and an Adjunct Associate Professor of Social Science at New York University. He lives and works in suburban Connecticut.

• Gross: “No one has yet evaluated the hallucinatory effects of cocaine on Freud’s mind during the formative years of psychoanalysis. Without cocaine, could Freud have created such improbable flights of human fancy?”

Author’s Comment: Freud shared his love for cocaine (his own “magic carpet”) with both family and friends, including his fi-ancêe, Martha Bernays. Cocaine would, Freud promised, lead to increased levels of energy and strength. Assisted significantly through his encouragement, cocaine addiction ran rampant in Austria by 1886.

## Boeree

Dr. C. George Boeree (1952- ) professor in the Psychology Department at Shippensburg University, Pennsylvania, where he specializes in the philosophical side of psychology. He was born near Amsterdam in the Netherlands and came to the US in 1956 with his parents and brother, and grew up mostly on Long Island. He received his Bachelors of Arts degree from Penn State, and his Masters of Arts and PhD from Oklahoma State, all in psychology. He presently lives with his family in the Cumberland Valley of Pennsylvania.

• Boeree: “It is ironic that much of the cultural change in sexual attitudes was in fact due to Freud’s work!”

Author’s Comment: It takes a hundred years to grow a forest, but it can be burnt to the ground within a day. Freud is not without blame for setting ablaze the long-standing forest of propriety by his painstaking and deliberate opposition to religion and moral restraint.

## Sophie Freud

Sophie Freud (1924- ) granddaughter of Sigmund Freud, was born in Vienna, Austria, and emigrated via France and Casablanca to the United States in 1942, at the age of 18. She attended Radcliffe/ Harvard, and then Simmons College School of Social Work. She practiced as a social worker mostly in the field of child welfare and child (parent) guidance, and received her PhD from the Florence Heller School for Social Welfare in 1970.

She then spent the next 30 years as a social work educator, chairing the Human Behavior Sequence at the Simmons College School of Social Work and taking an active part in their doctoral program. She continues to give talks through the United States, Canada and Europe.

• Sophie Freud: “In my eyes, both Adolf Hitler and my grandfather were false prophets of the 20th century.”

Author’s Comment: In one aspect Freud was only the scapegoat that hedonistic society sacrificed on the altar of its uncontrolled senses. Still, Sophie Freud candidly assesses her grandfather’s complicity by his over-emphasis of unrestricted sex as the ultimate goal for human society to achieve. This activity also is enjoyed by dogs, hogs, monkeys and pigeons with fewer restrictions than those imposed on humans. The bottom line of his philosophizing, then, is to become like guilt-free hogs.

• Sophie Freud: \[on Freudian theory\] “I think it’s such a narcissistic indulgence that I cannot believe in it.”

Author’s Comment: Sigmund Freud and other Western philosophers have written many books studying the psychology of sex life. However, it is unnecessary because there is already a natural psychology for that. Nobody is taught it in the schools and colleges yet everyone knows how to do it. Education should be given to reduce it.

• Sophie Freud: “Some of my grandfather’s ideas have become obsolete.”

Author’s Comment: According to the Center of Complex Systems, Randall Laboratory, The University of Michigan, Ann Arbor, Michigan, Sigmund Freud’s genius was not for science or healing, but rhetoric and, in the oldest sense, mythology and telling-of-tales.

## Grubin

David Grubin (1945- ) is a producer, director, writer and cinematographer who has produced over 100 films on subjects ranging from history to art, from poetry to science and won nine Emmy Awards. He grew up in Hillside, N.J., near the Weequahic section of Newark. He was educated at Hamilton College, where he majored in English, and Harvard, where he received a master’s degree in education and an honorary doctorate from Hamilton College. He is married with three children and lives in New York City.

• Freud: “I have found in my own case the phenomena of being in love with my mother and jealous of my father, and I now consider it a universal event in early childhood... If this is so we can understand the gripping power of \[the Greek legend\] Oedipus Rex.” (From Young Dr. Freud by David Grubin)

Author’s Comment:

1\) When Freud was young, he admittedly had strong sexual attraction to his mother,

2\) He was prone to depression,

3\) He was addicted to using cocaine (nearly 20 years),

4\) He was addicted to smoking cigars (20 per day till death by mouth cancer),

5\) He admitted to having “unruly homosexual feeling\[s\]” for a male friend,

6\) He adopted a theory of a universal bisexual impulse.

Based on the following quotation from Mr. Freud, Dr. Pravin The-vathasan of the WGBH Educational Foundation corroborates the imagined universality of Mr. Freud’s Oedipus Complex theory:

• Freud: “My libido toward my mother was awakened, on the occasion of a journey during which we must have spent the night together and there must have been the opportunity of seeing her nude. In my own case, I discovered that I was in love with my mother and now consider it a universal event in early childhood.”

## Cioffi

Frank L. Cioffi of Princeton University is the author of books on Freud including Freud and the Question of Pseudoscience. For three decades Frank Cioffi has been at the center of the debate over Freud’s legacy and the legitimacy of psychoanalysis.

• Frank Cioffi likened the claims of Freud and his disciples to sightings of the Loch Ness monster, a phenomenon that can never be proved.

Author’s Comment: Freud claimed there were major stages of a child’s growth that ultimately affect the rest of one’s life—most notably the oral stage and the anal stage. Freud argued that a child’s first pleasure was when he drank milk from his mother’s breast. He went on to say that children received pleasure from this and that the event was in fact a prototype of an orgasm.

• Cioffi: “Examination of Freud’s interpretations will show that he typically proceeds by beginning with whatever content his theoretical preconceptions compel him to maintain underlies the symptoms and then, by working back and forth between it and the explanandum, constructing persuasive but spurious links between them.”

Author’s Comment: Freud probably would have been better off if he had described the pleasure-seeking tendency as the desire for sense gratification rather than trying to bend every act into a sexual context. Certainly sex is a pivotal and predominant desire of adults but the fact that children and some adults do not engage in sex defeats Freud’s theory that all persons are doomed to morbid sex orientation. In other words, all the senses crave satisfaction as explained in Srila Rupa Gosvami’s verse:

*vaco vegam manasah krodha-vegam*  
*jihva-vegam udaropastha-vegam*  
*etan vegan yo visaheta dhirah*  
*sarvam apimam prthivim sa sisyat*

**“A sober person who can tolerate the urge to speak, the mind’s demands, the actions of anger and the urges of the tongue, belly and genitals is qualified to make disciples all over the world.” \[Upadesamrta (1)\]**

## Shamdasani

Sonu Shamdasani PhD—a historian of psychology and a research associate at the Wellcome Trust Centre for the History of Medicine at UCL. He is the author of several books including CG Jung and the Making of Modern Psychology and The Dream of a Science.

• Sonu Shamdasani says that Freudian beliefs and psychoanalysis “were never a science. Freud was a fashion, and then he became unfashionable... Completely absurd.”

Author’s Comment: One thoughtful person from India remarked that Freud did not have perfect knowledge, nor was he trained by a perfect man. Therefore his theories are all nonsense.

Freud normalized the sexual experience, including masturbation, through his five-stage psychosexual development model based upon the memories of his adult patients. Although his theory has been critiqued as the research was based upon memory rather than actual observation of children progressing through the five developmental stages, Freud’s five-part theory normalized masturbation and sexuality by stating that every human experiences oral, anal, homosexual, narcissistic and genital fixations and that it is a normal procedure for proper sexual development.

## Einstein

Albert Einstein (1879-1955) recognized as one of the greatest physicists of all time. He was born at Ulm, Germany. His more important works include the Special Theory of Relativity (1905), Relativity (English translations, 1920 and 1950), General Theory of Relativity (1916), Investigations on Theory of Brownian Movement (1926), and The Evolution of Physics (1938). He received the Nobel Prize for physics in 1921.

• Einstein: “I regret that I cannot accede to your request, because I should like very much to remain in the darkness of not having been analyzed.”

Author’s Comment: Einstein and Freud corresponded often and met once during the New Year’s holiday season of 1927 at the home of one of Freud’s sons in Berlin. When a friend wrote Einstein just a few months later suggesting that he allow himself to undergo psychoanalysis, Einstein declined.

## LaHaye

Dr. Tim LaHaye, prophecy scholar, minister, and educator. He has written over forty nonfiction works that have been published in over thirty languages. He lives in Southern California.

• LaHaye: “A major contributor to the present-day tendency to accept and encourage homosexuality is Dr. Sigmund Freud... In other words, homosexuality was no longer to be considered an illegal form of debauchery or perversion in which one willingly engaged a person of his own sex, but a ‘mental illness’ which one blamed on his mother. Consequently, a homosexual is not responsible for his behavior—it’s his mother’s fault!”

Author’s Comment: Freud admitted his own homosexual or bisexual tendency. Add to this his peculiar penchant for axioms insisting that whatever impulses he, Sigmund Freud, felt should be extrapolated and painted on everybody with the same brush, and you create a situation where homosexuality is promoted as normal and acceptable behavior.

## Nabokov

Vladimir Nabokov (pen name Vladimir Sirin) (1899-1977) Russian-born American novelist, critic and acknowledged lepidopterist. He was born in St. Petersburg, Russia into a wealthy, aristocratic family and his full name was Vladimir Vladimirovich Nabokov. The Encyclopedia Britannica describes him as a “Russian-born American novelist and critic, the foremost of the post-1917 immigrant authors. He wrote in both Russian and English, and his best works, including Lolita (1955), feature stylish, intricate literary effects.”

• Nabokov: “I don’t want an elderly gentleman from Vienna with an umbrella inflicting his dreams upon me.”

• Nabokov: “Let the credulous and the vulgar continue to believe that all mental woes can be cured by a daily application of old Greek myths to their private parts.”

Author’s Comment: Edward R. Pinckney MD writes: “To me, psychoanalysis is a hoax—the biggest hoax ever played on humanity. By showing who analysts are, how they work, what they believe, and what they have done, I hope to show Freud as a fraud. If I succeed, I am idealistic enough to hope that the world may return to the belief in love, ideals, good taste and courtesy—the ‘books’ that have been burned by the Freudian Inquisition.”

## Friedan

Betty Friedan (Naomi Goldstein), (1921-2006) writer and social reformer. Considered by some to be the foremost spokesperson for women’s rights in the world. Born in Peoria, Illinois, she graduated from Smith College with a degree in psychology and, after a year of graduate work at the University of California at Berkeley, settled in New York City. In 1963 her bestselling book The Feminine Mystique (1963) was published. In October 1966 Friedan co-founded the National Organization for Women (NOW), a civil-rights group dedicated to achieving equality of opportunity for women.

• Friedan: “His own memories of the sexual jealousy he felt for his father, whose wishes she also gratified, were the basis of his theory of the Oedipus complex.” (The Feminine Mystique: Chapter 5, The Sexual Solipsism of Sigmund Freud; 1963)

Author’s Comment: Freud was unaware that everyone must again be put into another womb, and the same traumatic experiences will happen again.

• Friedan: “Freudian and pseudo-Freudian theories settled everywhere, like fine volcanic ash. Sociology, anthropology, education, even the study of history and literature became permeated and transfigured by Freudian thought.”

• Friedan: “After the depression, after the war, Freudian psychology became much more than a science of human behaviour, a therapy for the suffering. It became an allembracing American ideology, a new religion. It provided a convenient escape from the atom bomb, McCarthy, all the disconcerting problems that might spoil the taste of steaks, and cars and color television and backyard swimming pools... The new psychological religion—which made a virtue of sex, removed all sin from private vice, and cast suspicion on high aspirations of the mind and spirit.”

Author’s Comment: Freud has focused more attention on sex, but what is the actual benefit of this? As a result, society embraced the sexual revolution; its offspring became hippies and degraded the whole nation more.

## Jones

Alfred Ernest Jones, (1879-1958) psychoanalyst and a key figure in the advancement of his profession in Britain. He was born in Glamorgan, Wales, and became one of Sigmund Freud’s closest associates and staunchest supporters. After receiving his medical degree (1903), Jones became a member of the Royal College of Physicians, London (1904). Jones wrote an exhaustive three-volume biography of Freud.

• Jones: “Freud’s deviation from the average in this respect, as well as his pronounced mental bisexuality, may well have influenced his theoretical views to some extent.”

Author’s Comment: The “deviation from the average” referred to here is Freud’s homosexual tendency referred to by Freud’s respectful biographer, Ernest Jones.

## MacDonald

Professor Kevin MacDonald PhD—pres-ently teaching at the Department of Psychology CSU-Long Beach. He is the author of Psychoanalysis in Its Death Throes: The Moral and Intellectual Legacy of a Pseudoscience.

• MacDonald: “Allen Esterson’s (1992) Seductive Mirage: An Exploration of the Work of Sigmund Freud demonstrates convincingly that Freud’s patients did not volunteer any information on seduction or primal scenes at all. The seduction stories that provide the proffered empirical basis of the Oedipal complex were in fact a construction by Freud who then interpreted his patients’ distress on hearing his constructions as confirmation. Freud then deceptively obscured the fact that his patients’ stories were reconstructions and interpretations based on his a priori theory. He also retroactively changed the identity of the fancied seducers from non-family members (servants, etc.) when his Oedipal story required fathers instead.”

Author’s Comment: Materialistically endowed persons are doomed to four significant flaws: the tendency to commit mistakes (bhrama) \[Sanskrit\], subjectivity to being illusioned (pramada), imperfect senses (karanapaöava) and the inclination to cheat others (vipralipsa). The latter characteristic of Mr. Freud is highlighted by Professor MacDonald’s statement above.

• MacDonald: “Indeed, Kurzweil (1989, 89) notes that ‘In the beginning, the Freudians tried to ‘prove’ the universality of the Oedipus complex; later on, they took it for granted. Ultimately, they no longer spelled out the reasons for the pervasiveness of childhood sexuality and its consequences in the cultural monographs: they all accepted it.’ What started out as a speculation in need of empirical support ended up as a fundamental a priori assumption.”

Author’s Comment: This phenomenon also was apparent in the acceptance of Darwin’s Theory of Evolution. It has been and continues to be taught in schools and universities as the scientific explanation although lacking any scientific evidence.

• MacDonald: “Now 100 years after its inception, the theory of the Oedipal complex, childhood sexuality, and the sexual etiology of the neuroses remain without any independent empirical validation. Even from a Darwinian evolutionary perspective, the idea that children would have a specifically sexual attraction to their opposite sex parent is extremely implausible, since such an incestuous relationship would result in inbreeding depression. Boys who had succeeded in killing their fathers and having sex with their mothers would not only be left with genetically inferior offspring, they would also be deprived of paternal support and protection. Many fathers and sons have very close, reciprocated affectional relationships beginning in infancy, and the normative pattern in Western societies is for mothers and sons to have very intimate and affectionate, but decidedly non-sexual relationships.”

Author’s Comment: Mr. Freud appears to have had an extremely warped view of actual relationships between children and their parents.

• MacDonald: “Freud’s wider speculations on human culture rest on a number of extremely naive, pre-scientific conceptualizations of human sexual behavior and its relation to culture. Particularly outrageous was Freud’s ‘primal horde’ story of how over many generations sons had killed their fathers in order to mate with their mothers until Oedipal guilt had forced them to repress this activity.”

Author’s Comment: Children imitate. If children see unscrupulous parents having sexual intercourse before them, they will imitate that activity even though they do not understand what sex is. Freud’s narrow, non-representative experience ruins his theory. If children are given right association, they will go rightly, and if given wrong association, they will go wrongly. They are a blank slate eager to learn and easily molded like soft dough.

• MacDonald: “Crews recounts the case of Horace Frink, an American psychoanalyst who was having an affair with a bank heiress. Freud diagnosed Frink as a latent homosexual (!) and advised him to divorce his wife and marry the heiress, with the stated aim of tapping into the heiress’ funds for a financial contribution to psychoanalysis. To make the plan work, the heiress had to divorce her husband as well. All of this came about, but the two abandoned spouses were devastated and soon died, Frink’s new wife sued for divorce, and Frink himself sank into depression and repeated attempts at suicide.”

Author’s Comment: Yes, and how many other thousands of patients throughout the past 70 years have been advised by Freud’s apostolic psychoanalysts to yield to their sexual urges at the cost of their marital and family responsibilities?

• MacDonald: “Recent Freudian scholarship shows very clearly that Freud tended to make dogmatic claims about the source of his patients’ unhappiness based on nothing more than his own suggestions. His failure to follow even the minimum standards of scientific or rational intellectual inquiry extended to his cultural writings as well.”

Author’s Comment: As late as 1956, a psychiatrist discussing psychoanalysis in the American Journal of Psychiatry complained that “Is it possible that we are developing the equivalent of a secular church, supported by government monies, staffed by a genital-level apostolate unwittingly dispensing a broth of existential atheism, hedonism, and other dubious religio-philosophical ingredients?”

• MacDonald: “Step by step, we are learning that Freud has been the most overrated figure in the entire history of science and medicine, one who wrought immense harm through the propagation of false etiologies, mistaken diagnoses, and fruitless lines of inquiry... By the second decade of the 20th century in America Freud was firmly associated with the movement for sexual freedom. Psychoanalysis has a lot to answer for.”

Author’s Comment: Freud presented several pivotal propositions:

1\. That sex was the most important motivating force for humans.

2\. That sexual inclination of the child toward the parents is natural (incestuous desire is natural).

3\. Religion was a man-made concoction—which “served only as a repressor of humanity and as security to society.”

4\. Human society should not repress its sexual urges but rather should disregard the moral codes of religion.

5\. To replace religion, Freud favored atheistic Buddhist nirvana as a void or release from the suffering of life (of course with out the celibacy stricture).

His intention was anti-religious because he felt religion restricted sex indulgence. So his contribution was to debunk religious restrictions in a so-called scientific manner. He invented a new terminology to combat his enemy, religion. He did not use religion’s terminology of sin/piety, good/bad, hell/heaven, right/wrong.

Freud supposedly provided only analysis and theories—no cures. However, the magic psychoanalytic elixir for most problems was regular doses of sex. Now we see that countries such as the United States of America, which devotedly followed Freud, are shocked to discover generations laden with escalating mental disorders.

• MacDonald: “Since its inception, psychoanalysis has been denounced as a pseudoscience. By the early 1960’s philosophers of science such as Michael Polanyi, Karl Popper, Ernst Nagel and Sidney Hook had noted the self-authenticating nature of psychoanalytic assertions. More recently, highly critical accounts of psychoanalysis from Henri Ellenberger (1970), Frank Sulloway (1992/1979), Adolph Grunbaum (1984), Frank Cioffi (1969, 1970, 1972), and, most recently, Malcolm Macmillan (1991) have appeared.”

Author’s Comment: Freud speculated on some sex-oriented shocks that may or may not have occurred in one’s childhood. Acquiescing to sex impulses will never lead to discovering the root disease because a child can be trained to live happily and productively without sex throughout his whole life.

## Crews

Photo by Jane Scherr: Institute of International Studies, UC Berkeley

Frederick Crews—Professor Emeritus of English and former Chair of the English Department at the University of California at Berkeley. He is a member of the American Academy of Arts and Sciences. He has written twelve books including Skeptical Engagements; The Critics Bear It Away: American Fiction and the Academy (nominated for the National Book Critics Circle Award); The Memory Wars: Freud’s Legacy in Dispute; Unauthorized Freud; and The Random House Handbook for writers, which is in its sixth edition.

• Crews argues that psychoanalysis is a spurious, ineffective pseudoscience, based on the fudged data of an unscrupulous and calculating founder and perpetuated by followers who mimic his craftiness.

Author’s Comment: There is a Bengali proverb which states: vane haye srgala raja. “‘In the jungle, the jackal has become a king.” Jackals are known for their cunning. So one day, as the story goes, a jackal came into a village and fell into a tub of blue dye. He fled to the forest, but he had become blue. So all the animals said, “What is this? Who is this animal?” Even the lion was surprised: “We have never seen you before, sir. So who are you?” The jackal replied, “I have been sent by God.” So they began to worship him as God. But then one night some other jackals began to howl and he could not restrain himself from returning the call of his own kind. So this blue jackal also began to howl and thus he exposed himself before all the other animals as being nothing more than a jackal.

The significance of this verse is that sometimes, disguising himself, a jackal declares himself king and other foolish animals accept him as such. Similarly, Freud has become a big philosopher and is writing philosophy on sex life as if he were supreme.

• Crews: “As late as 1906, he \[Freud\] writes to Jung that he has not successfully completed a single psychoanalysis.”

• Crews: “America embraced Freudianism in a spirit that Freud himself found ridiculous. What Freud wanted was more civilization, but people didn’t care. They cared about finding someone to validate the tendency that they were already inclined to follow.”

Author’s Comment: Thanks to Freud, 20th-century America saw a sexual awakening and a negligence of past moral virtues.

He stood as the primary authority on the so-called advantages of sexual freedom.

• Crews: “The entire system of classical psychoanalytic thought rests on nothing more substantial than Freud’s word that it is true. And that is why the late Nobelist in medicine Sir Peter Medewar famously condemned that system as a stupendous intellectual confidence trick.”

Author’s Comment: Freud was, according to Crews, a charlatan who did not cure patients of hysteria by his getting them to open up about sexual abuse and incest from their past.

## Srila Prabhupada

His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupada (1896-1977) (also known as Srila Prabhupada) the world’s most influential spiritual master from India; founder of the International Society for Krishna Consciousness. He took birth in Calcutta, India, in a family of pure devotees of Lord Krishna. He first met his spiritual master, Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati Gosvami, in 1922, and in 1933 became his formally initiated disciple. After his household duties were completed, Srila Prabhupada accepted the renounced order of life (sannyasa) in 1959. In 1965 he sailed to the United States and founded the International Society for Krishna Consciousness. He wrote 84 books on Vedic knowledge including the Bhagavad-gita As It Is, Srimad-Bhagavatam (31 volumes) and Sri Caitanya-caritamrta (17 volumes).

• Student: “Freud’s philosophy is that people have neuroses or disorders of their total personality, that there is conflict, there is anxiety, there is frustration, and that all of these have origin.

Srila Prabhupada: But I am telling you that all these are due to sex.

Student: That’s what Freud is also saying.

Srila Prabhupada: That we admit. An embodied living being must have hunger, and he must have the sex impulse. We find that even in the animals these impulses are there. Freud is encouraging, and our process is to stop. That is the difference. Freud says that when there is sex impulse, enjoy.”

Author’s Comment: In a sense Freud is correct in observing that sex pervades the psyche and dominates. Freud’s proposal, however, is to indulge that propensity for sex, whereas Srila Prabhupada claims that the correct thing to do is to dissuade people from sex indulgence, as far as possible. In this way there is agreement about the malady and disagreement over the cure.

• Srila Prabhupada: “Take Freud’s philosophy and become highly advanced in civilization. You can become perfect pig, whole day and night eat stool, and as soon as you get another opposite party, have sex. Doesn’t matter whether it is daughter or mother or sister. That’s all. Now the Freud’s philosophy is being translated in Hindi and so many other languages. We are advancing in civilization, Indians. They are translating this Freud’s philosophy, pig civilization.”

Author’s Comment: Freud postulated that: A) children desire sex with their parents, B) this desire is natural and C) no one should repress one’s natural sexual feelings. Thus Freud circuitously legitimized incest.

Shocked Again

• Student: In his theory of psychoanalysis, Freud states that by remembering and reevaluating emotional shocks from our childhood, we can release the tension we are feeling now. Srila Prabhupada: But what is the guarantee that one will not get shocked again? He may cure the results of one shock, but there is no guarantee that the patient will not receive another shock. Therefore Freud’s treatment is useless. Freud tries to cure the reactions of one kind of shock, but other shocks will come, one after another. This is how material nature works. If you solve one problem, another problem arises immediately. And if you solve that one, another one comes. As long as you are under the control of material nature, these repeated shocks will come.

Author’s Comment: The point being made here is broader than just observing one’s experience in one lifetime. The Vedic teachings explain that each person is a spiritual being who traverses through many lifetimes, in different bodies and species. The accumulated karma or reactions for past work from previous lives affects one’s present and future situation.

• Student: Freud says that anxiety develops when the superego represses the primitive desires of the id to protect the ego. Is such repression of basic instincts very healthy? Srila Prabhupada: Yes. For us repression means restraining oneself from doing something which, in the long run, is against one’s welfare. For example, suppose you are suffering from diabetes and the doctor says, “Don’t eat any sweet food.” If you desire to eat sweets, you must repress that desire. Similarly, in our system of brahmacarya there is also repression. A brahmacari should not sit down with a young woman, or even see one. He may desire to see a young woman, but he must repress the desire. This is called tapasya, or voluntary repression.

Student: But aren’t these desires given outlet in other ways? For instance, instead of looking at a beautiful woman, we look at the beautiful form of Krsna.

Srila Prabhupada: Yes, that is our process: param drsöva nivartate. If you have a better engagement, you can give up an inferior engagement. When you are captivated by seeing the beautiful form of Krsna, naturally you have no more desire to see the beautiful form of a young woman.

Author’s Comment: Although the influence of his theory is still very strong, there is a possibility that it may be lessened as reported in a popular internet encyclopedia: “Freud’s psychological theories are hotly disputed today and many leading academic and research psychiatrists regard him as a charlatan.”

## 7. Libido-Less Diet

It appears to me necessary to every physician to be skilled in nature and to strive to know, if he would wish to perform his duties, what man is in relation to the articles of food and drink and to his other occupations and what are the effects of each of them on everyone. Whoever does not know what effect these things produce upon a man cannot know the consequences which result from them. Whoever pays no attention to these things, or, paying attention, does not comprehend them, how can he understand the diseases which befall man? For by every one of these things a man is affected and changed this way and that, and the whole of his life is subject to them—whether in health, convalescence or disease. Nothing else then can be more important and necessary to know than these things.

—Hippocrates

Nutrition and the consumption of certain foods have a profound biological influence on the process of reproduction and sexual activity.

## Pythagoras

Wikipedia: Portraits from the Dibner Library of the History of Science and Technology

• Pythagoras claimed that protein foods augment sexual inclinations and that a low-protein, strictly vegetarian diet was essential for all who wish to live in continence.

Author’s Comment: Pythagoras’ followers abstained from eating protein-rich foods with the goal of reaching a high level of continence. Among them are some of the world’s foremost scientists, philosophers and physicians of the ancient world, including Aristotle, Alexander the Great, Plato and Ovid.

## Lycurgus

Lycurgus (7th Century BC) known as the father of Sparta and the founder of the Spartan constitution. The earliest mention of him is in Herodotus. Nothing is known of his life; however, he is generally associated with the 7th cent. BC at the time when a revolt of the Messenian subjects nearly ruined Sparta. Lycurgus led a reform in the government and in the city’s social system to establish a machine of war that would preclude further trouble from the helots and other subjects. (Columbia Encyclopedia)

• Lycurgus forbade the consumption of meats and enforced a vegetarian diet. Alcohol was also prohibited. He advised intestinal purification through proper diet as the basic factor in the cure of tabes dorsalis \[a disorder of the nervous system characteristic of late syphilis and marked by degeneration of nerve fibers, wasting, pain; Encarta Encyclopedia\].

• Lycurgus forbade his people “to call in the assistance of butchers and cooks, or to fatten like voracious animals in private. For so not only their manners would be corrupted but their bodies disordered, and abandoned to all manner of sensuality and dissoluteness.”

Author’s Comment: Dr. Elliot, in a thesis called “Aedology,” wrote that it indeed is because of a poor diet, high in protein, that one experiences heightened levels of sexual energy. “The abnormal intensity of the sexual impulse is largely due to improper and too stimulating food, to liquors of all kinds, spiced meats, etc.—because such improper diet causes irritation in the mucous membrane lining the digestive tract; and as this is similar to that lining the genital organs, they also become irritated and congested.”

## Porphyry

• Porphyry: “Given man who considered whence he came and whither he must go and from these considerations resolves not to be led astray or governed by his passions; and let such a man tell me whether a rich animal diet is more easily procured or incites less to irregular passions and appetites than a light vegetable diet? But if neither he nor a physician, nor indeed any reasonable man whatsoever, dares to affirm this, why do we oppress ourselves with animal food?”

• Porphyry: “Find me someone who is eager to live, as far as possible, in accordance with intellect and to be undistracted by the passions which affect the body, and let him demonstrate that meat-eating is easier to provide than dishes of fruits and vegetables; that meat is cheaper to prepare than inanimate food for which chefs are not needed at all; that compared with inanimate food, it is intrinsically pleasure—free and lighter on the digestion, and more quickly assimilated by the body than vegetables; that it is less provocative of desires and less conducive to obesity and robustness than a diet of inanimate food.”

Author’s Comment: The tongue is the most formidable sense to control. However, if one can conquer the tongue, then he can subjugate the urge to overeat and then one can triumph over the lust of the genital. There is a straight line going down from the tongue to the belly to the genital. If the first is subdued then the next two can be as well.

## Celsus

• Celsus advised avoidance of alcohol and a vegetable diet.

Author’s Comment: In our own experience, it can be corroborated that avoiding alcohol and eating vegetarian foodstuffs is a superior method of cultivating higher intellect.

## Galen

• Galen: “Those who deny that the difference in food renders some temperate and others dissolute, some chaste and others incontinent, some modest and others conspicuous, let them, I say, who deny this truth come to me; let them follow my directions in regard to eating and drinking; and I will promise them that their powers of their mind will be augmented, and they will acquire more genius, more memory, more prudence, and more diligence.”

Author’s Comment: This is a tremendous challenge to our society. A few days ago we chanced to see the cover of a popular news weekly magazine. It spoke of efforts to overcome the loss of memory among the aged. If people truly desire memory, etc., they might do well to follow the advice of Galen.

## Plutarch

Johannes Gutenberg-Universitat Philologie (Alfred Hornung)

Mestrius Plutarch (c.45-c.120) Greek historian/biographer and essayist. He was born in the small town of Chaeronea, in the Greek region known as Boeotia, probably during the reign of the Roman Emperor Claudius. Plutarch traveled widely in the Mediterranean world, including twice to Rome. His best-known work is Lives of the Noble Greeks and Romans, a series of biographies of famous men, arranged in tandem to illuminate their common moral virtues or failings. The remainder of his surviving work is collected under the title of the Moralia (loosely translated as Moral Matters). Plutarch’s writings had enormous influence on English and French literature. Shakespeare, for example, extensively quoted—and more extensively paraphrased—from Plutarch’s work.

• Plutarch: “Wine and meat blunt the soul.”

Author’s Comment: The modern world has seen the rise of aphrodisiacal materials that stimulate sexual urges. These materials include high-protein diets and indulgence in foods and activities such as alcohol, coffee, and suggestive literature and motion pictures.

## Diogenes Laertius

Diogenes Laertius (3rd century AD) a biographer of ancient Greek philosophers. He was a native of Laerte in Cilicia, (not to be confused with the cynic philosopher Diogenes of Sinope.) His Lives of the Eminent Philosophers (Philosophoi Biol), in 10 books, is still extant and is considered an invaluable source of information on the development of Greek philosophy.

• Laertes Diogenes: “A state of purity is brought about by abstaining from flesh that has either been killed or died of itself—from melanuri \[mice\], from mullet \[fish\], from eggs, and from such animals that lay eggs.”

Author’s Comment: Protein-rich diets produce high amounts of uric acid. These toxins constipate the intestines causing hardened fecal matter to exert pressure on the colon. Therefore, to eliminate constipation, one must closely monitor one’s intake of foods high in uric acids.

## St. Jerome

Saint Jerome (345-420) made a translation of the Bible which came to be called the Vulgate. He was born in Stridon, Dalmatia (in the former Yugoslavia); after his preliminary education, he went to Rome, the center of learning at that time, and thence to Trier, Germany. He was a master of Latin, Greek, Hebrew and Chaldaic and he translated most of the Old Testament from the Hebrew. Finally he settled in Bethlehem, where he lived in the cave believed to have been the birthplace of Christ.

• St. Jerome: “The forge of Vulcan and the volcanoes of Vesuvius and of Mt. Olympus do not burn more than those young men who live on succulent meats and indulge in wine.”

Author’s Comment: In his “Letter to St. Eustochium” Saint Jerome wrote, “I tamed my flesh by fasting whole weeks.” It is a fact that diet affects one’s sexual appetite in a direct manner. The more moderate the diet, the more moderate the lust.
