Foods low in protein that form alkalines include fruits and vegetables that neutralize the acids, which cause the prostatic urethra to become inflamed.

## Tolstoy

Count Leo Nikolaevich Tolstoy (1828-1910) one of Russia’s greatest authors. He was born at his family’s estate at Yasnaya Polyana. His young adulthood is best described with his own words from his book Confession: “Lying, stealing, promiscuity of every kind, drunkenness, violence, murder—there was not a crime I did not commit... Thus I lived for 10 years.”

Later in life, Tolstoy formulated a unique Christian philosophy which espoused non-resistance to evil as the proper response to aggression, and which put great emphasis on fair treatment of the poor and working class. He was a vegetarian, and his writings strongly influenced Gandhi.

• Tolstoy taught that chastity was necessary after marriage as it was before and that husband and wife should live together as brother and sister and recommended continence as the only natural and effective method of birth control.

Author’s Comment: The notion that marriage is meant for constant sexual activity between husband and wife is veritably a great cause of distress, frustration, death (abortion) and divorce.

• Tolstoy advised abstinence from meat, alcohol, and all stimulating foods and drinks and a life of hard labor, which would use up bodily energy and thus reduce the excessive sexualism of the idle rich who live on a highly stimulating diet of animal foods.

Author’s Comment: The diet has a direct relationship to one’s sex drive—foods such as meat and coffee, for instance, are aphrodisiacs. Meat especially contains high levels of uric acids, which can cause arousal in those who consume it, but those who enjoy a vegetarian diet also will enjoy the opposite effect.

• Tolstoy: “Any man can find hundreds of examples around him showing that continence is possible and less dangerous or harmful to health thanincontinence.”

Author’s Comment: A low-protein, vegetarian diet, then, is crucial to eliminating these life-threatening conditions.

## Kellogg

Dr. John Harvey Kellogg (1852-1943) American surgeon, hygienist, food manufacturer and author of 50 books, Kellogg was born in Tyrone, Michigan, and educated at Bellevue Hospital Medical College in New York City. He became superintendent of the Battle Creek Sanitarium in 1876. He was the founder of Battle Creek College and the founder and medical director of the Miami-Battle Creek Sanitarium in Miami Springs, Florida. He and his brother, Will Keith Kellogg, developed processes for improving and successfully manufacturing nutritious healthy cereal foods.

• Kellogg: “Flesh, condiments, eggs, tea, coffee, chocolate, and all other stimulants have a powerful influence directly upon the blood; and through nervous sympathy with the brain, the passions are aroused. Poor blood, filled with crude, poorly digested food, is irritating to the nervous system and especially to those extremely delicate nerves which govern the reproductive function. Irritation provokes congestion; congestion excites sexual desires; excited passions increase the local disturbances; and thus each reacts upon the other ever increasing the injury and liability of future damage.”

Author’s Comment: A low-protein diet will help curb physical ailments and sexual desires in adults, so too in youths will it decrease the urge to masturbate. In females, especially, a low-protein diet will stave off the inflammation of the genital mucosa \[a membrane which secretes mucous\].

• Kellogg: “While children are raised upon such articles, or upon food with which they are thoroughly mingled, what wonder that they occasionally turn out bad. How many mothers, while teaching their children the principles of virtue in the nursery, unwittingly stimulate passions at the dinner table until vice becomes almost a physical necessity? Thus these exciting causes continue their insidious work through youth and more mature years. Right under the eyes of fathers and mothers they work the ruin of children, exciting such storms of passion as may become uncontrollable. Nothing tends so powerfully to keep the passions in abeyance as a simple diet, free from condiments, especially when coupled with a generous amount of exercise.”

Author’s Comment: The powerful tongue dictates, and persons submit to its demands by supplying cigarettes, alcohol, coffee, tea, flesh, chicken, etc.

## Ellis

• Ellis noted the mild sexual feeling among the Irish who live chiefly on potatoes and buttermilk, as against the extreme sexuality of the French who live on a highly stimulating diet of meat, sea foods and wine.

Author’s Comment: The aged and venerable act of fasting (according to scriptural regulations), is another useful method by which one can control one’s sexual urges, for it helps clean the body of uric acids. In Vedic culture one is advised to fast, at least partially, twice monthly.

• Ellis: “The great bulk of them \[Trappists\] are in absolutely ideal health and strength, seldom ailing and capable of vast work, mental and physical. Their life is very simple and very regular. A healthier body of men and women with perfect equanimity of temper, this latter I lay great stress on, it would be difficult to find. Health beams in their eyes and countenance and actions.”

Author’s Comment: The Trappists referred to here are a vegetarian religious sect whose members live in chastity.

• Ellis: “Food and drink are powerful sexual stimulants. This is true even of the simplest and most wholesome nourishment and is more especially true of flesh meat and above all alcohol. A beefsteak is probably as powerful a sexual stimulant as any food. Fish, shellfish (especially oysters) and eggs have a popular reputation as aphrodisiacs. The same applies to caviar (fish eggs)... Certain sea foods contain even more uric acid than meat, and this accounts for their being even stronger sexual stimulants.”

Author’s Comment: In the Krishna consciousness movement, the initiates promise to avoid the eating of meat, fish and eggs. Furthermore, they abstain from alcohol and other intoxicants such as coffee and tea.

## Lydston

Bellevue Medical Hospital where Lydston graduated; the nation’s oldest public hospital, located in New York City (1736 to present) NYU Medical School Division of Pediatric Infectious Diseases and Immunology.

George Frank Lydston (1858-1923) a well-known and respected surgeon and endocrinologist in the academic world who also was involved in the early clinical trials of this procedure. He graduated from Bellevue Hospital Medical School in New York in 1879 and later became professor of diseases of the genitourinary organs at the Medical School of the University of Illinois and professor of criminal anthropology at Kent College of Law. He was the author of Sexual Neurasthenia and the Prostate.

• Lydston: “The importance of careful attention to dietetic regimen in controlling the tendency to masturbation and sexual excess can scarcely be overrated. The relation of a stimulating and highly nutritious diet to sexual desire and capacity was well understood by the ancients. The old maxim—‘Sine Bac-cho et Cerere frigit Venus’—is as pertinent as it is classic. In the lives of many individuals, Bacchus and Venus go hand in hand as dominant powers. With some persons, excessive sexual desire is directly dependent upon high living.”

Author’s Comment: The Latin maxim “Sine Cerere et Baccho friget Venus” means without food (Ceres) and wine (Bacchus), love (Venus) grows cold. Dr. Miller gives his opinion: “Feeding children upon pork, gravies, eggs, and pastry made of lard, salt, meat, with mustard and pepper, rich pies and cake and sweetmeats, vinegar, pickles, tea and coffee, and everything of this description, tends to fire the blood, derange the nerves, and bring on a precocious development of the sexual passion.”

• Lydston: “If one would remain continent, he must not only abjure all mental sources of sexual excitement, but he must abstain from stimulants, tobacco, highly seasoned foods—in short, from all articles of diet that tend to induce nervous irritability. It is probable that a strictly vegetarian diet is the best one that can be advised for an individual who desires to remain continent in mind and body. With present pernicious social customs the unaided efforts of the will are not always successful in conquering the lusts of the flesh.”

Author’s Comment: The Bhagavad-gita explains that “while contemplating the objects of the senses, a person develops attachment for them, and from such attachment lust develops.” As Dr. Lydston explains as well, mental sources of sexual excitement incite lust. The pornography business, for example, has developed into a monstrosity of apparently unstoppable proportions. The leaders of society have no proper understanding of the damage created by pornography nor do they have enough strength to overcome the crisis.

## Mowry

Dr. Albert Mowry—author and instructor in genitourinary surgery at Northwestern University Medical School.

• Mowry: “I am a dyed-in-the-wool believer that these \[nocturnal emissions\] are unnatural and when frequent are pathological, doing damage to the economy and causing a sensation of weakness and lassitude that is not imaginary.”

Author’s Comment: Later, in Chapter Eight, there are eight aspects to sex described by Srila Sridhara Svami. Although the first seven kinds of sex do not cause the actual loss of semen, they do, according to Ayurveda, cause semen to separate from the blood. Such separated semen then tries to escape when opportunity arises, either in dreams or in other ways.

• Mowry: “Water kills more soldiers than bullets; and so do nocturnal emissions in reality cause more real damage than ordinarily supposed. The main cause is a digestive condition of the deep urethra... It has been suggested that these may be caused by some irritant in the urine due to faulty metabolism... A well defined diet must be ordered and a light supper must be eaten leisurely. The bowels must be regular. Prevent distension \[putting internal pressure upon\] of the bladder. These passions occur practically always in the early morning and are due to the distended bladder pressing the already irritated deep urethra, causing an explosion.”

Author’s Comment: Preventing distention or internal pressure upon the bladder means not to overeat. Overeating fills the belly with food and causes indigestion, which, in turn, puts pressure on the bladder or genital. Fluids such as coffee and tea should be avoided, as they are high in uric acid. Dr. Mowry describes uric acid as an irritant to urine. One must regulate one’s blood and urine to be as alkaline as possible; otherwise, the fluids could irritate the mucus lining of the prostatic urethra. Contrary to popular belief, nocturnal emissions will not occur in a physically healthy young man whose diet is low in protein.

## Ehret

Professor Arnold Ehret (1866-1922) German nutritionist. He was born near Freiburg, Germany; he moved to California in 1914 and became an influential lecturer on natural diet. He died untimely due to a fall on slippery pavement.

• Ehret put patients on a low protein fruit and vegetable diet, strictly prohibiting the use of aphrodisiacal foods and beverages, such as meats, alcoholics, coffee, pepper, tea, and also tobacco. This caused the nocturnal emissions to disappear in males and leucorrhea (genital mucous discharge) in women.

Author’s Comment: A diet high in uric acids, especially in females, will damage the uterine mucus membrane and will bring on leucorrhea. New England gynecologist Dr. Shroyer has written that alkaline diets have proven quite helpful in eliminating such maladies. Tumors, too, may recede and disappear, since they simply are the product of an untreated membrane inflammation.

## Lorand

• Lorand: “It seems to me quite certain that a plentiful diet, containing in particular more protein, would have an excitant influence upon the sexual function. We are familiar with the fact that the male sex glands, when well filled, exert a certain stimulus upon the sexual sensory centers. The heads of the spermatozoa, like the cells of the nuclei consist chiefly of nucleoproteids. When a wonderable amount of food rich in nuclein is absorbed in the diet, the spermatozoa become more apt to exert an excitant effect... With poor food, the sexual impulse, or libido, is but little stimulated and the fulfillment of the act is incomplete; with overfeeding, especially with meat and certain other foods, the sexual impulse may be stimulated.”

Author’s Comment: The “poor” food described here does not mean poor in quality. Rather it means “simple” or “not rich” food. The urges of the tongue and genitals are formidable. Unable to control the tongue, conditioned souls eat more than they need and naturally genital agitation follows forcing one to seek illicit sex.

• Lorand said that animal proteins (meat, fowl, fish, and eggs) are most potent in increasing sexual activity—which may be a principal reason why many persons use them, as is also true of alcohol.

Author’s Comment: Dr. Newton offers his concurring opinion: “They who have ever carefully noted the effects on themselves of most kinds of alcoholic stimulants, of coffee, oysters, eggs, spices, and an excess of animal food of almost any kind—and especially those who prefer these things because of their stimulating effects in this direction—as well as they who find pleasure in the filthy conversation and practices referred to cannot surely with justice charge upon ‘nature’ the exuberance of their amatory \[sexual\] desires. In so far, then, as this appetite is excessive in us beyond the requirements of nature and enlightened reason, it is unnatural and diseased. A careful abstinence from exciting foods, drinks, acts, and thoughts, and the use of appropriate means to allay excitement—these, persisted in, will bring victory in duetime.”

## Carnegie Institute Experiment

“Carnegie Institute” Program of the International Centennial Celebration and Conventions of the Disciples of Christ (1909)

## Benedict

Francis Gano Benedict (1870-1957) chemist who rose to prominence when he assisted Atwater in the Department of Chemistry at Wesleyan University. Over a 12-year period, they conducted more than 500 experiments concerning rest, exercise and diet using the Atwater-Rosa respiration calorimeter. Their results appeared in six bulletins of the Office of Experiment Stations of the U.S. Department of Agriculture under the general title Experiments on the Metabolism of Matter and Energy in the Human Body. In addition, Benedict published studies on the physiological action of alcohol and the effects of muscular exercise and mental effort on energy metabolism. In 1907, Benedict became Director of the Nutrition Laboratory in Boston, a post he held for 30 years until retirement.

The following report is mostly taken from Dr. Bernard’s Nutritional Sex Control and Rejuvenation:

One of the most extensive and careful nutritional experiments in modern times on the influence of diet on human vitality, including sexual behavior, was conducted at the Boston Nutrition Laboratory of the Carnegie Institute of Washington located under the direction of Dr. Francis G. Benedict, the noted nutritionist. The results of this experiment have been published under the title “Human Vitality and Efficiency under Prolonged Restricted Diet.” (Publication 280, Carnegie Institute, Washington, D.C., 1919).

This experiment was carried on during the First World War when many food restrictions were in force. Its object was to investigate the effects of such food restriction on the general metabolism of the body. For this purpose, two squads of twelve men each were chosen from the students of Springfield Training School. One of these squads served as a control but was also used during a short period of dietary restriction. The other squad was put on a restricted low-protein diet for a period of four months, during which time these men were carefully studied.

The protein content of this diet was reduced from 100 grams per day, the amount consumed prior to the experiment, to about 60 grams. At the same time, the caloric value of the diet was reduced from between 3,000 and 4,000 prior to the experiment to about 2,100. Though the men lost twelve percent of their weight, they were apparently normal throughout the course of the experiment and were able to carry on their regular work and exercises. During this time they were under observation, and regular readings were taken of their blood pressure, gaseous metabolism, nitrogenous metabolism, pulse rate, sex life, etc.

It was found that this dietary restriction caused a noticeable drop in blood pressure, in pulse rate, and in basal metabolism. During the entire experiment there was an accumulated loss of nitrogen of approximately 175 grams, which would correspond to about 1,100 grams of protein. These changes were correlated with definite changes in the sex life of these men, and these were of such a striking nature that they confirmed Evans’ observations on the effects of: a low-protein diet on the reproductive cycle of animals and showed that such a diet lowered the reproductive activity of men as well. In almost all cases, the subjects noted a reduction or cessation of sex desire, nocturnal emissions, erections, erotic dreams, and other voluntary as well as involuntary sexual phenomena.

## Miles

Walter Richard Miles PhD (1885-1978) Professor of Psychology at Yale University and President of the American Psychological Association (1932).

In an article entitled “Sex Expression of Men Living on a Lowered Nutritional Level,” Dr. W. R. Miles gives a detailed report on the sex lives of the subjects participating in Benedict’s experiment. From the report it is clear that since physical and mental energy was maintained at a normal level throughout the course of the experiment, the resulting disappearance of sexual manifestations was not due to exhaustion but rather to the removal of a source of aphrodisiacal stimulation (by uric acid and other metabolic toxins) ordinarily supplied by food, especially by animal proteins. Dr. Miles writes:

“Our data indicates that nature demands a rather high metabolic level for the normal functioning of sex in man. Riddle and other workers find with animals that sex is more or less dependent on the metabolic level. These investigators have shown that by modifying one they can modify the other. It is commonly believed that the sex instinct is stronger in men than in women. The large amount of metabolism data from this laboratory and other institutes has proven that the metabolism of man is higher than that of woman.

In connection with the periodic psychological measurements taken during the course of the experiment, the men were asked from time to time for brief accounts of their subjective impressions, especially concerning their sex life. One man volunteered the statement that the reduced diet had in his case eradicated all sex desires. He was quite positive in this statement, and it therefore seemed necessary to collect evidence from the other subjects in order to prove or disprove the generalization of this assertion. The results clearly indicate a method of treatment for achieving restraint of sexual tendencies in pathological cases of sexual dissipation.”

## McLester

James Somerville McLester, MD (1877-1954) internationally respected physician, professor, and nutritionist. He was born in Tuscaloosa, Alabama in 1877 and moved with his family to the young city of Birmingham in 1883. He entered the University of Alabama in 1892. In addition to faithfully fulfilling his responsibilities to his students and patients, Dr. McLester continued to study and publish works on nutrition. In 1934 he was elected president of the American Medical Association, and in 1953 the association’s board of trustees honored him with the prestigious Joseph Goldberger Award for his “outstanding contributions in the field of clinical nutrition.” Shirley Salloway Kahn, PhD and vice president for development, alumni, and external relations at University of Alabama said: “It \[The McLester Endowed Medical Scholarship Fund\] will ensure that the legacy of excellence in health care started by Dr. McLester will live on for generations to come.”

Referring to Dr. Benedict’s experiment on the effect of a low-protein diet in reducing sexual desire and expression, Professor McLester, in his “Nutrition and Diet in Health and Disease,” writes: “The sexual urge is materially influenced by diet: It has been the experience of those men who have served as subjects in feeding experiments with reduced rations, particularly when the protein quota was restricted, that there was a distinct lowering of the libido. During the war period in Germany, Rubner observed that the restricted food supply was responsible not only for a lowering of the libido in men but for amenorrhea (cessation of menstruation) and other menstrual disturbances.”

## Rubner

MAX RUBNER (1854-1932) German physiologist who experimented with a respiratory apparatus and calorimeter. His experiments conclusively showed that “the exclusive source of heat in warm-blooded animals is... the energy supply of the nutritive materials.”

Let us now consider Dr. Benedict’s experiment in greater detail. During the course of the experiment, the men were questioned concerning such matters: as nocturnal emissions, tendency to erection, sex desire at dances and during periods of association with women, erotic dreams, etc. In most cases the men reported a marked diminution or a complete disappearance of sex desire, sexual dreams, and nocturnal emissions during the four months of low-protein feeding, but soon after returning to the previous high-protein diet, with increased consumption of meat, all of these sexual phenomena returned with normal intensity, including nocturnal emissions. Dr. Benedict says: “We believe, that the self-observations of the men are on the whole, trustworthy. It is clear that they show a decrease in sexual interest and expression, which, according to some of the men, reached the point of obliteration with lowered nutritional level, coincident to the prolonged reduced diet, and that, furthermore there was a prompt return to normal conditions with uncontrolled feeding.”

Let us now review the reports of these young men. Subject A noticed frequent erections while on the previous high-protein diet especially when embracing his girl, and every morning upon awakening. Nocturnal emissions occurred once a month. But on the low-protein diet he observed: “During the diet experiment there was no emission I can recall and, I believe, no erection. My roommate at college noticed this; and one morning he said, ‘Say! You have lost your manhood!’ When with my fiancêe during this period, nothing of a sexual nature would cone to my mind. I think that if I had not been engaged, I would have left off visiting the lady or attending to any social functions. I was quite astonished and wondered at the change that had come over me.”

Subject B noticed reduced sexual feelings. Subject C observed disappearance of nocturnal emissions—but on returning to high-protein feeding, these promptly returned and also erections. Subject D noticed decreased sexual tendencies and absence of erection while on the low-protein diet. Subject E writes: “I am very definite in the conviction that there is a reduction in sexual desire during the period of low-protein diet. During the time of losing weight, there was the least irritability. I think the kind of food affects sex appetite—meat causing stimulating of it. Do not recall any nocturnal emissions during the diet. Before the diet they were rather frequent. I have usually to put up a pretty stiff fight against the instinct and noticed that it was not nearly so difficult to control during the period of the experiment.”

Subject I noticed after the experiment and on returning to a rich meat diet, increased nocturnal emissions, erections, and sexual excitement, which were reduced during the diet experiment. Subject J, previously, had nocturnal emissions once a week or more frequently. The low-protein diet caused them to greatly diminish in frequency. On returning to uncontrolled high-protein eating, they became as frequent as before. Subject L says: “The low-protein diet experiment just about unsexed me. During the first two weeks of the period there was at the end of each week sexual intercourse, but at these times there was no keen desire for passion, nor did the occasions produce the normal pleasure commonly associated with them. I thought to myself, ‘How foolish to indulge in this simply by habit.’

Subject L and M had no nocturnal emissions during the four months of the low-protein experiment. The latter had them twice or more a month before. Subject Q writes: “During the diet, sex was repulsive. I had no emissions and no erections; but after eating uncontrolled, both of these were frequent. The change, considering the short period of dietetic reduction, was more than I would have believed.” Subject R said: “It seems to me that during the last week of the period of diet there was less flow of blood through the sex organs. They were less irritable, and there was much slighter tendency to erection; and not in reference to any particular time of the day, there was an absence of sex sensation.”

The subjects in Dr. Benedict’s experiment consumed 60 grams of protein daily, which was found by Professor Chittenden of Yale University, in his classic experiments on the protein requirement, which he reduced by nearly half the amount previously thought necessary, to be sufficient for physiological needs.

## Hindhede

Mikkel Hindhede (1862-1945) eminent Danish nutritionist.

But the later experiments of Dr. Hind-hede, the eminent Danish nutritionist, have shown that the Chittenden protein standard of 60 grams daily was over twice as much as was really necessary. He proved that on a diet in which Irish potatoes furnished the exclusive protein, only about 25 grams was necessary, since he maintained hard working men in perfect health and vigor for 300 days to nearly two years on potato diets, supplying no more than this amount of protein daily. This would mean that Benedict’s subjects were really consuming more than twice as much protein as their bodies really needed and that if their protein intake was reduced to actual physiological requirements, according to Hindhede’s nitrogen equilibrium experiments, all sexual phenomena would disappear from their lives, as is the normal state among wild animals during non-reproductive periods.

From the above evidence, we must conclude that sex in its ordinary manifestation among civilized human beings is not the product of natural instinct that it is generally supposed to be but is a chemotropism evoked or conditioned reflex (in Pavlov’s sense) evoked in response to aphrodisiacal stimulation by foods and beverages, especially animal proteins, alcohol, coffee, and also tobacco. This tropistic reaction, in both its physical and psychical aspects, is subject to voluntary control through diet, an alkaline-forming, low-protein vegetable diet reducing it, while an acid-forming high-protein meat diet increases it.

## Chittenden

Russell Henry Chittenden (1856-1943) physiological chemist and a prolific writer; generally regarded as the “father of American biochemistry,” having led the country’s first biochemistry department (ca.1870s-1920s) at Yale University. He was born in New Haven, Connecticut. Chittenden continued his mentor’s (German biochemist Willy Kuhne) research program on the action of proteolytic enzymes, at the same time adapting the work at the New Haven laboratory to local and national needs. During World War I Chittenden was a member of a committee in charge of nutrition and food supply to the Allies. His research program, tied to the region’s agricultural chemistry and nutrition, was noted for work on toxicology and on protein requirements in humans. He was the president of the American Physiological Society and a member of editorial boards of journals such as the American Journal of Physiology, the Journal of Experimental Medicine, and the Journal of Biological Chemistry.

• Chittenden: “We are all creatures of habit, and our palates are pleasantly excited by the rich animal foods with their high content of proteid (protein), and we may well question whether our dietetic habits are not based more upon the dictates of our palates than upon scientific reasoning or true physiological needs.”

Author’s Comment: Prof. Chittenden believed that people did not become prosperous because they ate high protein diets, but rather they ate meat and other expensive high protein foods because they could afford them.

• Chittenden: “With proteid (protein) foods... when oxidized, (they) yield a row of crystalline nitrogenous products which ultimately pass out of the body through the kidneys. (These nitrogen-based protein byproducts)—fre-quently spoken of as toxins—float about through the body and may exercise more or less of a deleterious influence upon the system, or, being temporarily deposited, may exert some specific or local influence that calls for their speedy removal.”

Author’s Comment: Prof. Chittenden carefully studied the effects of high-protein diets on the bodies of both men and women and concluded that such diets cause the greatest damage to the liver and kidneys. To prove his hypothesis, Chittenden experimented on himself for nine months with a low-protein diet. This new diet helped ease the pain of the frequent headaches to which he was prone, as well as his arthritis of the knee, which had plagued him for years. He continued to remain active in body and in mind, operating on just 40 grams of protein each day.

Chittenden also developed three studies in continuing his efforts to link protein intake with health. For his first study, he employed the assistance of five young men who attended Yale University. For his second experiment, Chittenden studied thirteen men from the U.S. Army. And finally, Chittenden studied eight student athletes at Yale University. He put all on a diet of approximately 60 grams of protein daily. They all remained healthy and performed well.

After the studies were completed in 1904, Chittenden found that 35-50 grams of protein each day was enough to maintain proper physical health while being involved in great physical activity. Other researchers in later years found Chittenden’s findings to be accurate.

## Tissot and Deslandes

• Tissot and Deslandes consider nocturnal emissions as pathological due to an inflammatory condition of the urethra, resulting from hyperacidity of the blood and urine. This means that foods which acidify the blood and which contain an excess of acid-forming elements tend to irritate the genital mucous membranes and predispose toward such emissions, as well as to sexual manifestations in general. The first step in the cure of this morbid manifestation is to overcome constipation through an alkaline, low-protein, strictly vegetarian diet which reduces mucous membrane irritation in the prostatic urethra, the seat of sexual feeling.

Author’s Comment: It is interesting to refer to Dr. Hindhede’s observations which have shown that a diet high in potato consumption has a marked alkalizing effect on the blood and helps it to counteract and neutralize uric acid.

## Michelet

Old engraving reproduced in the History of the French literature of Emile Faguet, Plon-Nourishes, 1916, 20th edition Portrait of Michelet by Thomas Seams (Carnavalet Museum). Presentation of work (History by the image, 1789—1939)

Jules Michelet (1798-1874) French professor, historian and prolific author. He was born in Paris to a father who was a printer of modest income. He attended Charlemagne College. His most famous work was a nineteen volume set on the History of France chronicling the past from Charlemagne to the French Revolution in the 18th century.

• Michelet: “A profound revolution has taken place; we have abandoned the sober French regimen and increasingly adopted the heavy bloodstained diet of our neighbors. Worst of all, we inflict it on our children. How strange to see a mother offer her daughter to whom but a short time previous she gave the breast, a coarse diet of bleeding meat and such excitants as wine and coffee. And yet she is astonished to find the girl violent, whimsical, and passionate. It is herself she ought to accuse. What she does not see, a matter far more serious, is that in our precocious French race, the senses are directly stimulated by such a diet. Instead of strengthening, it disturbs, weakens, and enervates. Both for the woman and the child, it is a favor, an act of love, to be necessarily frugivorous, to avoid the fetid odor of meat and live rather on innocent food which causes the death of no living thing and pleases smell and taste alike.”

Author’s Comment: It is a valid observation by Michelet that promiscuity and a diet rich in meat go hand in hand. France is famous for its promiscuity whereas the largely vegetarian-based Indian subcontinent is far more subdued in sexual matters.

## McDougall

William McDougall (1871-1938) British-born American psychologist. He was educated at Cambridge, Oxford and Gottingen Universities. He taught at University of London, Oxford University, Harvard University and Duke University. William McDougall is considered by some to have been the foremost psychologist of all English-speaking countries. He has contributed significantly to more branches and departments of psychology than anyone else writing in English. His publications include: Introduction to Social Psychology, Body and Mind, Outline of Psychology and Outline of Abnormal Psychology.

• McDougall said that several of his patients afflicted with spermatorrhea \[involuntary emission of semen without orgasm\] and generative debility discovered that tea and coffee always proved harmful to them by provoking such discharges.

Author’s Comment: Caffeine increases the heartbeat, raises the blood pressure and exerts an aphrodisiacal effect, while its uric acid irritates and causes inflammation of the genital mucous membrane, which is the seat of sexual sensibility. Lallemand mentions the case of a man who suffered from frequent nocturnal emissions as a result of excessive coffee drinking, but when the coffee was discontinued, the emissions stopped, and the patient was restored to health. Cocoa, which contains the alkaloid theobromine, has long been regarded as an aphrodisiac. Coffee, tea, cocoa, as well as chocolate contain uric acid and caffeine, and consequently are sexually stimulating.

## Gandhi on diet

Control of the palate is the first essential in the observance of the vow (of Brahmacharya) \[celibacy\]. I found that complete control of the palate made the observance very easy, and so I now pursued my dietetic experiments not merely from the vegetarian’s but also from the Brahmachari’s point of view. Control of the palate is very closely connected with the observance of Brahmacharya. I have found from experience that the observance of celibacy becomes comparatively easy, if one acquires mastery over the palate. Food has to taken as we take medicine, that is, without thinking whether it is palatable or otherwise.

A Sanskrit text says that a man becomes what he eats. A glutton who exercises no restraint in eating, is a slave to his animal passions. One who has not been able to control his palate, will never be able to control the other senses.

One should take just enough food for the requirements of the body and no more. The diet should be healthy and well-balanced. The body was never meant to be treated as a refuse-bin, holding the foods that the palate demands. Food is meant to sustain the body. His body has been given to man as a means of selfrealization. Self-realization means realization of God. A person who has made this realization the object of his or her life, will never become a slave to the animal passion.

The following are to be strictly avoided by those whose desire it is to lead a pure, chaste, and continent life: Tobacco in all its forms. All manner of alcoholic liquors, tea, coffee and chocolate.

—My Experiments with Truth: Part III, Chap. VIII

Author’s Comment: According to Prof. Howe, tobacco has the tendency to increase involuntary seminal emissions by its irritating action on the genital mucous membranes. This is even more marked in women than in men, since tobacco creates havoc in the female genital tract, producing sterility and cancer of these organs.

## 8. Religious Purity

## Lord Krishna

Lord Krsna or Krishna (living at the end of the Dvapara age) the original, two-armed form of the Supreme Lord, who is the origin of all universes, incarnations and living entities. Although He eternally resides in His own planet Goloka, He descended as an avatara to this earth, appearing at Mathura in Northern India. His most famous treatise is Bhagavad-gita or Song of God spoken to His disciple and friend Arjuna on the battlefield of Kuruksetra in India. His transcendental pastimes are the subject of Srimad-Bhagavatam and other Vedic literatures.

• Lord Krishna: “I am sex life which is not contrary to religious principles.”

Author’s Comment: One lifelong celibate and saintly person declared that if he could produce God-conscious children through sex, he would be prepared to do so 100 times.This is an encouragement for family householders to produce good progeny as a pious service.

• Lord Krishna: “Persons who are learned in the Vedas, who utter omkara and who are great sages in the renounced order enter into Brahman. Desiring such perfection, one practices celibacy.”

Author’s Comment: Brahman means the spiritual realm. Omkara is the sound om which is the sound representation of God. It is comprised of three syllables a, u, m.

• Lord Krishna: “As far as the life of brahmacarya is concerned, if one can continue the life of a brahmacari under the direction of a spiritual master, that is extremely good; but if in brahmacari life one feels sex impulses, he should take leave of his spiritual master, satisfying him according to the guru’s desire. According to the Vedic system, a gift is offered to the spiritual master, which is called guru-daksina. Then the disciple should take to householder life and accept a wife according to religious rites.”

Author’s Comment: Everyone is advised to practice complete celibacy throughout their entire lives, like Jesus and other saintly persons, devoting their full energy to serving God and attaining self realization. Most persons, however, feel the need for association with the opposite sex, so Lord Krsna describes here the accepted method of doing this. The training of the boy is brahmacarya, and the training of the girl is chastity. A faithful wife and spiritually trained brahmacari are a good combination for advancement of the human mission.

• Lord Krishna: “Those who are not married—sannyasis, vanaprasthas and brahmacaris—should never associate with women by glancing, touching, conversing, joking or sporting. Neither should they ever associate with any living entity engaged in sexual activities.”

Author’s Comment: Brahmacaris are the students studying from the spiritual masters. A brahmacari may remain unmarried or he may marry as he chooses. Vanaprasthas are the retired family persons who concentrate on performing austerities. Sannyasis are those who have taken vows of complete sexual abstinence and travel for enlightening others.

• Lord Krishna: “Real happiness is to transcend material happiness and unhappiness, and real misery is to be implicated in searching for sex pleasure (kama-sukha). A wretched person is one who cannot control his senses, whereas one who is not attached to sense gratification is a real controller. One who attaches himself to sense gratification is the opposite, a slave.”

Author’s Comment: The search for mundane sex pleasure, kama-sukha, is not appreciated by Lord Krishna. The art of lust is portrayed in the book Kama Sutra, and of course, it is not meant for persons wishing to make spiritual advancement.

• Lord Krishna: “A householder may approach his wife for sex only at the time prescribed for begetting children. Otherwise, the householder should practice celibacy, austerity, cleanliness of mind and body, satisfaction in his natural position, and friendship toward all living entities.”

Author’s Comment: Lord Krishna has many names, one of which indicates that He captivates Cupid. It is well known that Cupid (Madana) has a strong influence upon the conditioned souls of the material world by piercing them with his five arrows of lust. Yet Madana is conquered by Krishna, and thus He is called Madana-mohana. The devotees call upon Madana-mohana to kindly rescue them from the attacks of Cupid or mundane lust, and He responds.

## Brahma

Brahma (born at the beginning of the uni-verse) the first living entity in the universe, born directly from God (Visnu). Brahma is the first disciple of God, the architect of the universe, the primary progenitor of living entities and the spiritual master of residents of this universe. His treatise Brahma-samhita expounds the science of self-realization.

• Brahma: “The spiritual world, which consists of three fourths of the Lord’s energy, is situated beyond this material world, and it is especially meant for those who will never be reborn. Others, who are attached to family life and who do not strictly follow celibacy vows, must live within the three material worlds.”

Author’s Comment: The material realm is understood to comprise only one fourth of the total manifestation, whereas the spiritual world is three times larger. Those who practice strict celibacy become eligible to enter into the spiritual world and are not subjected to rebirth or reincarnation in the mundane sphere.

• Brahma: “They \[devotees of the Lord\] should completely follow the principles of devotional service and remain free from illicit sex, gambling, intoxication and animal slaughter. Surrendering themselves fully with body, words and mind, they can live in any asrama or social status.”

Author’s Comment: There are four asramas or social statuses within an advanced civilization—students, householders, retired and renounced. One who follows spiritual principles avoiding the four pillars of sinful life mentioned above can live in any of the four social statuses and make spiritual progress.

## Yajnavalkya

Sage Yajnavalkya, (he lived as early as the Treta age) the son of Devarata. He is one of the principle philosophers, saints and authorities of ancient India. He learned the Yajur Vedic mantras from Vaisampayana and compiled the treatise known as Vajasaneyi-samhita.

• Sage Yajnavalkya: “The vow of brahmacarya (celibacy) is meant to help one completely abstain from sex indulgence in work, words and mind—at all times, under all circumstances, and in all places.”

Author’s Comment: Boys should be trained in brahmacarya today. This systematic approach is most helpful to attaining purity. In this system, the three higher levels of social divisions promise to practice celibacy until they become married.

## Prahlada Maharaja

Prahlada Maharaja (lived during the era of Vaivasvata Manu within the Daitya dynasty) famous devotee who was personally rescued by a unique form of the Lord. Prahlada was the youngest son of the wicked king Hiranyakasipu and his wife, Kayadhu. Prahlada Maharaja was instructed in the science of self-realization by the great sage Narada Muni while he was within his mother’s womb. His atheist father tried to kill him in many ways but the child was always protected by God and could not be harmed. Finally, the Lord incarnated as a half-man, half-lion (Nrsimhadeva—nara-man; simha-lion) to kill the father. Prahlada’s son was known as Virocana, and his grandson was Bali Maharaja.

• Prahlada Maharaja: “Sex life is compared to the rubbing of two hands to relieve an itch. Grhamedhis, so-called grhasthas who have no spiritual knowledge, think that this itching is the greatest platform of happiness, although actually it is a source of distress. The krpanas, the fools who are just the opposite of brahmanas, are not satisfied by repeated sensuous enjoyment. Those who are dhira, however, who are sober and who tolerate this itching, are not subjected to the sufferings of fools and rascals.”

Author’s Comment: The term grhamedhis mentioned here refers to householders who simply are interested in sense gratification and have no spiritual vision. When one has an infection, the doctor advises not to scratch it because the infection will spread all over. If one does scratch it to experience some momentary pleasure, then he risks serious danger of compounding the infection. Sometimes that scratching brings the infection to the point where amputation is necessary. Similarly, one should tolerate the itch to have sex.

• Prahlada Maharaja: “Those bereft of knowledge of the Lord are bound by the material laws. They are factually addicted to sense gratification, and their target is woman (kama-drsam). Indeed, they are actually playthings in the hands of attractive women.”

Author’s Comment: Everyone, from Brahma, to presidents of nations to ordinary folks, is affected by kama or lust and no one should think himself or herself immune. Rather with great humility one should recognize his own vulnerability when associating with the opposite sex.

## Vaivasvata Manu

Vaivasvata Manu is the seventh and present Manu (ruler of mankind). His father was the sun-god Vivasvan, to whom Lord Krsna instructed the teachings of Bhagavad-gita prior to His teaching them to Arjuna. Manu’s reign will continue for another 184,900,000 years. The eldest among his ten sons is King Iksvaku.

• Manu: “Women should not be independent.” (Manu-samhita)

Author’s Comment: Manu is the father of mankind and thus the term “man” comes from him. His treatise Manu-samhita is considered the “Lawbook of Mankind.” There it is explained that women should be carefully protected by their fathers when children, by their husbands when they are young, and by their grown sons when old. In this way women will not be subjected to irreligious activities and exploitation.

Customarily the groom should be older than the bride; 25 years for the grooms and 16 years for the brides are the recommended ages for entering marriage. Marriage is a sacred institution that is vital for perfecting life and society. By minimizing the importance of marriage, our society degrades to the level of animals. Also, within marriage sex is not to be used without the purpose of procreation.

• Manu: “Lust cannot be satisfied by any amount of sense enjoyment, just as fire is never extinguished by a constant supply of fuel.”

Author’s Comment: This analogy is apt. We think erroneously that by engaging in lusty affairs our desire for them will abate; however, it does not happen. Rather as fuel never extinguishes a fire but instead makes it blaze more, so indulgence causes more insatiable desires. The solution is to reduce the fuel until the fire stops burning.

• Manu: “Unless one gives up sinful activities \[meat-eating, intoxication and illicit sex indulgence\], there is no possibility of achieving the actual perfection of life.”

Author’s Comment: According to Manu, our present civilization is based on imperfect principles. Meat-eating destroys mercy, intoxication destroys austerity and illicit sex destroys cleanliness. It is seen in nightclubs that meat, liquor and sex go side by side.

• Manu: “Men and women should be married properly and should unite only for producing nicechildren.”

Author’s Comment: Sometimes scholars like to quote Manu as recommending more sex within marriage for less spiritually evolved persons, yet here we see that Manu limits sex to procreative purposes. Consequently, teaching from scriptures is supposed to be limited to those whose mind and senses are under control, otherwise one is prone to concoct an interpretation which suits one’s sense desires.

## Rsabhadeva Maharaja

Rsabhadeva Maharaja (lived during the Svayambhuva age) an incarnation of God who appeared as the son of King Nabhi and his wife Merudevi in Jam-budvipa. He ruled the earth planet and fathered 100 sons. His eldest and most well-known son was King Bharata after whom this planet was named Bharata-varsa. Rsabhadeva Maharaja instructed his sons on the science of self-realization, and afterwards he retired to the forest for practicing austerities and penances.

• Rsabhadeva Maharaja told His sons: “My dear boys, of all the living entities who have accepted material bodies in this world, one who has been awarded this human form should not work hard day and night simply for sense gratification, which is available even for dogs and hogs that eat stool. One should engage in penance and austerity to attain the divine position of devotional service. By such activity, one’s heart is purified, and when one attains this position, he attains eternal, blissful life, which is transcendental to material happiness and which continues forever. One can attain the path of liberation from material bondage only by rendering service to highly advanced spiritual personalities. For those who are not interested in such activities, who associate with people fond of women and sex (yositam sangi-sangam), the path to hell is wide open.”

• Rsabhadeva Maharaja: “Even though one may be very learned and wise, he is mad if he does not understand that the endeavor for sense gratification is a useless waste of time. Being forgetful of his own interest, he tries to be happy in the material world, centering his interests around his home, which is based on sexual intercourse (maithunyam agaram) and which brings him all kinds of material miseries. In this way one is no better than a foolish animal.”

• Rsabhadeva Maharaja: “The attraction between male and female is the basic principle of material existence (pumsah striya mithuni-bhavam etam). On the basis of this misconception, which ties together the hearts of the male and female, one becomes attracted to his body, home, property, children, relatives and wealth. In this way one increases life’s illusions and thinks in terms of ‘I and mine.’”

## Jada Bharata

Jada Bharata (lived during the Svayambhuva age) born in India to a very pure brahmana (priest) father in the dynasty of Angira. He achieved spiritual perfection and instructed King Rahugana, ruler of the states known as Sindhu and Sauvira, on the science of self-realization.

• Jada Bharata: “My dear King, the merchant on the forest path of the material world, his intelligence victimized by home, wealth, relatives and so forth, runs from one place to another in search of success. Sometimes his eyes are covered by the dust of a whirlwind—that is to say, in his lust he is captivated by the beauty of his wife, especially during her menstrual period. Thus his eyes are blinded, and he cannot see where to go or what he is doing.”

Author’s Comment: People perhaps are not willing to accept the fact that continence is dependent upon regulation of passion. Humans seem to be dominated by their own desires and pleasure-seeking natures.

• Jada Bharata: “The living entity in the forest of the material world tries to give up the association of these so-called yogis, svamis and incarnations and come to the association of real devotees, but due to misfortune he cannot follow the instructions of the spiritual master or advanced devotees; therefore he gives up their company and again returns to the association of monkeys who are simply interested in sense gratification and women. He derives satisfaction by associating with sense gratifiers and enjoying sex and intoxication. In this way he spoils his life simply by indulging in sex and intoxication. Looking into the faces of other sense gratifiers, he becomes forgetful and thus approaches death. When the living entity becomes exactly like a monkey jumping from one branch to another, he remains in the tree of household life without any profit but sex.”

## Maharaja Yayati

Maharaja Yayati: (lived during the era of Vaivasvata Manu) was the son of Maharaja Nahusa. He was crowned emperor of the world because his elder brother preferred to lead the life of a liberated saintly mystic. King Yayati ruled for several thousands of years and performed many sacrifices and pious activities recorded in history. His early youth was very lustful and full of romantic stories. Consequently, his fatherin-law SukrAcaryacursed him to become impotent, but his youngest son accepted the curse on his behalf. His five sons founded all great nations of the world who belong to the Aryan and Indo-European stock.

• Maharaja Yayati: “As supplying butter to a fire does not diminish the fire but instead increases it more and more, the endeavor to stop lusty desires by continual enjoyment can never be successful. \[In fact, one must voluntarily cease from material desires.\]”

Author’s Comment: In the illustration above, Maharaja Yayati is pictured rescuing Devayani, his future wife from an abandoned well.

• Maharaja Yayati: “One should not allow oneself to sit on the same seat even with one’s own mother, sister or daughter, for the senses are so strong that even though one is very advanced in knowledge, he may be attracted by sex.”

Author’s Comment: This verse does not intend to insinuate that all fathers, brothers or sons maintain impure intentions towards their daughters, sisters or mothers. Rather, it is a strong warning as to the danger of lust. Lust can carry away even the mind of a learned, cultured or spiritually progressive person as a strong wind can destroy a strong ship.

## Saubhari Muni

Saubhari Muni (lived at the time of the reign of King Mandhata) was a mystic yogi who meditated submerged within the water at the bottom of the Yamuna River. He later married the daughters of King Mandhata and overindulged his senses. Later, however, he regretted his fall down and spoke the following verse.

• Saubhari Muni: “A person desiring liberation from material bondage must give up the association of persons interested in sex life and should not employ his senses externally \[in seeing, hearing, talking, walking and so on\]. One should always stay in a secluded place, completely fixing his mind at the lotus feet of the unlimited Personality of Godhead, and if one wants any association at all, he should associate with persons similarly engaged.”

Author’s Comment: Bad association is so undesirable that one should prefer to live alone to avoid it. On the other hand, good association with saintly persons is so desirable that one should seek it at all costs—it is better than living alone in a peaceful situation.

## Avadhuta Brahmana

Avadhuta Brahmana (lived during the reign of King Yadu) was capable, learned, expert, handsome and most eloquent. Nevertheless, he was living a very saintly life beyond regulative principles simply wandering fearlessly. He instructed King Yadu on transcendental knowledge explaining how he had 24 gurus including the earth, air, sky, water, fire, moon, sun, pigeon, python, etc.

• Avadhuta Brahmana: “A foolish person with no intelligent discrimination is immediately aroused at the sight of a lusty woman beautifully decorated with golden ornaments, fine clothing and other cosmetic features. Being eager for sense gratification, such a fool loses all intelligence and is destroyed just like the moth who rushes into the blazing fire.”

Author’s Comment: The male perspective is given here; however, the same applies for the female. She is equally attracted by seeing the form of a man, which also is created by the illusory energy of the Supreme Lord. Indeed, when the man speaks enticing words, smiles flirtatiously and exhibits his strength and agility, her mind is immediately captured, and thus she falls blindly into the darkness of material existence, just as the moth maddened by the fire rushes blindly into its flames. Sensuous men captivate women with their worldly singing, dancing and musical entertainment. We recall seeing the musical group Beatles perform before thousands of maddened young women.

• Avadhuta Brahmana: “A saintly person (bhiksuh) should never touch a young girl. In fact, he should not even let his foot touch a wooden doll in the shape of a woman. By bodily contact with a woman he will surely be captured by illusion, just as the elephant is captured by the she-elephant due to his desire to touch her body.”

Author’s Comment: Here the word “saintly person” or bhiksuh is significant. The standard for those in the renounced order or those who have taken vows of perpetual celibacy is more stringent than those for others. Naturally one who has committed himself to a life of sexual abstinence must be more careful than one who is married or than one who intends to become married at some later date—thus the extreme prohibitions for priests and monks.

Be that as it may, it also should be explained that in Vedic culture, a virgin girl was not meant to mix with boys or men because of the same principle. Once she comes in bodily contact with the opposite sex, she becomes bewildered and is prone to be exploited by men. This principle is prominent in today’s society. Consequently, this verse is applicable to both renunciate men and to women wishing to remain chaste.

• Avadhuta Brahmana: “Becoming attracted to the worldly singing, dancing and musical entertainment of beautiful women, even the great sage Rsyasrnga, the son of Mrgi, fell totally under their control, just like a pet animal.”

Author’s Comment: Rsyasrnga, the young son of the sage Mrgi, was intentionally brought up by his father in an atmosphere of complete innocence. Mrgi thought that if his son were never exposed to the sight of women he would always remain a perfect brahmacari (celibate). But by chance the inhabitants of the neighboring kingdom, who were suffering from a long-term drought, received divine advice that rain would return to their kingdom only after the brahmana named Rsyasrnga stepped foot in it. Therefore they sent beautiful women to the hermitage of Mrgi, while he was away, just to entice Rsyasrnga and bring him back with them. Since Rsyasrnga had never even heard about women, he easily fell for their trap.

## Uddhava

Uddhava (lived in the Dvapara age) one of Lord Krishna’s most dear childhood friends and brilliant student of sage Brhaspati. Uddhava was highly respected for his political advice and is considered the greatest devotee of Lord Krishna among the Yadu dynasty.

• Uddhava: “Sages who seriously endeavor in spiritual practice, who have raised their semen upward, who are peaceful and sinless members of the renounced order, attain the spiritual abode called Brahman \[liberation\].”

Author’s Comment: “Raising their semen upward” or urdhva-retah means not expelling semen in sex of any kind and instead drawing the vital force up to the brain by spiritual pursuits. In earlier chapters we heard scientists describe this action as “reabsorption of semen.” One who can completely control sex life is able to work wonderfully with his brain, especially in remembering.

## Suta Gosvami

Suta Gosvami (circa 3000 BC) a great sage who recounted the Srimad-Bhagavatam to the sages at Naimisaranya. He was the son of Romaharasana Suta and he was present when Sukadeva Gosvami explained the science of Godhead to Maharaja Pariksit.

• Suta Gosvami: “Maharaja Pariksit, thus being petitioned by the personality of Kali, gave him permission to reside in places where gambling, drinking, prostitution and animal slaughter \[i.e. where four types of sinful activities (adharmas catur-vidhah)\] wereperformed.

Author’s Comment: The personality of Kali is the personification of the age in which we live. The age began over 5100 years ago upon the moment Lord Krishna departed from earth. “Kali” means literally “quarrel,” and this age is beset with small and big quarrels due to the sinful behavior of the humans. The age is destined to continue for another 427,000 years at which time the next age, Satya begins again.

## Buddha

Lord Buddha (560 BC) a powerful incarnation of the Personality of Godhead and founder of the Buddhist religion. He appeared in the province of Gaya (Bihar) as the son of Anjana (sometimes called Siddhartha Gautama). He preached his own conception of nonviolence and deprecated even the animal sacrifices sanctioned in the Vedas. He grew up in a royal family, so he was sometimes called Prince Siddhartha, and at the age of 29, he left the kingdom, his wife and newborn son, to lead an ascetic life. His appearance was predicted in the Vedic scriptures written in 3000 BC.

• Buddha taught five precepts (known as Right Action). Right Action follows after Right Speech. Right Action entails refraining from killing, stealing, and sexual misconduct. These three evil deeds are caused by craving and anger, associated with ignorance. By the gradual eliminating of these causes from the mind, blameworthy actions will find no expression. Being pure in mind, a person will lead a pure life.

Author’s Comment: Refraining from sexual misconduct is an injunction in the sastras (scriptures). The Bhagavad-gita says, dhar-maviruddho’ bhutesu kamo ’smi bharatarsabha, sex indulgence which is not against religious principles is allowed by marriage. In Western countries, they are having sex without any restrictions. But according to the Vedic system, there are restrictions.

• Buddha: “A mendicant (Bhikkhu) leads a life of voluntary poverty and celibacy.”

Author’s Comment: “Bhikkhu,” and its form in Sanskrit, bhiksu, means literally means one who begs. This term is given to those who have taken the fourth order of sannyasa. They voluntarily vow not to have any relationships with women from the time of taking the vow.

## Jewish tradition

• Jewish tradition also mentions the celibate Zenu’im (lit. “chaste ones”) to whom the secret of the Name of God was entrusted, for they were able to preserve the Holy Name in “perfect purity” (Kiddushin 71a; Midrash Ecclesiastes Rabbah 3:11; Yer. yoma 39a, 40a).

Author’s Comment: The same maxim is present in the Vedic system—that a person who is so dedicated to scripture and devotional service to God benefits the world more by renouncing family ties. Jesus the Jew did this and Lord Caitanya Mahaprab-hu did it. Sri Caitanya Mahaprabhu was only 24 years old when he renounced family life in favor of accepting the world as his family. The Torah and learned Rabbis understand this higher principle.

When for the sake of the Torah (i.e., intense study in it), a rabbi would abstain from relations with his wife, it was deemed permissible, for he then was cohabiting with the Shekinah (the “Divine Presence”) in the Torah (Zohar re Gn 1:27; 13:3 and Psalm 85:14 in the Discourse of Rabbi Phineas to Rabbis Jose, Judah, and Hiya).

• Those in hope of a divine revelation consequently refrained from sexual intercourse and were strict in matters of purity (Enoch 83:2; Revelation 14:2-5).

Author’s Comment: In the Skulkhan Arukh (EH 1:4) it is written: “If a person cleaves to the study of the Torah (i.e., dedicates all his time to it) like Shimon ben Azzai, his refusal to marry can be condoned.” Rabbinic scholar Shimon ben Azzai was extraordinary in his learning and also renowned for his saintliness (Berakoth 57b, B.T.). For example, when Eleazar b. Azariah reproved him for not procreating, Shimon ben Azzai (90-125 CE) replied:

“What shall I do? My soul clings lovingly to the Torah; let others contribute to the preservation of the race.”

• For “the chaste are rewarded by receiving illumination from the concealed heavenly light” (Zohar 11. 229b-230a). Because “if the understanding is safe and unimpaired, free from the oppression of the iniquities or passions... it will gaze clearly on all that is worthy of contemplation” (Philo, Sob. 1.5). Conversely, “the understanding of the pleasureloving man is blind and unable to see those things that are worth seeing... the sight of which is wonderful to behold and desirable” (Philo, Q. Gen.IV.245).

Author’s Comment: From our experience in Israel it appears that many Jews believe that celibacy is not part of their culture. In fact, they feel it is irreligious to practice it, or putting it another way, they feel it is irreligious not to procreate. However, as is often the case, this constricted vision misses the superior axiom as Ben Azzai demonstrates. Jews too may also rejoice in making their lives a pure and celibate offering to Yahweh (God) without contradicting their heritage.

About Ben Azzai it is said:

“With the passing of Ben Azzai diligent scholars passed from the earth.” (Sotah 9:15).

“Ben Azzai never married and was celibate all his life so as not to be distracted from his studies, and because he considered the Torah his wife, for whom he always yearned with all his soul.” (Yebamoth 63b).

## Moses

Artist: Gustave Dori; University of Minnesota Duluth

Moses (1570-1450 BC) great Hebrew prophet and legislator. He was the son of Amram of the Levitical tribe, born in Egypt. In pursuance of a royal command that all male infants of Hebrew birth should be destroyed, Moses, to escape this fate, was laid in a basket among a clump of bulrushes on the banks of the Nile, and there discovered by the daughter of the Pharaoh, who adopted him as her son. He served as a shepherd in Midian until his 80th year, when the Lord commanded him to guide the children of Israel out of captivity into the Land of Canaan. He accordingly conducted them through the Red Sea into the wilderness, and became their apostolic chief and lawgiver. After appointing Joshua as his successor, Moses died on Mount Pisgah.

• Moses kept himself “apart from woman,” remaining in the sanctity of separation to be at the beck and call of God at all times.

Author’s Comment: The rabbis who authored Midrash Exodus Rabbah 19:3 and 46.3 cited God’s command to Moses in Deuteronomy 5:28 in the statement above. Jewish tradition mentions that, although the people had to abstain from sexual relations with their wives for only three days prior to the revelation at Mount Sinai (Ex 19:15), Moses chose to remain continent the rest of his life with the full approval of God. The rabbis explained that this was so because Moses knew that he was appointed to personally commune with God, not only at Mount Sinai, but in general throughout the 40 years of sojourning in the wilderness.

• Moses: “Thou shalt not lie with mankind, as with womankind: it is abomination.” (The Book of Leviticus: Chapter 18; Verse 22)

Author’s Comment: This appears to be a clear forbiddance of homosexual behavior.

• Moses: “And if a man shall lie with a woman having her sickness, and shall uncover her nakedness; he hath discovered her fountain, and she hath uncovered the fountain of her blood: and both of them shall be cut off from among their people. And thou shalt not uncover the nakedness of thy mother’s sister, nor of thy father’s sister: for he un-covereth his near kin: they shall bear their iniquity. And if a man shall lie with his uncle’s wife, he hath uncovered his uncle’s nakedness: they shall bear their sin; they shall die childless. And if a man shall take his brother’s wife, it is an unclean thing: he hath uncovered his brother’s nakedness; they shall be childless.” (The Book of Leviticus: Chapter 20; Verses 18-21)

Author’s Comment: The first item mentioned here is not to have sex during the time of menstruation. Srila Bhaktivinoda Öhakura has given a definition for celibacy as follows:

“Brahmacarya—continence or abstaining from misuse of one’s semen (not indulging in sex with women other than one’s own wife and not having sex with one’s own wife when sex is forbidden, like during the period of menstruation.)”

Another restriction mentioned here is also included in the Vedic teachings wherein it is written: devarena sutotpattim kalau panca vivarjayet. This means that in this age of Kali, five acts are forbidden, one of which is a man’s begetting children in his brother’s wife. Formerly if a husband was unable to beget a child in the womb of his wife, he could request his brother to do so on his behalf and the child would belong to the husband, not to his brother. However, in this age such action is forbidden.

• Moses: “There shall be no whore of the daughters of Israel, nor a sodomite of the sons of Israel.” (The Book of Deuteronomy: Chapter 23; Verse 17)

Author’s Comment: This is a strong commandment prohibiting illicit sex among those governed by the Old Testament—if not mistaken, this would include the Jews, the Christians and the Muslims.

• Moses: “Cursed be he that lieth with his father’s wife; because he uncovereth his father’s skirt. And all the people shall say, Amen. Cursed be he that lieth with any manner of beast. And all the people shall say, Amen. Cursed be he that lieth with his sister, the daughter of his father, or the daughter of his mother. And all the people shall say, Amen. Cursed be he that lieth with his mother in law. And all the people shall say, Amen.” (The Book of Deuteronomy: Chapter 27; Verses 20-23)

Author’s Comment: If humanity fails to abide by these instructions, it devolves to the platform of hogs, which do not discriminate in sex relations.

## King Solomon

Artist: Gustave Dori; University of Minnesota Duluth

Solomon (King of Israel from 1015 to 975 BC) second son of David and Bathsheba, and David’s successor. Solomon was held in high repute for his prolific knowledge of art and science and for the glory of his reign. He had a passion for magnificence, and he erected the Temple in Jerusalem and a palace on Mount Zion. He wrote 3,000 proverbs and 1,005 songs among which are the Song of Solomon, Ecclesiastes, and most of the Book of Proverbs.

• Solomon: “Lust not after her beauty in thine heart; neither let her take thee with her eyelids. So he that goeth in to his neighbour’s wife; whosoever toucheth her shall not be innocent. But whoso committeth adultery with a woman lacketh understanding: he that doeth it destroyeth his own soul.” (The Book of Proverbs: Chapter 6; Verse 25, 29 and 32)

Author’s Comment: A Godbrother of mine expresses a nice sentiment in this regard: “Our sexual desire is simply a perverted reflection of our heart’s deepest longings to love Krishna \[God\]. If we just knew this, we could all be happy. But in the darkness of the modern age it is very difficult to convince anyone that there is anything wrong with sex at all.”

• Solomon: “Whoso loveth wisdom rejoiceth his father: but he that keepeth company with harlots spendeth his substance.” (The Book of Proverbs: Chapter 29; Verse 3)

Author’s Comment: It seems that the word “substance” refers here to vital force or vital fluids. In this case, it clearly forbids any action which will waste semen by male or female through the association of lewd persons. During youth it appears that one has unlimited sexual power and can enjoy more and more. Lamentation however arises when one realizes that he has been cheated by the bad masters of lust and greed. Srila Rupa Gosvami writes:

“O my Lord, there is no limit to the unwanted orders of lusty desires. Although I have rendered these desires so much service, they have not shown any mercy to me. I have not been ashamed to serve them, nor have I even desired to give them up. O my Lord, O head of the Yadu dynasty, recently, however, my intelligence has been awakened, and now I am giving them up. Due to transcendental intelligence, I now refuse to obey the unwanted orders of these desires, and I now come to You to surrender myself at Your fearless lotus feet. Kindly engage me in Your personal service and save me.”

Such pleasures are surely short lived—instead one’s spirit, intellect, mind, brain and nervous system become increasingly dulled or drained of vitality by the flickering happiness of sex.

## Plato

Plato (427-347 BC) the best known of all Greek philosophers. He was born in Athens, with the original name Aristocles, but in his school days he received the nickname Platon (meaning “broad”) because of his broad shoulders.

In early life Plato saw war service and had political ambitions. He was a devoted follower of Socrates, whose disciple he became in 409 BC. When Socrates was executed in 399 BC, it was a crushing blow to Plato. Nevertheless, afterward, Plato carried on much of the work of his teacher and eventually founded his own school, perhaps the first Western university, known as the Academy, in 385. The Academy would become in its time the most famous school in the classical world, and its most famous pupil was Aristotle. Plato’s works consist of a series of dialogues in which the discussions between Socrates and others are presented with infinite charm. Plato’s philosophy maintained a strong influence on the thinking of the Christian Church throughout the early Middle Ages.

• Plato: “The only justification for sexuality to exist at all was for procreation. Therefore, all sexuality outside of marriage should be forbidden by law.” (Laws 838-839)

Author’s Comment: One who practices pure celibacy throughout his life is eligible to go, after death, to the heavenly planet Satyaloka.

• Plato: “We must not forget that this pleasure is held to have been granted by nature to male and female when conjoined for the work of procreation; the crime of male with male, or female with female, is an outrage on nature and a capital surrender to lust of pleasure.” (Laws I 636a-d)

Author’s Comment: Also in Buddha’s teachings sexual misconduct refers to all sexual relationships outside of marriage. The Dalai Lama said the same Buddhist scripture that advises against gay and lesbian sex also urges heterosexuals to refrain from oral sex, anal sex and masturbation.

• Plato: “No one should venture to touch any of the noble and freeborn save his own wedded wife, nor sow any unholy and bastard seed in fornication, nor any unnatural and barren seed in sodomy,—or else we should entirely abolish love for males, and in regard to that for women, if we enact a law that any man who has intercourse with any women save those who have been brought to his house under the sanction of Heaven and holy marriage, whether purchased or otherwise acquired, if detected in such intercourse by any man or woman, shall be disqualified from any civic commendation.” (Laws 841)

Author’s Comment: There are so many tribulations associated with sex: labor pains, maintaining offspring, giving them education, etc. The pleasure of sexual intercourse has the purpose of offsetting the pains of begetting. However if one tries to take the pleasure without the responsibility of begetting, he is a thief.

## Saint Peter the apostle

Saint Peter the Apostle (?-64) also known as Simon, Cephas, Brother of Saint Andrew the Apostle who led him to Christ. He was formerly a fisherman and was renamed “Peter” (rock) by Jesus to indicate that Peter would be the rock-like foundation on which the Church would be built. He was the first Pope.

• Saint Peter: “That he no longer should live the rest of his time in the flesh to the lusts of men, but to the will of God.”

• Saint Peter: “For the time past of our life may suffice us to have wrought the will of the Gentiles, when we walked in lasciviousness, lusts, excess of wine, revellings, banqueting.”

Author’s Comment: It is a great mistake to think that life without illicit sex is detestable. From experience, we see those who renounce it have not experienced any dreadful black holes in their lives. They are still the same type of person they were previously only now with the added ability to focus on more important things. Peter himself (Matthew 4:19) was transformed by Jesus from being a fisherman to a “fisher of men.”

## Saint Luke the Apostle

Saint Luke the Apostle—possibly born a slave in Antioch, Syria, of Pagan parents and one of the earliest converts. Saint Luke was a physician, and legend has that he was also a painter who may have done portraits of Jesus and Mary. He travelled with Saint Paul and evangelized Greece and Rome. Also he wrote a history of the early Church in the Acts of the Apostles.

• Saint Luke: \[Quoting Jesus\] “Likewise also as it was in the days of Lot; they did eat, they drank, they bought, they sold, they planted, they builded; But the same day that Lot went out of Sodom it rained fire and brimstone from heaven, and destroyed them all.” (Bible: Book of Luke 17.28-29)

Author’s Comment: Sodomy is not allowed in any religious tradition that we are aware of. It is considered against nature and harmful for oneself and others. A homosexually inclined person wishing to advance in spiritual life should engage in favorable activities according to the scriptures and according to his/her level of understanding. Assuming such a person is still attached to engaging in illicit sex, then he should have hope that by chanting, hearing, serving, praying, worshiping and remembering in relationship with God, eventually he/she will become freed from that habit.

By the grace of God and His representatives, the candidate can advance on the path of devotion, realize the futility of homosexuality and give it up entirely. If one chooses to give up homosexuality but not sexuality altogether, he may utilize the marriage opportunity as it is meant to be used—by taking a wife, becoming a responsible householder and raising children. There are sufficient successful examples of this today.

• Saint Luke: \[Quoting Jesus\] “You know the commandments: ‘Do not kill, Do not commit adultery, Do not steal, Do not bear false witness, Do not defraud, Honor your father and mother.’” (Bible: Book of Luke 18.20)

## Saint Matthew the Apostle

Saint Matthew the Apostle—(Also known as Levi; Apostle of Ethiopia) was the son of Alphaeus. He lived at Capenaum on Lake Genesareth. He was a Roman tax collector, a position equated with collaboration with the enemy by those from whom he collected taxes. He preached among the Jews for fifteen years where his audiences may have included the Jewish enclave in Ethiopia and places in the East.

• Saint Matthew: \[Quoting Jesus\] “For out of the heart proceed evil thoughts, murders, adulteries, fornications, thefts, false witness, blasphemies.” (Bible: Book of Matthew 15.19)

• Saint Matthew: \[Quoting Jesus\] “And I say unto you, whosoever shall put away his wife, except it be for fornication, and shall marry another, committeth adultery: and whoso marrieth her which is put away doth commit adultery.” (Bible: Book of Matthew 19.9)

Author’s Comment: Divorce encourages illicit sex as described here by Saint Matthew. The Vedic system and formerly the Biblical system allowed for a man to accept more than one wife but divorce was not permissible. The man must agree to maintain his wife or wives and the children throughout life.

## Saint Paul the Apostle

Saint Paul the Apostle (c.3-65) born as Saul at Tarsus, Cilicia (modern Turkey). He was a Talmudic student, a Pharisee and a tent-maker by trade. Saul the Jew hated and persecuted Christians as heretical, even assisting at the stoning of Saint Stephen the Martyr. On his way to Damascus to arrest another group of Christians, he had a profound spiritual experience which caused his conversion to Christianity. He was baptized, changed his name to Paul to reflect his new persona, and began traveling and preaching.
